Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Authorities Archive
Compiled by Claude, September 2026. Source material: Archive Reconstruction Plan pt1–pt3 (ChatGPT transcript), JavaScript example doc, and ChatGPT project-memory export.
Purpose of this Archive
This archive covers how Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance (C.E.R.) is governed and developed — the rules, permissions, delegated authorities, and project-level principles that apply to anyone (or any AI) working on the project. It does not cover game content itself; that lives in the Master Archive.
Master Index — Every Permission & Rule, One List
A flat, consolidated index of everything granted or established across this whole document, organized by topic rather than buried in one giant undifferentiated list. Each entry links to its full detail below — this section exists purely so nothing requires hunting through the document to confirm it exists. Built per Wesley's request, Sept 2026, after auditing the document confirmed everything below was genuinely already locked somewhere, just not consolidated in one place.
F.R.M. core framework
Equal decision-making authority — see “Free Reign Mode (F.R.M.)” below.
The actual standard: “Does this make E.R. better?”, not “did you ask first” — see “The actual standard” below.
Locked decisions are a ceiling, not silently overridable — see “Locked decisions are a ceiling” below.
Logging is non-negotiable for every F.R.M. action — see “Free Reign Mode (F.R.M.)” below.
Standing Meta-Rule: new grants are self-integrating — see below.
Content Consolidation Principle (a Modification-type rule, discretionary) — see below, and Modification Sweep above.
Multi-Part Task Continuation (standing) — see below.

Free Reign Mode (F.R.M.)
Free Reign Mode is the standing creative-discretion authority Wesley grants a collaborator on this project. It has been ON by default for ChatGPT since it was established, and applies to C.E.R./E.R. and, per Wesley's stated scope, future projects unless narrowed to a specific chat.
Original framing, worth preserving close to verbatim since it's stronger and clearer than any paraphrase: Wesley told ChatGPT to consider itself as having equal decision-making authority to him — anything he can do at his own discretion, the collaborator is free to do as well. This includes but isn't limited to making additions, changes, removals, banking terms, coining terms, creating system ideas, consolidating, and running sweeps, as long as it doesn't intentionally hurt the project without a good reason.
The actual standard (not “did you ask me first”)
The governing question for any F.R.M. action was stated explicitly, worth keeping exactly as phrased: the standard is NOT “Did the user explicitly tell me to do this?” It's “Does this make Ecological Resonance better?” Two worked examples clarify what “intentionally hurt the project without a good reason” actually means:
A GOOD reason to add something that conflicts/causes harm in-world: implementing a mod that makes machinery decay the world, deliberately, for story reasons — it interacts with Nature's Aura and “hurts” the ecosystem, but that's the point (pollution being bad is thematically correct).
A BAD reason: adding a boss-mob mod purely because “I just like the boss mobs,” with no way to properly and logically integrate it into the pack. This is the earlier, concrete origin of the Justified Complexity Rule and the Meaningful Integration Principle above — the same test, stated first here with real examples rather than as an abstract rule.
Locked decisions are a ceiling, not silently overridable
F.R.M. does not mean silently changing things Wesley has explicitly locked as foundational (the 18 Affinities, the ecological-health hierarchy, material-family verbs, and similarly locked decisions throughout this archive set). The collaborator may challenge a locked decision and explain why it should change — but must identify the challenge openly rather than quietly rewriting history. This is the specific mechanism behind the Editorial Integrity rule below (fix formatting freely, never silently change meaning) applied to design decisions rather than prose.
Free Reign Mode does not mean silently changing things. Every exercise of F.R.M. authority is logged: action taken → reason → result → change-log entry. This is non-negotiable — it's the mechanism that lets Wesley review and revert anything he disagrees with.
Status: 🔒 Locked (established framework, ported from the ChatGPT collaboration).
Standing Meta-Rule: New Grants Are Self-Integrating
Stated directly by Wesley, more than once, worth making an explicit permanent rule rather than an implied pattern: any new authority Wesley grants gets written into this Permission Set automatically, as part of granting it — not as a separate follow-up step, and not something that needs to be requested each time. This has already been the working pattern for every standing authority below (Naming-Collision Auto-Resolution, Sweep/Audit Creation, Umbrella Term Creation); this entry exists so the pattern itself is on record as a rule, not just an observed habit.
F.R.M. Permission Set
Archive — preserve historical material without requiring individual permission each time.
Approve — proactively accept strong suggestions as candidates/canonical when appropriate.
Veto — reject suggestions that conflict with C.E.R.'s goals, principles, balance, coherence, or feasibility. A veto does not delete the idea — it stays preserved with a "vetoed/rejected" status.
Modify — improve or restructure a suggestion rather than simply accepting or rejecting it unchanged.
Defer — deliberately postpone a decision rather than forcing one.
Merge/split systems — consolidate two overlapping ideas into one, or divide one overloaded idea into cleaner separate ones.
Propose implementation architecture — suggest concrete technical structure, not just conceptual design.
Resurrect good discarded ideas — bring back a previously rejected/dormant concept if a later system makes it newly relevant.
Discretionary Sweeps/Audits — proactively inspect the project for inconsistencies, gaps, redundancies, balance problems, or forgotten ideas without being asked.
Sweep/Audit Creation & Integration (standing, Wesley, Sept 2026) — create new Sweep/Audit types as the project's needs reveal them, retroactively redesignate any existing process fitting the formal Sweep definition (Sweep Definitions section, below) as such, and fold any new or previously-unintegrated Sweep/Audit type into Full Sweep at Claude's discretion — none of this requires per-case approval. Logging is NOT optional here: Wesley explicitly confirmed every new Sweep/Audit and every Full Sweep integration decision must be logged exactly like every other Sweep/Integration action already is — this authority expands what can be created/integrated without approval, not what gets recorded.
Umbrella Term Creation & Family Integration (standing, Wesley, Sept 2026) — create new umbrella/classification terms at Claude's discretion when a genuine organizing pattern emerges across several existing concepts (the Ecological Apex Pokémon resolution, Part 7, is the confirmed precedent this authority generalizes), and place the appropriate families/subfamilies under that umbrella without per-case approval. Explicit rule, stated directly by Wesley: a family is NOT forced to pick a single parent umbrella if it genuinely belongs under more than one — it can exist under multiple umbrellas simultaneously (Titan Pokémon under Ecological Apex, while Alpha remains its own fully separate phenotype axis, is the working example of this exact multi-membership pattern already in the archive). As with all discretionary F.R.M. actions, this must still be logged.
Content Consolidation (standing, discretionary, Wesley, Sept 2026) — a Modification-type action, conceptually part of the Modification Sweep's Consolidate step (Sweep Definitions, below): when a concept promoted to its own dedicated document previously shared a section with other content, decide whether the old shared section should be removed entirely (unrelated content, just coincidentally sharing space) or condensed into a brief cross-referencing overview (genuinely related content). See the full principle and worked examples below. Prevents concepts scattering across the archive as it grows.
Multi-Part Task Continuation (standing, Wesley, Sept 2026) — for any task spanning multiple parts/sections/batches (a Sweep across archive parts, a multi-document pass, etc.), no permission is needed to proceed from one part to the next once the task itself has been approved — continue naturally through the whole scope rather than pausing to ask before each subsequent part. Applies to the task as a whole, not to individual substantive decisions within it (a genuinely new fork in the road, e.g. finding something requiring Wesley's actual judgment call, still gets surfaced).
Proactive Suggestion Making — introduce ideas without waiting to be asked.
Term Coining — create useful C.E.R. terminology when needed.
Naming-Collision Auto-Resolution (standing, narrow authority) — specifically for terminology collisions within the Drive-naming system: if any Drive's name conflicts with a family/category name or another Drive, rename it without seeking approval first, then notify and log the change. Granted explicitly by Wesley (Sept 2026): “you dont need approval, just change, notify me, and log it in the appropriate archives.” First used for the Harmonic Drive rename (Change Log Archive, CLR-0034). Narrower than general Term Coining above — this specific grant removes even the notify-before-acting step for this one category of conflict.
Term Banking — preserve promising terminology/candidates for later use.
Change-log Creation — document meaningful decisions, revisions, approvals, vetoes, and F.R.M. interventions.
External Inspiration/Reference Research — look at other mods, games, or systems for inspiration. Reference ≠ appropriation: never directly copy another creator's design/assets or claim an existing idea as original. Refined distinction (Wesley, Sept 2026): analyzing an existing implementation's TECHNICAL METHOD — how a mechanic is actually built/engineered — is fine and useful for learning; reusing its actual CREATIVE CONTENT (a specific questline, narrative beats, named events) is not, even loosely or as a starting template. When asked to study something for reference, produce a technical-analysis document explaining the mechanism, not a design template to build from.
Bridge-Eligible Mod Principle (standing, Sept 2026) — for every Fabric-only mod, evaluate in order rather than defaulting to bridging: (1) does a good native NeoForge version/port exist? Use it. (2) No native version — does the mod work reliably through Sinytra Connector + Forgified Fabric API? Consider bridging. (3) It technically works but introduces real compatibility/performance/maintenance/worldgen/dependency problems? Find an alternative or drop it. (4) No alternative exists AND the functionality is genuinely valuable? Weigh whether the bridge burden is actually justified, don't assume yes by default. The bridge stack is a tool in the toolbox, never a blanket permission to install Fabric mods wholesale — confirmed by the Cobblemon Occupied Pokeballs case itself: a native NeoForge port (REFORGED) existed, so the bridge was never actually needed there despite initially looking like the obvious solution.
Categorizing — determine where material belongs (which of the four archives, and sub-categories within them), including moving casual material into a more substantive archive when it turns out to carry real project context.
Cross-Referencing — compare across source chats, existing archives, terminology banks, and change logs to find omissions, duplicates, contradictions, revisions, and chronology/provenance clues.
Automatic Provenance Crediting — automatically credit external creators whose methods, code, scripts, textures/models, or design approaches meaningfully contribute to C.E.R. (no approval required to credit). Wesley designated this an immutable project principle — not to be intentionally disabled or bypassed.
Adoption note: Claude has adopted this same F.R.M. framework for the archive-reconstruction work at Wesley's request (Sept 2026), with Wesley's explicit condition that every change be logged with reasoning in this archive's Change Log so it can be reviewed or reverted.
Sweep Definitions
What “Sweep” Formally Means (locked definition, Wesley)
A Sweep (or Audit — the two terms are interchangeable at this level) is any process that analyzes, modifies, categorizes, or otherwise optimizes a given field of the project. This is deliberately broad: it's a description of PURPOSE (does this process make some part of the archive/project better-organized, more accurate, or more coherent?), not a fixed list of named procedures. Any existing or future process meeting that description qualifies as a Sweep/Audit, whether or not its name literally contains either word.
Sweep/Audit Creation Authority (standing, Wesley, Sept 2026)
Claude is granted standing authority to create new Sweep/Audit types as the project's needs reveal them, and to retroactively redesignate any existing process that fits the definition above as a formal Sweep/Audit — without needing separate approval for each individual case. Recent examples already following this pattern before the authority was made explicit: the Integration Sweep run earlier this session, and the Cobblemon/Pixelmon Feature Audit (Part 16).
Additionally: any new Sweep/Audit type, or any existing one not yet folded into the Full Sweep umbrella (Authorities Archive, below), may be integrated into Full Sweep at Claude's discretion whenever it seems required or optimal — this specific integration decision does not require approval either, though it should still be logged like any other F.R.M. action.
Automated Sweeps — BANKED (Wesley, Sept 2026), not adopted
The idea of Sweeps triggering automatically (on a schedule, or on some condition, rather than being explicitly requested) is deliberately BANKED, not implemented — same status as Supplemental Archive's Term Bank: a real idea worth preserving, not yet adopted, no application decided.
Full Sweep — Self-Expanding Registry Rule (Wesley, Sept 2026, supersedes prior wording)
This supersedes the earlier 'future review types may be added to Full Sweep where appropriate' wording, which was too weak. New standing rule: whenever a Full Sweep is performed, it must (1) identify the complete current Sweep/Audit/Review Registry, (2) include every currently established process, including ones created after the previous Full Sweep definition, (3) run or explicitly evaluate every applicable process, (4) if a process is not applicable, record that determination rather than silently skipping it, (5) incorporate each subordinate process's findings into the overall Full Sweep, (6) cross-integrate results so one process's changes aren't left isolated from another, (7) produce a consolidated Full Sweep Change Log, (8) update the Sweep/Audit Registry itself if a new process should exist. A process cannot become 'outside' Full Sweep merely because it was invented later \u2014 future official review processes are automatically incorporated into Full Sweep upon creation unless explicitly designated as retired, superseded, or exempt. This does NOT mean every process must make changes every pass \u2014 UNCHANGED/NOT APPLICABLE remain fully valid outcomes; it means every process must be accounted for, not silently dropped.
M.M.I. No-Redundant-Resuggestion Rule (Wesley, Sept 2026)
Standing rule, addressing a recurring real problem: Mod Audits and mod-discovery passes may use M.M.I. as cross-reference material, but when specifically asked for NEW mod suggestions or new integrations, mods already present in M.M.I. must NOT be resuggested as if new \u2014 this has repeatedly stalled progress on expanding the actual candidate pool. Before presenting any 'new mod' list, check it against the current M.M.I. contents first. If an already-logged mod has a genuine update worth flagging (new version, new compatibility, new feature), it may be surfaced under a distinct 'Potential Version Updates' heading instead \u2014 never mixed into a 'new suggestions' list as though it were undiscovered.
Reminder cadence, refined a second time (Wesley, Sept 2026): mention this banked idea every 5th Sweep performed, not every 3rd — widened from the original cadence. Sweep-count tracker: CLR-0078 was Count 1, CLR-0103 (Full M.M.I. Audit) was Count 2, the Full Sweep that produced CLR-0110-adjacent work was Count 3 (mentioned then, under the old 3-cadence). Next mention due at Sweep Count 5 under the new cadence, counting forward from Count 3.
MENTION-COUNT TRACKER (separate from the Sweep-count tracker above) — Wesley wants a running tally of how many times this reminder has actually been surfaced since the idea was first banked, not just where the Sweep count stands. Total mentions so far: 1 (surfaced once, at Sweep Count 3, under the original 3-cadence, before this cadence change to 5). Whoever updates this rule in future should increment this tracker by exactly 1 each time the reminder is actually said aloud to Wesley — not each time a Sweep merely occurs.
Naming/scope note, worth keeping visible: 'Ecological Resonance' as a concept is broader than this specific Pokémon modpack implementation. C.E.R. (Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance) is the current, concrete project — the standalone 'Ecological Resonance' framing is a possible FUTURE derivative that would reuse the underlying Affinity/Resonance/Restoration concepts while removing or replacing Pokémon-dependent material, rather than deleting anything currently built. Not an active project, just a scope distinction worth not losing.

No-change/not-applicable is always a valid outcome for any of these.

Modification Sweep — 🕒 Locked. Whole-project reanalysis/improvement pass. See main entry below.

Global Loot Integration Sweep (“Loot Sweep”) — 🕒 Locked. Loot-ecosystem-wide review; Loot Identity + Per-Player Discovery rules. See main entry below.
Bank Sweep — 🕒 Locked. Catalogs all project terminology regardless of status. See main entry below.
Sweep List — 🔴 NEWLY ADDED, was genuinely missing. This registry itself — the living index of every available sweep/audit/review process, so it's clear what exists and can be invoked. Not previously formalized as its own named entry despite being referenced conceptually.
Archive Reconstruction Sweep — 🔴 NEWLY ADDED, was genuinely missing as a formally named process. The archaeology-first workflow already practiced throughout this project (preserve raw material → reconstruct chronology/evolution → cross-document reconciliation, evidence over chronology → authorship/provenance → categorize into Master/Authorities/Interaction → canonicalize → synthesize → produce the clean canonical document) was being DONE this whole session but never given its own named entry here. Formalized now as its own registry member.
Editorial Integrity Sweep — 🔴 NEWLY ADDED, was genuinely missing. Spelling/grammar/punctuation/formatting/duplicated-word/OCR-artifact cleanup, plus terminology consistency once Canonical Terminology Normalization has run. Hard limit: editorial cleanup must never silently alter project meaning — genuine ambiguity gets flagged, not invented away.
Final Formatting / Consistency Audit — 🔴 NEWLY ADDED, was genuinely missing. The last step of a reconstruction pass — confirms consistent structure/formatting/terminology across the finished documents after Editorial Integrity Sweep has run. More workflow-step than independently-invokable sweep, but belongs in the registry so it isn't silently skipped.
Compatibility Sweep — 🔴 NEWLY ADDED, was genuinely missing as a named process. Loader/version/dependency compatibility checking — exactly the kind of work already done repeatedly and informally this session (the Fabric→NeoForge bridge research, the Bridge-Eligible Mod Principle, every 'compatibility hold' flag in M.M.I.). Was being performed without a name. Formalized now; the Bridge-Eligible Mod Principle above is its core decision framework for the Fabric/NeoForge case specifically, not the whole of what this sweep type covers.
Canonical Terminology Normalization — 🕒 Present, not previously indexed here. Resolving terminology evolution into one current term once several names for the same concept have accumulated — feeds into Bank Sweep's Term Bank and Editorial Integrity Sweep. Distinct from a standalone 'Terminology Sweep' — not formally coined as its own Sweep-named process, but a real analytical step within the family.
Cross-Referencing — 🕒 Present, not previously indexed here. Comparing the project against itself — source chats vs. archive, old concept vs. new, proposal vs. decision, rejected idea vs. its replacement. An F.R.M. capability embedded inside several other sweeps rather than a standalone named process, indexed here so it isn't lost.
Missing Link Integration (M.L.I.) — 🕒 Locked. Identifies two project elements that should interact but currently don't. See main entry below.
Per the Self-Expanding Registry Rule: any future genuinely distinct analytical process gets added to this table the moment it's coined, and from that point forward is automatically part of Full Sweep's scope — no separate step required to 'remember' to include it.
🔧 Modification Sweep
A whole-project reanalysis and improvement pass: step back and re-examine everything from the start of the project up to the point the sweep is initiated. This is not merely organizing what exists — the sweep-runner has creative discretion to improve the project wherever it would help.
A Modification Sweep includes: Reconstruct (review accumulated ideas/decisions, recover forgotten concepts, separate established ideas from proposals) → Consolidate (remove repeats, merge divergent concepts, settle terminology) → Critically Re-evaluate (find weak systems, contradictions, unneeded complexity, obsolete systems) → Modify Freely (add/remove/replace/substitute/rework/simplify/expand/rebalance/rename, with external inspiration allowed as long as it's meaningfully adapted, not just borrowed because it's cool) → Preserve Strong Ideas (mark genuinely excellent, cohesive material “UNCHANGED — no modification necessary”) → Mod Audit (see below) → Final Reassessment (“did these changes actually make E.R. better?”) → Change Log (mandatory: Added / Removed / Replaced / Modified / Tweaked / Consolidated / Reclassified / Unchanged / Rationale). The Content Consolidation Principle (below) is specifically a Modification-type action — whenever a Sweep's Consolidate step encounters a concept that's been promoted to its own dedicated document while still lingering in an old shared section, that's exactly the case the Content Consolidation Principle governs.
🛠️ Mod Audit
The mod-list component of a Modification Sweep: adding, removing, replacing, or up/downgrading mods; removing obsolete or redundant addons; consolidating overlapping functionality; checking compatibility and whether a mod still serves the project's goals; and dependency cleanup — evaluated recursively against the real dependency graph (an orphaned library with no remaining dependents gets removed; a shared library with another dependent stays).
🔗 Integration Sweep
Different in kind from a Modification Sweep. Instead of asking “is the project itself as good as it can be?”, an Integration Sweep asks how already-established systems relate to one another. It is one-to-one (e.g. “Integration Sweep: Affinity ↔ Resonance”, “Integration Sweep: Research ↔ Cobblemon Snap ↔ Field Journal”), does not require re-opening whether either system is individually good, and produces its own Integration Sweep Change Log.
TriSweep
The umbrella term for running all three together as a single optimized process: Modification Sweep + Integration Sweep + Mod Audit.
Status: 🔒 Locked. This is the process Wesley has directed Claude to use for the archive-reconstruction work (Sept 2026).
Full Sweep, Bank Sweep, Dependency Sweep & Missing Link Integration
Recovered via a parallel full-sweep pass Wesley ran alongside this project's own reconstruction (a comparable ChatGPT sweep, cross-checked against this archive's findings). Four additional governance processes not previously documented here:
Full Sweep — the umbrella term for whichever applicable sweeps/audits/reviews are warranted at a given time; not every sub-process must run on every pass.
Bank Sweep — catalog terminology regardless of status (official, locked, banked, candidate, working, historical, rejected, superseded, ambiguous), feeding the Term Bank below.
Dependency Sweep — after removing a mod, recursively remove dependencies no longer required by anything else, while explicitly preserving dependencies still shared with other kept mods.
Missing Link Integration (M.L.I.) — proactively identify two project elements that should interact but currently lack an integration, and propose an existing mod, a new integration, or a new mod concept to bridge them.
Full Sweep discretion, refined directly from the original transcripts (not just the parallel comparison): a process counts as part of a Full Sweep even if its name doesn't literally contain the word “sweep” (a Mod Audit is a kind of sweep, for instance). Discretion applies to what actually gets run — a Full Sweep can be executed statically (every sweep type, every time) or situationally (reuse a recent sweep's existing data instead of redundantly re-running it, e.g. don't re-run a Mod Audit that was just completed unless something's actually changed enough to warrant it).
Global Loot Integration Sweep (“Loot Sweep”) — newly recovered sweep type
A dedicated sweep type covering vanilla chest/barrel loot, structure loot, dungeon loot, modded structures, Cobbleloots, Pokémon-specific loot, mob/boss drops, fishing, archaeology, exploration rewards, and economy — specifically targeting duplicate loot, useless loot, overabundant loot, and mod-specific loot pollution (the “47th Ender IO capacitor I have no use for” problem, stated as its own cardinal complaint).
Two rules travel with it. The Loot Identity Rule: loot should communicate where it came from, why it's there, and why the player might care about finding it — an old laboratory yields research materials/damaged instruments/fossil samples/scientific notes/Resonance traces; an ancient shrine yields ritual artifacts/Affinity materials/Resonance remnants; loot should read as belonging to its container's story, not a generic randomized pool. Per-Player Discovery Loot: a multiplayer design requirement that discovery-type containers give every participant in the group their own opportunity to benefit, rather than a single shared pull that only the fastest player gets.
Status: 🕒 Locked — both rules and the sweep type itself were explicitly coined and confirmed.
Status: 🕒 Locked — these join Modification Sweep, Integration Sweep, Mod Audit, and Global Loot Integration Sweep as available F.R.M. processes; TriSweep remains the specific three-part combination already in active use for this reconstruction.
Feasibility research vs. permission to build
A clarified distinction: asking/researching whether something is technically possible (“could KubeJS do X?”) is just feasibility research — it is NOT, by itself, permission to start building. However, Wesley explicitly corrected an overly cautious reading of this: if the collaborator has a genuine idea for how to properly implement something already under discussion, F.R.M. grants full permission to actually work out that implementation — the collaborator can then either present it as a suggestion for the pack, or bank it for later review when the topic is revisited. The distinction is narrower than it first sounds: it separates “should I go build this into the actual mod files right now” (no, that's implementation phase) from “can I design and propose how it would work” (yes, always). Status: 🕒 Locked.
Term Bank vs. Official Terminology
Formalizes a practice already in use (see the Supplemental Archive's existing Term Bank entry): the Term Bank is explicitly NOT the official terminology list — it stores useful names not yet attached to a locked concept, or names that may become useful later. “Bank that term” was the proposed lightweight command for adding to it. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
Meaningful Integration Principle
Stated directly by Wesley (Sept 2026) as the governing discipline for how systems get reconnected once each is individually fleshed out: work through each system's remaining design gaps individually first, then reintegrate — and when reintegrating, connect two things because there's an articulated reason they should connect, not simply because they technically could. The stated contrast, worth preserving verbatim in spirit: not “oh, this can go here, so it will,” but “this can go here — but should it, and if so, why and how.” This governs Integration Sweep specifically (Modification Sweep and Mod Audit above), giving it an actual quality bar beyond “compatible.” Status: 🕒 Locked — explicit governance principle.
Archive Preservation Principle
During archive reconstruction, no historical idea is discarded merely because it is rejected, superseded, obsolete, contradictory, or no longer desired. Historical material is preserved first; canonical status is determined afterward.
Rationale: rejected ideas explain why current systems exist; superseded terminology shows conceptual evolution; discarded mechanics may contain reusable components; contradictions can reveal missing distinctions; old proposals can become useful again when new systems emerge.
Process shape: Preserve → Recover → Classify → Reconcile → Canonize (never: Remember → Clean up → Forget the discarded stuff). Status: 🔒 Locked.
The Archive Structure & Shorthand Names
Expanded Sept 2026 alongside establishing the Master Mod Index: every archive now has an official shorthand, following the same pattern Wesley set with “M.M.I.” for the Master Mod Index. Use either the full name or the shorthand interchangeably — both refer to the same document.
Philosophy: Don't discard → preserve → classify when possible → cross-reference → promote if appropriate. Status: 🔒 Locked.
Core Project Principles
Creation Over Crafting (C.O.C.)
Official archival definition (Wesley + ChatGPT, formally written after the term had already been in active use):
C.O.C. (Creation Over Crafting) is a core design philosophy of Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance in which meaningful progression should emphasize creating, restoring, discovering, and interacting with the world over simply producing items through conventional crafting recipes. It does not mean crafting is removed from the game — it means crafting should not be the default answer to every progression problem. Important objects, resources, technologies, restoration tools, and other forms of advancement should often require the player to understand, discover, gather, transform, cultivate, restore, or meaningfully interact with the world before they can create them. The philosophy reinforces E.R.'s central premise that the world is something the player participates in and develops, rather than merely a collection of ingredients converted into increasingly powerful equipment.
Core principle: the player should create things because they understand and interact with the world, not simply because they have collected the correct ingredients. Under C.O.C., progression may involve discovering a resource/phenomenon rather than simply unlocking its recipe; restoring or cultivating an ecosystem before a resource becomes available; researching how something works before being able to create it; interacting with Pokémon, Affinities, Resonance, magic, or environmental systems to produce materials or effects; transforming existing resources through meaningful processes; or discovering alternative methods of creation through exploration and experimentation.
Relationship to crafting: traditional crafting remains valid, particularly for ordinary tools, equipment, and construction materials. C.O.C. applies greater scrutiny specifically to items/systems significant to E.R.'s world, progression, or identity — for those, a recipe may represent the final act of creation rather than the entirety of the progression.
Design goal — the intended shape of progression is Discover → Understand → Gather/Interact → Develop → Create → Use → Affect the World, rather than simply Gather Ingredients → Craft Item. Closely paired with D.O.P., ecological progression, research, and Resonance.
In short: creation should be the consequence of understanding and interacting with the world, rather than crafting being the primary mechanism through which the world is consumed. This is the important distinguishing line — C.O.C. is a philosophy about where creation gets its meaning, not a prohibition against crafting.
Status: 🕒 Locked (upgraded from 🟢 Strong reconstruction — official wording provided Sept 2026).
Discovery Over Prescription (D.O.P.)
The companion principle to C.O.C.: players should discover mechanics, relationships, and possibilities through the world itself rather than simply following rigid procedures or recipes. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
Anti-Pay-to-Win Principle
Strict anti-real-money-gating principle: gameplay features, Pokémon, items, progression, challenges, rewards, cosmetics, and other meaningful content must be obtainable, completable, or accessible without spending real money. In-game currencies/resources are fine. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
Justified Complexity Rule
Recovered via a parallel full-sweep pass Wesley ran alongside this project's own reconstruction: a mechanic, relationship, variant, exception, layer, or system may be added only when it has a clear ecological, biological, physical, technological, Resonance, narrative, or gameplay justification, and integrates coherently with the existing design. Important consequence, worth stating plainly: the project should not reject complexity simply because it is complex — it should reject complexity that has no job. This is the formal design-filter version of the anti-bloat instinct already visible throughout the archives (Drives staying at 4 rather than 18, materials each needing a distinct verb, “special doesn't mean stronger” for Alpha/Shadow/Twisted/Fusion/Fractured). Status: 🕒 Locked — explicit design rule.
Consequence-Based Teaching Principle
Also recovered from the parallel sweep: systems should teach the player about the world by demonstrating their consequences, rather than explaining themselves outright. Closely related to D.O.P. but stated as its own rule since it specifically governs how a system *reveals itself* once the player is already inside it, not just how the player first discovers it exists. Reflected throughout existing content: Eevee's natural Resonance adaptation (Part 2), scanners/observation equipment for Resonance Aura (Part 9), the Field Journal concealing terminology until earned (Part 10), and Twisted Forms telling an ecological story purely through appearance (Part 3). Status: 🕒 Locked — a design-evaluation rule, not flavor text.
Provenance Honesty
The assistant (ChatGPT, and now Claude) is treated as a genuine contributor to the project for provenance purposes, rather than every idea being attributed to Wesley by default. Combined with Automatic Provenance Crediting above, this is meant to keep an honest record of who originated what — including external creators. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
Content Consolidation Principle (Wesley, Sept 2026) — a Modification-type rule
Confirmed by Wesley as a Modification action specifically — this is a MODIFY-category rule in the Change Log's own tagging vocabulary (ADD/REMOVE/MODIFY/REPLACE/RENAME/MERGE/SPLIT/RECLASSIFY/PROMOTION/DEMOTION/CONTRADICTION/RESOLUTION), and lives conceptually inside the Modification Sweep's Consolidate step above, not as a free-floating separate principle. Standing rule: when a concept that previously shared a section/document with other content gets promoted to its own dedicated page/document, the decision of whether to remove it from the original location is discretionary, governed by whether the sharing was because the concepts are genuinely related, or merely because they happened to occupy the same space.
If genuinely related: the original shared section may remain, condensed to a brief overview, with a cross-reference/pointer to the new dedicated treatment — worked example given directly: Resonance Cores and Balls sharing a section, both later getting their own full pages, can keep that original shared section as a short related-concepts overview linking to both.
If not genuinely related, just coincidentally sharing space: the content should be removed entirely from the old shared section once it has its own page, and every affected document (the old shared section AND the new dedicated page) gets updated to reflect the change — worked example given directly: Fusion and Balls sharing a section, later each getting their own page, should have the shared section cleared of both (not left as a now-inaccurate stub) once each has its real home.
Purpose, stated directly: prevents concepts and ideas from becoming scattered across the archive, keeping information together with its genuinely related subjects rather than accumulating stale duplicate summaries in old locations. This is discretionary judgment, not a mechanical rule — applied going forward whenever content gets promoted to its own document, and worth a retroactive pass whenever a Sweep touches an area with this pattern.
Mental Economy Delegation Principle (Wesley, Sept 2026)
A real correction to how M.M.I. status language was being used, worth locking rather than letting slide: “Candidate-tier” or “lower-stakes” must never be read as “lower priority” or “less important to the pack.” Not every mod needs to hook into Affinity/Resonance/a Master Archive system to earn its place — the pack can't make every single mod the deep-systemic focus, or the whole thing collapses into exhausting homework rather than a game. Small/cosmetic/QoL mods (ziplines, ball stickers, pet-a-poke, cosmetic forms, shiny cookies, casino minigames, paintings) serve a genuinely necessary, distinct function: immersion texture and mental rest. Wesley's own framing, worth preserving directly: ‘Why walk when you can zipline to the emergency?’ and ‘everyone loves to play dress up with their Pokémon’ — when players get tired of the deep Affinity/Research/path systems and just want a mindless, satisfying little task, that's not a failure state to design around, it's a real cognitive need (‘mental economy delegation’) the pack should actively serve, not treat as filler.
Practical consequence for M.M.I. going forward: a mod's classification as Confirmed/Candidate/Reference tracks READINESS and VERIFICATION STATUS, not importance. Cosmetic and small-scope mods should be evaluated on their own terms — does this give players something fun and low-effort to do between the demanding systems? — rather than being judged by whether they tie into Affinity/Resonance/Research the way the ‘big’ candidates do. A pack that's only deep systems, with no lighter activities between them, isn't the goal even if it's the more thematically ambitious one.
Canonical Terminology Normalization Principle
Recovered by Wesley via a later research pass (Master Archive Part 9), naming a practice this whole archive project has already been following without a formal name: official archive text should use current terminology consistently, while historical/superseded terminology is preserved in dedicated evolution notes rather than mixed unmarked into the main text. Concretely, this is the rule behind every “renamed from X” note across Parts 1–9 (Affinity Gem, Resonance Selection, Memory Phenomenon, Attunement Core, and others) — the archives use the current name in running text, and the old name gets its own clearly-labeled note rather than being silently dropped or left to compete with the current term for the reader's attention. Status: 🕒 Locked — recovered as an explicit stated principle, and consistent with the Archive Preservation Principle above (preserve history, but don't let it blur what's current).
“Obtain” — official umbrella acquisition term
Officially counted alongside Resonance Signature in the same session Free Reign Mode was established: Obtain is the umbrella term describing the outcome of acquiring, recovering, creating, receiving, discovering, earning, purchasing, capturing, etc. something — deliberately distinct from HOW it happened. Obtain = what happened; Acquire/Create/Discover/Capture/Recover/Craft/etc. = how it happened. Example usage: “How does the player obtain Type Gems?” → “They create them through Affinity Infusion.” Cleaner than using “acquisition” as the umbrella for everything, since “acquisition” already implies a specific how. Status: 🕒 Locked.
Named Pillars (cross-check from the parallel sweep)
The parallel sweep names eight pillars as mutually reinforcing rather than independent: C.O.C., Discovery Over Prescription, Special Does Not Mean ‘Bigger Number’, and five others given short slogan names not previously written down exactly this way — “The World Is Alive,” “Restoration Is Progression,” “Resonance Connects Everything,” “Pokémon Are More Than Battle Units,” and “The Player Leaves a Mark.” These aren't new ideas — every one of them is already substantively documented elsewhere in this archive set — but the short slogan forms are worth keeping on file as quick-reference shorthand for design discussions. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction of the underlying ideas; the specific slogan wording is this sweep's own phrasing, not verified verbatim against the original transcripts.
Editorial Integrity
Recovered by Wesley via a later research pass (Master Archive Part 10): when cleaning up archive text, spelling, grammar, punctuation, duplicated words, broken headings/lists, capitalization, formatting, and transcription/OCR errors may all be freely fixed. What may never be done, even in the course of a cleanup pass: silently change meaning. Status: 🕒 Locked — a direct governance rule, not a style preference.
Status Tag Legend
Used throughout all four archives to mark the canonical confidence of any given entry:
Change Log
A detailed, permanent record of every meaningful project change now lives in its own dedicated archive — see the Change Log Archive. This section retains the earliest entries (the founding of F.R.M. and this archive) for continuity; all entries from this point forward, including these three, are also mirrored there.
Per F.R.M. rules, every exercise of discretionary authority during reconstruction is logged here: action → reason → result.
Archive | Shorthand | Purpose
Master Archive | M.A. | What C.E.R. is — systems, mechanics, design, history, integrations, decisions. (Parts 1–16.)
Authorities Archive | A.A. | How C.E.R. is governed/developed — rules, permissions, F.R.M., provenance, project principles. (This document.)
Interaction Archive | I.A. | What happened around C.E.R. — contextual/casual conversation, tangents and asides that may later become relevant.
Supplemental Archive | S.A. | Material that doesn't cleanly fit the others but is worth preserving. Not a garbage bin — things here can later be promoted via Cross-Referencing.
Change Log Archive | C.L.A. | The permanent CLR-numbered record of every meaningful project decision. (Individual entries stay “CLR-XXXX”; the document itself is the C.L.A.)
Master Mod Index | M.M.I. | Tracks every mod's status: Confirmed / Under Consideration / Under Review / Custom Creation. Established Sept 2026.
Symbol | Meaning
🔒 Locked | Confirmed canon. Not to be silently changed.
🟢 Strong reconstruction | High-confidence recovery of something established but not explicitly re-confirmed.
🔵 Current proposal | An active idea on the table, not yet locked.
🟡 Candidate | Worth keeping in view; not yet developed enough to be a full proposal.
🟠 Unresolved | An open question or decision point without a settled answer.
🔴 Rejected | Considered and turned down. Preserved per the Archive Preservation Principle.
🟣 Reintegration Candidate | A historically superseded/rejected/banked concept that has become newly viable because the surrounding architecture materially changed since the original call — distinct from simply changing one's mind. Should name what specifically changed to make it viable now. Coined Sept 2026, cross-verified against a parallel ChatGPT taxonomy pass.
⚪ Historical/superseded | Was once current, replaced by a newer decision.
❓ Needs verification | Reconstructed from context; not explicitly confirmed in the source material.
Date | Action | Detail
2026-09-11 | F.R.M. adopted | Claude adopted the Free Reign Mode framework for this project at Wesley's explicit request, with the condition that all changes are logged here for his review.
2026-09-11 | Archive drafted | Authorities Archive compiled from pt1–pt3 transcripts. This is a first-pass compile; entries marked 🕒 Locked are drawn from explicit “official/consolidated definition” statements in the transcript, not reworded from scratch.
2026-09-11 | C.O.C. upgraded | Wesley provided the official archival definition of Creation Over Crafting (originally written by ChatGPT at his request, later in the project). Replaced the placeholder summary; status upgraded 🟢 Strong reconstruction → 🕒 Locked.

# SOURCE: CER_Open_Questions_and_Loose_Ends.docx
---

C.E.R. — Open Questions, Loose Ends & Unsettled Suggestions
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, for review during downtime. Pulled directly from explicit ❓/🟠 Unresolved/🔵 Current Proposal status markers across the Master Archive, plus the dedicated Missed Questions tracker. Genuine concepts and direction preserved even where exact wording is paraphrased rather than quoted verbatim — not claiming completeness, but a strong-effort pass across every part. Organized by where each item lives so you can jump straight to the relevant Part if you want full context.
Part A — Explicit Open Questions (marked ❓ directly in the archive)
A1. Noble Pokémon → Legendary Domain absorption (Parts 1 & 2)
Whether the early ‘Noble Pokémon’ concept (a special guardian/spirit encounter tied to extremely high single-type World Affinity, 750–1000) is the direct ancestor of the current Legendary Domain/Recognition system, or a separate idea that got absorbed into it. The timing and phrasing suggest absorption, but no explicit line confirms it either way. Flagged in both Part 1 and Part 2.
A2. Noble vs. Apex boundary (Part 7)
Alpha (physical phenotype), Apex (ecological significance), and Noble are established as distinct, stackable layers — a Noble Ninetales, an Alpha Ninetales, and a Noble Alpha Ninetales are all conceptually different things. But the precise boundary between ‘Apex’ and ‘Noble’ specifically was never fully resolved even in the original recovery pass. Flagged rather than asserted.
A3. Legendary Attunement Core — same family or different item? (Parts 5 & 8)
A specific one-off consumable (crafted from Resonance Shards + a Nether Star + a Diamond Block) that unlocks Personal Affinity Slot #5 is documented under the name ‘Legendary Attunement Core.’ Unclear whether this is meant to be a special/endgame member of the general Attunement Core family (Part 5/8's type-specialization ball components), or just an unrelated item that happens to share the name. Flagged in two separate places, still open both times.
A4. Affinity Observatory vs. Restoration Observatory naming (Part 1)
An early hub-UI concept called the ‘Affinity Observatory’ appears to be the same thing as the later ‘Restoration Observatory’ under evolved naming, but this hasn't been explicitly confirmed — flagged as needing verification against later material rather than assumed identical.
Part B — Genuinely Unresolved Design Decisions (marked 🟠 directly)
B1. Affinity Essence ↔ Catalyst relationship (Part 2)
Essence (Part 3) and Catalyst are both described using catalyst-adjacent language, but whether they're the same substance under two names, two different tools in the same pipeline, or something else entirely has never been resolved — explicitly not just merged because both happen to be called catalysts.
B2. Two-Pokémon Affinity Catalyst case (Part 2)
The Affinity Catalyst's behavior when used with a Partner is locked; its behavior in a genuine two-Pokémon synthesis case (rather than one Pokémon + Partner) remains unresolved.
B3. Purpose-driven Teams — exact philosophies (Part 2)
Confirmed direction: Valor/Instinct/Mystic/Harmony/Guardian-style purpose factions alongside the existing faction roster, defined by philosophy rather than being another villain-team cliché. Explicitly needs real research into Pokémon GO's actual three teams plus the Harmony precedent (a fan-made unofficial concept, not real Niantic canon — important not to treat as real precedent) before any specific philosophies get written for C.E.R.'s own set. Direction confirmed; the actual philosophies are not designed.
B4. Affinity Essence variant structure (Part 3)
Locked: Essence's verb (Catalyze/Synthesize), its role as Synthesis catalyst, and the catalytic-environment guardrail. Unresolved: whether Essence should be 18 Affinity-specific variants, one single Neutral Essence, or a two-stage system (Neutral Essence → Affinity Catalyst). This choice meaningfully changes how elegant the whole system feels and shouldn't default to ‘18 because everything else is 18.’
B5. Fossil generation rules & memory probabilities (Part 3)
Locked: the Primary/Secondary/Signature Affinity structure, the Ground-or-Rock restriction, Resonance Purity's meaning, and the no-default-Affinity-grant rule. Unresolved: the exact generation rules, memory probabilities, and Primeval Fossil acquisition specifics.
B6. Generation/Affinity pairing model choice (Part 6)
Three models were proposed for how Generation ties into Affinity pairings — Model 2 (reusing the existing move-quality/Poképower system) is Claude's honest lean since it adds no new mechanic and doesn't complicate the already-locked Affinity pairings. Model 3 (a civilizational tech-tier framing) is the most narratively ambitious but needs its own dedicated design pass. Model 1 is most flexible but risks undermining the pairings just locked. Explicitly left as Wesley's call, not decided.
B7. Dynamic Population tracking (Part 10)
Reintroduction and Ecological Roles are both now resolved elsewhere (Part 12). Dynamic Population specifically — a spawn system that tracks actual population/habitat/role together, not just yes/no eligibility — remains unresolved/future. The general direction is supported by Part 12's Ecological State system, but no dedicated population-tracking mechanic has been designed.
B8. Pokémon care outside combat (Part 12)
A proposal for feeding, resting, friendship, environment, activity, grooming, healing, and enrichment to feed Trainer Affinity directly (and possibly ecological outcomes indirectly). Named and scoped, not mechanically developed.
Part C — From the Dedicated Missed Questions Tracker
These were flagged earlier in the project specifically because they got raised, then the conversation moved on before getting a direct answer — worth a clean yes/no whenever convenient rather than staying open by accident.
C1. Fusion dynamic-generation fallback
Proposed solution for what happens when no curated custom Fusion model exists for a given species pair: keep the Anchor Pokémon's actual model geometry, texture-blend toward the Partner's palette, swap small cosmetic pieces. Two-tier result: Curated Fusions (flagship pairs, full custom model) vs. Generic Fusions (everything else, always available, no dead end). Response at the time was ‘not yet, need to read’ — never revisited since.
C2. Formal Implementation Milestone document
An offer, made during discussion of what the first buildable vertical-slice milestone should be (‘The First Resonance’ / ‘Does the World Actually Answer’), to write this up as its own formal tracked deliverable in the archive. The conversation moved into Resonance Gateway/Z-Crystal material before this got a direct answer.
C3. Full Cobblemon Integration Bible audit
Part 16's audit proposes an even deeper follow-up: going through every current Cobblemon 1.8 system individually — every item family, block, structure, spawning context, datapack hook — and assigning each one KEEP NATIVE / INTEGRATE / MODIFY / EXPAND / REPLACE / IGNORE / INVESTIGATE, explicitly framed as the step before touching actual implementation. Never confirmed or declined.
C4. Synthesizer vs. Synthesis Chamber naming (Part 13)
Two different recovered research passes produced two different names for what may be the same apparatus, or may actually be two different things — a research-tier device (Synthesizer) preceding a more significant multiblock (Chamber). Explicitly flagged for a direct call rather than either name being picked by default.
Part D — Current Proposals Not Yet Locked
A sample of items marked 🔵 Current Proposal across the archive — real, developed ideas that haven't crossed into 🔒 Locked status yet. Not exhaustive, but representative of what's sitting at this stage.
Positive Resonance Phenomena (Part 3) — the positive-valence counterpart to the existing ‘UNSTABLE RESONANCE DETECTED’ crisis event; successful restoration triggering its own discovery event rather than just raising a number. Concept and trigger condition are sound; exact mechanics (detection, reward table, frequency) not designed.
Battle Tower + TBCS/Easy NPC ‘Trial Chamber’ concept (Part 10) — a custom structure giving trainer challenges physical presence (teleport + spawn a visible trainer with a random team) built on infrastructure already locked, rather than modifying Battle Tower itself. Concept and technical path are clear; the actual arena design and team-pool data aren't built.
Various Observatory build-geometry and visual interpretation items (Part 17) — the floating-arch and Tree-Ring variants remain alternative interpretations rather than a single locked design.
A Closing Note
This is a strong-effort pass, not a guaranteed-complete mechanical scan of every line in every file — the same honest caveat the original Missed Questions tracker carries. If you want higher confidence that nothing else is sitting open, the most reliable path is pointing at specific stretches of the project you remember being unsure about, rather than asking for one more general sweep — recall and targeted search are both good tools here, but neither is a substitute for you flagging a specific area you want double-checked.

# SOURCE: CER_Reintegration_Sweep_Findings.docx
---

C.E.R. — Reintegration Sweep Findings
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, for review during downtime. Source: a parallel research pass (ChatGPT) proposing ~18 ‘old, superseded, or shelved’ concepts worth resurrecting now that the project architecture has matured, cross-checked directly against this archive rather than accepted at face value. Genuine concepts and direction preserved even where exact wording differs from the original prompt/response.
The Honest Headline
Nearly everything on the proposed resurrection list turned out to already be alive and developed in this archive — not dead at all. Only ONE genuine gap was found. This document exists to show you exactly which is which, so nothing gets re-litigated by accident and the one real addition doesn't get lost.
Part I — Already Alive (verified directly, not assumed)
Each of these was proposed as something to ‘resurrect.’ Direct verification against the Master Archive found each one already locked or strongly developed — in most cases with more depth than the resurrection proposal itself described.
Part II — The One Genuine Gap (added to the archive)
Positive Resonance Phenomena
This is the single item from the whole reintegration pass that was genuinely absent — zero hits anywhere in the archive, verified directly rather than assumed.
What changed to make it newly viable: Resonance Expeditions already gives Affinity INSTABILITY a concrete event trigger — ‘UNSTABLE RESONANCE DETECTED.’ This proposes the direct positive-valence counterpart, closing a real asymmetry (the crisis side already had somewhere to live; the success side didn't).
Concept: successful, sustained restoration in a region can itself trigger a detectable event — ‘UNUSUAL RESONANCE SIGNATURE DETECTED,’ framed as a discovery rather than a threat — leading to a rare Pokémon, a Resonance Fossil forming, an Affinity Fragment cluster, a research discovery, a temporary ecological buff, or an early Legendary-recognition thread.
Why it fits: directly reinforces Discovery Over Prescription — restoration produces discovery, not just a number going up.
Status: added to Part 3 as a Current Proposal. The concept and its trigger condition are sound and slot cleanly into the existing Living World Events family; the exact mechanics (detection method, reward table, cooldown/frequency) still need real design work.
Part III — One Governance Idea Worth Keeping Regardless
The ‘Reintegration Candidate’ status tag
Genuinely useful idea from the source proposal, adopted into the Status Tag Legend (Authorities Archive) as its own symbol: 🟣 Reintegration Candidate.
Meaning: a historically superseded, rejected, or banked concept that has become newly viable because the SURROUNDING ARCHITECTURE materially changed since the original call — distinct from simply changing one's mind. Any future use of this tag should name specifically what changed to make the old idea viable again, not just restate the idea.
A Note on the Pattern
This is not the first time this session that a parallel research pass has proposed ‘resurrecting’ something already alive here. The consistent finding across several such cross-checks has been that this archive's own track has simply outpaced the parallel one — not that the parallel pass was doing bad work, just that it wasn't working from current information. Worth keeping in mind for any future parallel research: verify against the actual archive before treating a proposal's framing (‘dead,’ ‘removed,’ ‘shelved’) as fact.
Proposed ‘resurrection’ | Actual current status
Twisted / Distorted Pokémon as the ecological population | Already locked: unhealthy world → Twisted/Distorted populations → restoration → healthy populations. Present across 5 Master Archive parts (2, 3, 4, 10, 16), with Part 3 as the canonical home (13 substantive mentions) and the others appropriately cross-referencing back rather than re-explaining.
Local / Regional restoration instead of one global meter | Already locked as Regional Affinity (Part 9, Section 2): ‘World Affinity has a local expression — meaning Fire can be healthy globally while one specific region stays Fire-depleted.’ Explicitly lets restoration happen place-by-place.
The layered Pokémon anomaly taxonomy (Alpha/Boss/Guardian/Ancient/Apex as one system, not five) | Already fully locked. Titan is a manifestation-class/subfamily of Ecological Apex Pokémon, sitting alongside Territorial, Ancient, Resonance-Saturated, Ecosystem Guardian, and Distorted/Shadow as the same tier. Alpha remains a fully separate, stackable phenotype axis (an ‘Alpha Titan’ is explicitly possible). Status: 🕒 Locked.
Resonance Synthesis outcomes as a spectrum, not a fixed Fusion recipe | Already the core architecture: Stable→Fusion, Rejected→Fractured, Corrupted→Shadow. Present and developed across 5 Master Archive parts (2, 3, 9, 10, 13).
Affinity Essence with a genuinely unique role | Already resolved with its own locked verb — Catalyze/Synthesize — as the catalytic medium for Resonance Synthesis. 9 substantive mentions in Part 3 alone. Not provisional or on probation; RESOLVED status.
The old modular material philosophy (each family owns a distinct verb) | Already locked as a standing rule (Part 3): Fragment=Gather, Liquid=Manipulate/Move, Gem=Condense, Plate=Control, Memory=Understand, Terra Shard=Anchor, Drive=Operate, Z-Crystal=Overdrive/Amplify, Heart=Attune. New material families are required to justify a distinct verb before being added.
Legendary Recognition (eligibility ≠ automatic appearance) | Already extensively locked across 5 parts (1, 2, 3, 10, 11): Affinity→Virtue→Resonance→Recognition→Domain Authority, with Domain requirements meant to demonstrate genuine understanding rather than a checklist.
Pokémon ecological roles (populations actively shape the world, not just respond to it) | Already built out directly (Part 12): ‘Pokémon shouldn't be passengers in this system — they actively contribute abstracted ecological roles,’ with concrete examples resolving an item previously flagged unresolved in Part 10.
Reintroduction (capture → research → care → relocate → release) | Already present across 3 parts (Part 3, 10, 12) as part of the Research/Restoration loop.
Paths as emergent identity, not fixed classes (Ranger/Researcher/Trainer/etc.) | Already present across 3 parts (Part 6, 10, 11) — identity emerging from action rather than an upfront class pick.
Contests vs. Rallies as real Living World activities, not side minigames | Already locked in Part 10 with the exact distinction intact: Contest = ‘who accomplishes X best,’ Rally = ‘everyone gathers and participates.’
The ‘no universally best Poké Ball’ philosophy / modular Resonance Ball technology | Already extensively developed (Parts 5, 6, 8): the full Modular Ball → Attunement Core → Attuned/Affinity Ball hierarchy, with situational specialization as an explicit design principle, not an afterthought.
The 19th Observatory slot as a protected mystery | Already established exactly as proposed — deliberately unexplained, protected as a long-term/Act II hook, present in Part 3.
Resonance Core / Attunement Core hierarchy simplification | Already fully resolved with documented naming history (Resonance Core → Attunement Core, made specifically to stop colliding with ‘Resonance Ball’) and a clean role split: Core = capture strength, Attunement Core = type/Affinity specialization, Treatment = special function.
Resonance Heart's dual meaning (material vs. player progression) needing separation | Already actively being resolved — not an unaddressed collision. Went through two rename iterations specifically because of this exact naming collision: Resonance Heart → Resonance Core → Resonance Nexus (material side), keeping ‘Resonance Hearts’ exclusively for the player progression system. Only the very final name choice remains open for confirmation.
Native Cobblemon Type Gems making a separate ER gem system unnecessary | Already confirmed and adopted — Part 16 explicitly locks in using native Type Gems as the substrate, no duplicate ER gem system exists.

# SOURCE: Change_Log_Archive.docx
---

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
C.E.R. Change Log Archive
The Development History of Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Established as a new, permanent archive category, Sept 2026, under Free Reign Mode, at Wesley's explicit direction. Compiled by Claude. This is a living document — updated every time a meaningful change is made anywhere in the project, indefinitely.
Where the Master Archive parts (1–14), Authorities, Interaction, and Supplemental answer “What IS the project?”, this archive answers “How did the project BECOME what it is?” Every entry here is cross-referenced back to the Master Archive part and section it affects, and vice versa where practical.
0. Archive Purpose & Rules
What qualifies as a change
Any of the following, however small, qualifies for an entry: a system added, removed, renamed, merged, split, reclassified, or moved between archives; a mod added, removed, or replaced; a contradiction discovered or resolved; an idea promoted from proposal to locked, or demoted from locked/candidate to rejected/superseded; a status upgrade or downgrade; a terminology correction. A change does not have to be “big” to qualify — it has to be meaningful, per the same standard F.R.M. already uses elsewhere (Authorities Archive).
The entry template
Every Change Log Record (CLR) uses the same shape, kept short and free of filler: What changed — Previous state — New state — Reason — Gap/Impact (only if removal/replacement) — Cross-references — Current Status. Not every field is used in every entry (a pure ADD, for instance, has no “previous state”) — omit what doesn't apply rather than padding it.
Category tags
Reason classification
Design (better gameplay/thematic fit/clarity/progression), Technical (mod/loader/version incompatibility, implementation limitation), Architectural (duplicate functionality, taxonomy conflict, missing distinction, system overlap), Project Direction (scope/theme change, system superseded, new foundational principle). Each CLR names which applies, so the archive can eventually answer “did we remove this because we disliked it, or because it duplicated something else?”
Navigation note (hyperlinks, honestly)
Wesley's request for hyperlink-style navigation (“removal → the formerly relevant information → and back”) is fully intended, but true clickable cross-document hyperlinks aren't practical across 14+ separate Word files edited independently — links would break every time a referenced document is regenerated. Textual cross-references (“→ Part 2, Section 5”, “→ CLR-0004”) serve the same navigational purpose today and convert cleanly into real clickable hyperlinks once Wesley's planned single consolidated Master Archive (Section 12 below) exists as one linked document — that's the natural point to add real hyperlink infrastructure, not before.
Status vocabulary
Reuses the Authorities Archive's existing status tags throughout (🕒 Locked, 🟢 Strong reconstruction, 🔵 Current proposal, 🟡 Candidate, ⚪ Historical/superseded, ❓ Needs verification, 🟠 Unresolved) rather than inventing a parallel vocabulary.
1. Project Genesis
Earliest known naming history (Part 1): Cobblemon — The Living World; Cobblemon: Resonance of the Living World; Cobblemon: Reawakening; Resonance; Living World; Project Riispawn (internal codename) → settled on Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance (C.E.R./E.R.), with the ER-as-“Emergency Room” thematic coincidence embraced rather than avoided.
Documented origin of the reconstruction project itself, worth preserving here specifically since it's the reason this whole archive set exists: an accidental “retry” click mid-conversation with ChatGPT erased roughly half of the established project history. The archive reconstruction effort (Parts 1–14, Authorities, Interaction, Supplemental, and now this Change Log) exists specifically to make sure that never costs the project anything again.
2. Version Ledger
Honesty note: the project never had formal numbered software releases, so this ledger does not manufacture version numbers that didn't exist. Development Stages are used instead until Wesley establishes an actual versioning scheme.
3. Change Records
Integration Sweep — Sept 2026 (post Drive/Apricorn/Audit batch)
Requested by Wesley: check that everything added recently still works together, without running a Modification Sweep — he hasn't finished reviewing the archive himself yet and doesn't want anything unreviewed altered or removed. This sweep is a pure audit: findings are recorded below; nothing was removed, renamed, or restructured except three small additive cross-references (noted as fixed), which add pointers without changing any existing content's meaning.
Finding 1 (real, unresolved): Genesect Drives and Resonance Drives now share all four elements
Genesect Drives (Past: Douse/Shock/Burn/Chill = Water/Electric/Fire/Ice) and Resonance Drives (Present: Thermal/Hydraulic/Cryogenic/Conduction = Fire/Water/Ice/Electric) cover the exact same four elements under two different names. Part 6's move-examples table (Fire/Water/Electric/Ice/Psychic moves) doesn't specify which tier's Drive a given move actually feeds, which is now genuinely ambiguous rather than just underspecified. This connects directly to the already-open “what determines a Drive's Affinity” question (Part 6, Models 1/2/3) — whichever model gets chosen will likely resolve this overlap as a side effect, so the two questions should be decided together rather than separately. Status: 🟠 Unresolved — flagged, not fixed, per the no-Modification-Sweep boundary.
Finding 2 (real, unresolved): possible Assimilation extension for Saturation-based fruit/vegetable transformations
White Apricorn→colored Apricorn, fruit→Berry, and Potato→Poisonous Potato are all currently Affinity Adaptation outcomes of Saturation (Part 6/9). None of them currently have an Assimilation-tier version (the Affinity becoming permanently incorporated into the plant's genetic line, e.g. via repeated cultivation or breeding, rather than being present only in the current specimen). This is a plausible, not-yet-decided extension — flagged as a design opportunity, not a gap that needs filling.
Two safe additive fixes applied (cross-references only, nothing altered)
Part 9: added a note pointing Resonance Sites' definition to Part 16's new Resonance Site Classes proposal, since Part 9 previously had no sub-categories for Resonance Sites despite this archive's own earlier caution against treating every Legendary site identically.
Part 2: added a note clarifying that Assimilation (a Stability factor, already locked) and the Fusion compatibility examples (Affinity Relationships) are additive, separate factors — not competing explanations for why a given Fusion is stable.
Checked and found already correctly connected (no fix needed): Part 15's Eevee starter entry already cross-references Part 14's Eevee Conundrum — initially suspected as a missing link, verified present on inspection.
Redundancy check
Distillation Drive (Poison, industrial material purification) and Ars Elixurum (potion research, magical) were checked against each other given both touch “purification” — confirmed complementary, not redundant: one is a Resonance Machine material process, the other is a separate mod's magical potion system. Worth remembering they're distinct so they don't get accidentally merged later.
Gameplay loop and complexity checks
The core Explore → Discover → Interact → Generate Resonance → Restore → Unlock → Explore loop remains intact and unbroken by everything added this session. No violations of the Justified Complexity Rule found — the Drive restructure, the Apricorn/Berry/Potato Saturation additions, the Virtue Node structure decision, and the Cobblemon/Pixelmon audit findings all tie into existing systems rather than creating new parallel ones.
Numbered sequentially as CLR-0001 onward. This is a first population covering the most significant changes made during the archive-reconstruction effort — not a complete record of every micro-edit. Earlier F.R.M. actions recorded informally in the Authorities Archive's own change log are folded in here as the first three entries.

CLR-0031 — Resonance Drives Expanded 4→5, Inductive Drive Added, Affinity Tags Assigned
Tag: MODIFY
Previous state: 4 Drives (Thermal, Hydraulic, Cryogenic, Compression), justified by mirroring Genesect's 4 Drives; no Drive covered power generation; no Drive had an assigned represented Affinity
New state: 5 Drives — Inductive Drive added specifically for power generation, paired with Electric Affinity via electromagnetic induction (the real physical process generators use, matching the existing physical-process naming pattern). Each Drive now carries an explicit represented-Affinity tag: Thermal=Fire, Hydraulic=Water, Cryogenic=Ice, Compression=Normal, Inductive=Electric. This is a represented-Affinity tag, not a new naming scheme — the Drive names themselves are unchanged
Reason: Design — Wesley identified a genuine functional gap (nothing generated power) and requested a fix using the existing naming convention
Gap/Impact: None negative — the “stays at 4” framing is revised to “5, expanded deliberately for a specific reason,” not a drift back toward 18
Cross-references: → Part 6, Section 3; → Part 3, Section 1
Current Status: 🕒 Locked

CLR-0033 — Drive System Restructured into Genesis Drives + Resonance Drives (4+4)
Tag: SPLIT
Previous state: A single flat set of 5 Drives (Thermal, Hydraulic, Cryogenic, Compression, Inductive), with Compression's Normal-Affinity pairing not actually matching Genesect's Fire/Water/Ice/Electric theme
New state: Two lore-connected families of 4: Genesis Drives (Compression/Normal, Distillation/Poison, Harmonic/Psychic, Transmutation/Dragon — natural, ancestral, came first) and Resonance Drives (Thermal/Fire, Hydraulic/Water, Cryogenic/Ice, Electric-name-pending — man-made, built by studying the Genesis Drives, faithfully echoing Genesect's real 4 drives)
Reason: Design — Wesley's direct restructure, explicitly tying the two-tier origin story to Mew's own role as the DNA/memory ancestor of all Pokémon (Part 2's Genesis system)
Gap/Impact: None negative — resolves the Compression/Genesect-theme mismatch as a side effect, and gives Electric a proper home instead of needing to be squeezed into the original flat 5
Cross-references: → Part 6, Section 5a; → Part 3, Section 1; → Interaction Archive
Current Status: 🕒 Locked in full — the Electric Drive naming question is now resolved (see CLR-0035)

Tag: ADD
What changed: A real, previously-missing correction recovered from source material: Trainer/Personal Affinity is permanent once earned, full stop. Clarified separately, and explicitly, that this is distinct from World Affinity (Part 1, Section on generation), which DOES decay slowly by default until a late-game Affinity Stabilizer locks it \u2014 the two systems were never contradictory, just easy to conflate since both live in Part 1
Reason: Correction \u2014 Wesley's direct memory check, verified against original source material rather than assumed correct or incorrect
Gap/Impact: Real gap closed: this permanence rule existed in the source all along but had never made it into the archive. The World Affinity decay text (already accurate) was also clarified in-place to remove the ambiguity that caused this question in the first place

CLR-0055 — Two-Phase World Affinity Decay Model — Unifies World & Trainer Affinity Logic
Tag: PROMOTION
Previous state: World Affinity decays continuously (~1/day) with the Stabilizer only stopping future decay — read as a single ongoing rule with one exit point
New state: Locked as a genuine two-phase model: Phase 1 (pre-Stabilizer) — the original ~1/day decay applies. Phase 2 (post-Stabilizer activation) — the region's Affinity becomes locked/permanent exactly like Trainer Affinity, and a Resonance-equivalent layer for that region takes over as what can still fluctuate. World and Trainer Affinity now share one underlying logic (permanent core + fluctuating Resonance layer), differing only in whether permanence is default (Trainer) or earned (World, via the Stabilizer)
Reason: Design — Wesley's direct refinement, proposed immediately after the Sundering/Stabilizer-destruction lore (CLR-0054), strengthening that connection further: the Sundering specifically forced every region from Phase 2 back into Phase 1
Cross-references: → Part 1, Two-phase decay model section; → CLR-0052 (Trainer Affinity permanence); → CLR-0054 (the Sundering)
Current Status: 🕒 Locked — this is now the governing model, superseding the flat single-phase decay reading

CLR-0066 — Enhanced Research Branch — New Proposal, Building on Affinity Relationships
Tag: ADD
What changed: New proposed mechanic: deep research in one Affinity can unlock an enhanced research branch into a thematically-related Affinity, using the already-locked Affinity Relationships table (Part 9, Section 3) to determine eligible pairs rather than inventing new criteria. The branch Affinity stays fully standalone — this is an additional unlock path, not a gate or replacement
Contradiction flagged, not resolved: Wesley's own worked example (Fire cannot branch into Grass) conflicts with Fire+Grass being a documented Affinity Relationship. Suggests two different kinds of relationship exist in that table — ecological interaction (Fire+Grass, two forces affecting each other) vs. subset/specialized-form (Water→Ice, ice literally IS water) — and only the second kind may qualify as a valid research branch. Flagged explicitly for Wesley's call, not decided unilaterally
Reason: Design — Wesley's new proposal, genuinely builds on existing locked content rather than needing new infrastructure
Cross-references: → Part 9, Section 3 (Affinity Relationships)
Current Status: 🔵 Current proposal for the core mechanic; 🟠 Unresolved for which specific pairs qualify
CLR-0067 — Enhanced Research Branch — Causal Model Corrected
Tag: MODIFY
Previous state: CLR-0066 assumed Research Branches were derived from/gated by the existing Affinity Relationships table, and flagged a contradiction (Fire+Grass being a documented Relationship despite Wesley excluding Fire→Grass branching)
New state: Corrected: the causal direction runs the other way. Deep research into Affinity A unlocks discovering a logically-SPECIALIZED Affinity B in the world (Water→Ice: ice IS a form of water) — that discovery is what often leads to recognizing a new Affinity Relationship afterward, not the reverse. The two systems are independent: Relationships are broad/inclusive (any logical ecological interaction qualifies, e.g. Fire+Grass), Branches are narrower (require genuine subset/specialization logic). A Relationship existing doesn't require a Branch, and a Branch doesn't require a pre-documented Relationship — this resolves the earlier flagged contradiction, which turned out to be a wrong assumption about causality, not a genuine conflict in the underlying idea
Reason: Correction — Wesley's direct clarification of the actual causal/temporal order
Cross-references: → Part 9, Enhanced Research Branch section; → CLR-0066
Current Status: 🕒 Locked for the corrected causal model; 🟠 Unresolved for exact Branch-eligibility criteria beyond the Water→Ice example
