Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Master Archive — Part 10: Hearthhome, Player Paths & Remaining New Material
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, under Free Reign Mode. Source: a 133-section informal project-wide dump Wesley recovered, explicitly NOT a formal canonicalization pass. The large majority of it confirms Parts 1–9 with no contradictions found — a good consistency check on its own. This part covers only what's genuinely new: the starting settlement, technical/dev process, story structure, Player Paths, an expanded Pokémon taxonomy, and several concrete technical findings. Two small corrections were also made elsewhere — see the end of this document.
1. Technical Baseline & Development Philosophy
Concrete baseline: Minecraft 1.21.1, NeoForge (21.1.250 discussed as the relevant version), Cobblemon 1.8.0, KubeJS, custom scripted/datapack infrastructure, Prism Launcher as the initial build environment.
A 10-step development sequence was established, worth preserving as actual process guidance rather than just lore: establish Minecraft/NeoForge → establish Cobblemon → make a clean working world → verify the baseline → back it up → add major systems incrementally → test after meaningful groups of changes → keep content/QoL/worldgen/performance/core-systems concerns separate → test world generation before committing heavily to worldgen-dependent mechanics → only then build the large custom Restoration Engine. Rationale given directly: with a system this interconnected, when something breaks you need to know whether it was Cobblemon, worldgen, KubeJS, Create, quests, spawning, an addon, or your own code — the incremental order exists to keep that diagnosable. Status: 🕒 Locked — explicit process guidance, not a design brainstorm.
2. Hearthhome — the Starting Settlement
A significant piece of worldbuilding not previously in any archive part: Hearthhome, a tiny settlement that is not destroyed — abandoned. Contains houses, a bakery, a workshop, a small library, a railway station, a shrine, and a Pokémon research building, all suggesting the people who lived there intended to return and never did. The player's first home is already livable (bed, fireplace, garden, storage, small workshop) and gets gradually renovated over the course of play rather than built from nothing. Emotional arc: “This is home” → “Why was everyone gone?” Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
The Restoration Observatory's 19th position
Confirms and extends Part 1's Restoration Observatory (circular ruin, 18 sockets, empty pedestal). New detail: there is a deliberately unexplained 19th Observatory position, beyond the 18 that map to the Affinities. Explicitly meant to stay a mystery — potential hook for Act II, a post-restoration mystery, or something beyond the known 18. Direct instruction preserved: do not prematurely explain it.
3. The Story Chapter Outline (historical, not locked)
An old chapter-outline structure for the central mystery, explicitly marked historical rather than current canon, but worth preserving since it's the clearest surviving shape of the intended mystery pacing: early — “Something is missing.” Chapter ~5 — “Something happened.” Chapter ~10 — “Someone caused it.” Chapter ~14 — “Someone sacrificed the Pokémon.” Chapter ~17 — “The Pokémon were part of something.” Chapter 18 — “Oh.” The broader emotional escalation this sits inside: “Something is missing” → “Why is this place like this?” → “Something happened” → “Someone caused it” → “The Pokémon were involved” → “They were protecting something” → “From what?” Status: ⚪ Historical — a real shape worth keeping as reference, not something to build against as if locked.
On showing numbers vs. showing the world
An explicit design pivot, worth flagging since it affects how Part 1's numeric tables should be read: an early version of World Affinity feedback used literal numeric HUD readouts (“Fire Resonance 82%, Heat generated 642/1000, Volcanic activity 2/3, Infernal exposure 71/100”). The later, preferred direction leans away from showing all the numbers, toward narrative text at milestones instead — e.g. at 25% “stones feel warm,” 50% “something moves beneath the ash,” 75% “distant roar,” 100% “FIRE REMEMBERS,” then Fire Pokémon begin returning. Part 1's numeric World Affinity table should be read as the underlying data model, not necessarily what the player sees directly — the intended player-facing presentation is closer to this narrative-text approach.
4. The Layered Pokémon Taxonomy
A cleaner, more formal version of the “special categories” discussion scattered across Parts 1–2. RESOLVED, Sept 2026: Wesley reconciled the two competing sweep models directly — this table supersedes both the original 7-layer draft and the parallel sweep's 5-layer version below.
Wesley's own reasoning, worth preserving directly: Fractured and Shadow are two sides of the same coin, not different kinds of thing — Fractured is essentially what Shadow would be without corruption present. A Synthesis attempt “fractures” because the connection broke and the Resonance had a backlash — like an open wound. If corruption is present during that backlash, it affects how the wound resolves — like the wound becoming infected and turning into something bigger to handle. That's Shadow. Fusion is the light to Shadow's dark: they need each other to exist as concepts, but a single Pokémon shouldn't normally be able to hold both statuses at once — which is exactly what makes Corrupted Fusion rare and special when it does happen, as a genuine rule violation rather than just “extra corrupted.”
Theoretical combinations can exist across layers, but not every combination should — and critically, special does not mean stronger: Alpha, Shadow, Twisted, Fusion, and Fractured are all explicitly NOT automatically the best version of a Pokémon. Every category needs its own reason to exist rather than functioning as a de facto power tier. Status: 🕒 Locked — directly resolved by Wesley, superseding the earlier unresolved flag.
‘Fake’ / ‘Fakemon’ — clarified, same term
Resolved: “Fake” and “Fakemon” are the same thing, not two categories — ChatGPT's sweep used “Fake” as shorthand. “Fakemon” is being used the way the wider Pokémon fan community already uses it: an umbrella term for any non-official Pokémon, i.e. anything not created by Nintendo/Game Freak. Applied to C.E.R.: technically, Shadow, Fusion, Fractured, Twisted, and every other custom state would all qualify as “Fakemon” by that outside definition, since none of them are official Nintendo content. In-universe, though, none of them carry that label — the story gives each one its own ecological, thematic, and lore-grounded identity instead, and “Fakemon” is reserved specifically for newly-adapted species discovered via Resonance saturation (the Ecological State/Distorted entry above), which don't have one of those other, more specific categories to belong to.
Post-Fusion degradation
Fusion failure isn't the only path to Shadow/Fractured — a Fusion that succeeded can later degrade. A successful Fusion exposed to corruption afterward becomes a Corrupted Fusion; a successful Fusion that later becomes unstable becomes a Tainted Fusion. Distinct from the synthesis-time failure branches in the Resonance Stability layer above — this is post-hoc decay of an already-successful result, not a different outcome of the original synthesis attempt. Rarity tiers now locked (Part 2): Tainted Fusion is a rare Aberration, Corrupted Fusion an extremely rare Aberration — renamed from Fractured Fusion/Shadow Fusion, repurposing terminology previously marked historical (Part 13). Status: 🕒 Locked.
5. Resonance Tags (distinct from the 18 Affinities)
A separate vocabulary describing what a Resonance Opportunity or phenomenon relates to — not Affinity categories: TIME, NATURE, WATER, LIFE, DEATH, SPACE, GENETICS, FIRE, KNOWLEDGE, DREAMS, DARKNESS, CREATION, DISTORTION. Useful for tagging opportunities/events by theme without overloading the 18-Affinity system with concepts that don't map cleanly onto a Pokémon type.
Seeking Resonance / the Resonance Window
A new mechanic tied to the Restoration Observatory: the player can actively “Seek Resonance” or “Listen,” opening a Resonance Window (or Stirring). This doesn't summon a Legendary — it signals “I'm listening” and lets the world respond on its own terms. Recovered flavor-text examples for the moment before a Legendary's identity is revealed: “Something stirred in the distance,” “For a moment, you feel as though you are being watched,” “Something has answered your call” — explicitly not an immediate “DIALGA HAS APPEARED!”-style reveal; atmosphere comes first.
6. Player Paths of Resonance
A significantly fuller version of the brief mention in Part 1 (“Ranger, Researcher, Trainer established; Artisan and Warden proposed”). Explicitly not rigid RPG classes — a player can pursue several paths at once, expressed through play rather than chosen at creation:
A maturity/reputation progression per path was also recovered: Developing → Established → Mastered → Awakened (exact path→tier mapping not specified). Governing rule: starter choice establishes initial Resonance, actions establish path, accomplishments establish reputation/Virtues, and the world responds — explicitly preferred over selecting a rigid class at character creation. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction for Ranger/Researcher/Trainer; 🟡 Candidate for Artisan/Warden and the maturity-tier system.
7. Factions, Trainers, Contests & Rallies
Technical foundation found (M.M.I.): the previously flagged ‘NPC/faction infrastructure has no mod backbone’ gap is substantially resolved — Cobblemon Trainer Battle Commands handles trainer battles, and Easy NPC provides real dialog/models plus an actual faction system (per-NPC faction assignment, scoreboard team sync, hostile-faction targeting) with Cobblemon-specific NPC model variants already supported. TBCS+Easy NPC is a strong COMPLEMENT for building original faction NPCs alongside RCT — CORRECTION (Wesley, Sept 2026): an earlier pass incorrectly treated this as a replacement for RCT and marked RCT dropped. That was never approved. RCT is restored to Confirmed — it's foundational for other real addons (Trainer Doubles, and related infrastructure from the same author), and TBCS+Easy NPC sits alongside it as an addition, not a substitute.
M.L.I. proposal (Wesley, Sept 2026): give trainer challenges a physical presence instead of a pure menu flow — rather than click-GUI-fight-battle, teleport the player to an arena and spawn a visible trainer with a randomized team. Two paths were weighed: (A) directly pair with Cobblemon Battle Tower itself, or (B) build a separate custom C.E.R. structure using the same underlying infrastructure, preserving Battle Tower as its own distinct feature rather than cannibalizing it.
RECOMMENDATION: path B. Battle Tower's internals aren't something C.E.R. can directly rewrite (no source-level access without forking it), and a custom structure keeps Battle Tower's own genuinely strong identity intact — its GUI-based floor climb, Battle Points shop, and Boss Mode Gigantamax encounters are worth keeping as their own thing, not merged into something else. Battle Tower's dependency is RCTApi, a separate, independent, lightweight trainer/battle-management library shared by RCT (now confirmed, not dropped) and TBCS alike. That means a custom C.E.R. 'Trial Chamber' or similar structure could use TBCS (spawn/manage the trainer and battle, on the same API Battle Tower itself runs on) + Easy NPC (give that trainer a visible model, dialogue, and faction identity) + a teleport-to-arena trigger + a randomized team pool built from original C.E.R. content, OR could draw on RCT's own roster directly — both are live options now, not one path forced by a mistaken removal.
Status: 🔵 Current proposal, not yet built — genuinely promising given the RCTApi finding, but the actual implementation (arena structure, team-pool data, teleport/spawn scripting) hasn't been designed in detail yet.
Expands Part 8's faction roster: two more names recovered — Aether League and Independent Rangers — alongside the five already documented (Verdant Circle, Iron Accord, Arcane Order, Umbral Covenant, Frontier Guild). Still explicitly examples, not a confirmed final roster.
Faction Trainers split into Allies (can't be battled; trade, offer benefits, assist the player) and Opponents (battle the player; rewards/penalties/faction consequences). Restricted areas can be gated by guards via a Cobblemon battle rather than combat/violence — explicitly preserves the franchise's tone.
Contest vs. Rally, a useful distinction not previously drawn this cleanly: a Contest asks “who can accomplish X best?” (individual competition — candidate types: fishing, catching, performance, exploration, scavenging, species/type challenges, timed challenges, bike races); a Rally asks people to gather and participate together (community/ecological events — restoration festivals, migrations, seasonal events, Affinity celebrations, communal projects).
Catching-Contest prize proposal: Candied Berry (Wesley, Sept 2026)
A concrete worked example for the already-listed “catching” Contest type: CobbleCuisine's Candied Berry (a real, active Cobblemon addon mod) as a prize — a food item that temporarily boosts catch rate. Proposed C.E.R. reframing rather than adopting the mod's mechanic as an unexplained flat buff: a Candied Berry is a Berry that has undergone further Affinity Saturation beyond the ordinary Grass+Saturation→Berry pathway already locked (Part 6) — resonant enough that it calms or draws a wild Pokémon of a compatible signature toward the player, rather than simply being an arbitrary catch-percentage modifier. Gives the item real in-world logic instead of being a disconnected vanilla-style consumable. Status: 🕒 Locked — the Saturation reframe confirmed directly by Wesley.
Safari Zone — flagged as a genuine open concept, not yet designed
Raised alongside the Candied Berry discussion as a possible additional home for catch-rate-adjacent mechanics; checked directly against the archive and confirmed there is currently NO existing Safari Zone concept anywhere in C.E.R. — unlike Contests (already established above), this would be new territory. Worth a dedicated design pass if pursued, rather than assuming it slots into something that already exists.
World-Level Scaling / Level Cap — RESOLVED into three distinct systems
A dedicated recovery pass confirmed the gap flagged above was correct — nothing on this existed as its own system — but also produced the resolution: ER's own historical material was already heading toward THREE separate caps/thresholds that should NOT be collapsed into one mechanic. Verified against the archive: the underlying Hearts+Badges obedience system was already locked in Part 2, Section 1 (independent confirmation, not new); the spawn-weight formula was already locked in Part 1 (independent confirmation, not new); what was genuinely missing was the organizing framework tying them together, plus a real technical mod candidate for the wild-scaling piece specifically.
The core philosophical throughline, worth preserving directly: the player gradually regains the ability to command increasingly powerful Pokémon (Cap A), while the restored world gradually becomes capable of PRODUCING increasingly powerful Pokémon (Cap B) — two different progression curves, not one. Wild Pokémon are creatures of the world's own restoration state, not mirrors of the player's level. Status: 🕒 Locked for the three-cap distinction and the philosophical throughline (both consistent with and clarifying already-locked material); 🟡 Candidate for Cap B's exact technical wiring to Cobbled Levels and for any specific Heart→level numeric table, which remains illustrative rather than fixed.
8. Pokémon Hunts
A structured system: Research question → Hunt → Exploration → Observation → Capture → Research → Affinity/Resonance → New research (a loop, not a one-off). Potential hunt targets: species, nature, ability, gender, form, Alpha, shiny, Affinity, habitat, biome, weather, time, ecological state, behavioral condition, anomaly/state, temporal variant. Explicit guardrail: don't turn this into an exhaustive database checklist — a hunt should exist because the world currently needs that Pokémon/information, not because a completionist list demands it.
Fusion (and, by the same logic, Fake Pokémon) shouldn't normally be a hunt target if it doesn't occur naturally — instead the player pursues a Research Investigation (e.g. “Synthetic Resonance”) that builds through evidence → research → experiment → discovery, making the eventual Fusion feel earned rather than found.
9. Progression Ladder & Automation Philosophy
Explicitly broad historical concepts, not locked balance values. Automation philosophy, stated as a clean rule: automation should represent mastery, not a shortcut available from the start — Early = manual, Mid = stabilization, Late = automation, keeping the player actively involved with the ecosystem rather than automating engagement away early.
10. Concrete Technical Findings
Catch Indicator is confirmed incompatible with Cobblemon 1.8.0. The preferred alternative discussed is Catch Rate Display 2.1.1. Worth folding into the mod list next time Part 4/8's tables are touched.
Loader Policy: mod candidates should eventually be organized by loader (NeoForge/Fabric/etc.); cross-loader infrastructure like Forgified Fabric is optional compatibility infrastructure, not an assumed part of the pack.
A full Cobblemon Feature Audit was proposed but explicitly has NOT been performed yet — examining every significant Cobblemon version update for what could be repurposed for C.E.R. (not just what Cobblemon uses it for natively). Flagged as a real planned task, not done.
Additional mod-list candidates surfaced here, not previously listed: FTB Quests (quest framework candidate alongside/instead of Cobblemon Quests), Terralith and Nature's Spirit (worldgen), Towns & Towers and YUNG's Better Dungeons (structures), possibly Alex's Caves, Sophisticated Backpacks and JEI/EMI (QoL/utility), Cobblemon Hunt, Cobblemon Picnic, and Occupied Pokéballs REFORGED (the specific mod behind Part 8's Occupied Pokéball system) — all still needing compatibility validation against the actual 1.21.1/Cobblemon 1.8 target.
11. Reintroduction, Ecological Roles & Dynamic Population
Reintroduction and the Capture→Release→Reintroduction chain: now fully confirmed, see Part 12 Section 7. Ecological Roles: now has concrete species examples (Combee/Vespiquen→pollination, Diglett→soil disturbance, and others) and an explicit abstracted-contribution implementation approach — see Part 12 Section 3, which resolves this from “unresolved/future” to 🟢 Strong reconstruction. Dynamic Population specifically (the spawn system tracking actual population/habitat/role together, not just yes/no eligibility) remains 🟠 Unresolved/future — the general direction is supported by Part 12's Ecological State system, but no dedicated population-tracking mechanic has been designed yet.
12. Traverser Mark (non-canon, future-sequel idea)
A Resonance Mark (per Part 9) proposed for a hypothetical future “Home” system letting a Pokémon transfer between separate C.E.R. worlds — a Pokémon that crossed worlds would carry a Traverser Mark. Explicitly flagged as not current implementation canon — a fun idea kept on file for a potential sequel/second iteration, not something to build toward now.
13. Closing Framing (worth preserving as-is)
The recovery ends on a genuinely useful piece of project self-assessment: C.E.R. has stopped being a collection of individual mechanics and become an architecture — World → Ecology → Affinity → Resonance → Pokémon → Research → Materials → Creation → Magic → Technology → Factions → Restoration → Legendary Recognition → Domains → History → Memory → back into the World. The stated open problem going forward isn't “what other systems should we invent,” it's “how do all these systems communicate with one another without becoming clutter” — i.e. the work now is defining interfaces between already-designed systems, not adding new ones. Worth keeping in mind for future sessions as a genuine project-management signal, not just lore.
Final one-sentence formulations worth preserving verbatim: “You arrive in a world that has forgotten how to live, learn to resonate with its 18 elemental forces, restore its Pokémon and ecosystems, and eventually teach the world to remember itself.” And shorter: Restore the Resonance. Restore the Ecosystem. Restore the World.
14. Alpha Pokémon — Native vs. External Mod, Resolved
A cross-sweep comparison (a parallel ChatGPT full-sweep pass Wesley ran alongside this one) flagged a real contradiction: the project record at one point states Alpha Pokémon come from the external Pokémon Alpha mod, while a separate modernization discussion recommends using Cobblemon's native Alpha mechanics instead once available, without the two ever being explicitly reconciled.
Resolved directly by Wesley: Alpha Pokémon functionality was originally a standalone mod, and has since been integrated into Cobblemon's base game as of version 1.8.0. There is no remaining contradiction — any earlier reference to a separate Alpha mod describes the pre-1.8.0 state of affairs; the current and correct direction is native Cobblemon 1.8.0 Alpha functionality, with no external dependency needed. Status: 🕒 Locked.
Corrections made to other archives from this pass
Authorities Archive: added the Editorial Integrity rule (fix spelling/grammar/formatting/transcription errors freely; never silently change meaning) as an explicit governing rule for future cleanup passes.
Master Archive Part 5: added a caveat that code appearing in the JavaScript example document is evidence of technical exploration, not proof anything was actually implemented or working — applies retroactively to how that part's content should be read.
Layer | Question | What lives here
Species | What Pokémon is it? | Base identity (Pikachu, Lucario...)
Form | What form does it have? | Regional; Sculk-Touched/“Wardenized” (Part 4)
Phenotype | How is the individual physically expressed? | Alpha
Ecological State (“Distorted”) | What has environmental/Resonance pressure done to what this Pokémon fundamentally is? | Twisted (the flagship, fully-developed case, Part 3); newly-discovered Fakemon via Resonance adaptation (a second Distorted outcome, same mechanism)
Resonance Stability | Did a Synthesis attempt hold together, and how? | Stable → Fusion. Unstable, no corruption → Fractured. Unstable, corruption present → Shadow. Mutually exclusive under normal circumstances — one Pokémon is Fusion OR Fractured OR Shadow, never combined.
Anomaly/Aberration | Has something happened that shouldn't normally be possible? | Corrupted Fusion — not a normal Resonance Stability outcome, but the rare violation of mutual exclusivity above (renamed from Shadow Fusion, see Part 2)
Path | Guiding question | Focus
Ranger | “What does this place need?” | Exploration, ecology, restoration, habitat, fieldwork
Researcher | “What is happening here, and why?” | Research, photography, Resonance, Affinity, anomalies, documentation
Trainer | “How far can we go together?” | Pokémon training, battles, Gyms, Battle Towers, tournaments, mastery
Artisan (proposed) | “What can I create to help the world?” | Create, machinery, infrastructure, Resonance equipment, automation
Warden (proposed) | “What is the world asking of me?” | Advanced Resonance, Nature's Aura, Resonance Hearts, Domains, Virtues
Cap type | Question it answers | Driven by
A. Obedience threshold | Can I reliably command this Pokémon? | Hearts + Badges/Trials + how it was obtained (already locked, Part 2, Section 1)
B. Wild level-generation range | What level can this wild Pokémon naturally spawn at? | Region + World Affinity/Restoration state + ecological stage (Part 1) — NOT the player's own level. Cobbled Levels (M.M.I.) is a strong technical candidate: its Region/Biome/Dimension config gives a real hook for World Affinity to drive spawn-level ranges, with Legendaries excluded entirely via its exclusion list
C. Challenge battle cap | Am I allowed into this specific sanctioned battle at this level? | Gym/League/faction/challenge-specific rules — already partially covered by Plushlocke's Hard/Iron tier level caps (Part 2). Radical Cobblemon Trainers (M.M.I.) verified as a real, working mod implementing exactly this: level cap only rises by defeating specific key trainers in order, over-cap Pokémon can't gain EXP, trainers refuse over-cap challengers. The CAP MECHANISM is a strong candidate; the mod's 1,500+ imported trainers (from Radical Red/Unbound/BDSP) are explicitly NOT to be used as-is, per the reference≠reuse principle (Authorities Archive) — C.E.R. would populate its own progression with original trainers/NPCs. Cobblemon: Fierce Competition (M.M.I.) found as an even stronger candidate for this specific role — same level-cap-by-defeating-key-trainers shape, but built from 5 custom gym leaders and 19 ORIGINAL trainer classes rather than imported ROM-hack rosters, sidestepping the reference≠reuse concern almost entirely
Stage | Vanilla-tier anchor | Focus
Early | Wood → Stone | Basic Cobblemon, basic Create, basic Ars
First restoration | Iron | Create machinery, first major region
Midgame | Diamond | Advanced magic, the Nether, industrial infrastructure
Late midgame | (post-Diamond) | Create factories, advanced Ars/Occultism, dangerous ruins
Endgame | (post-Nether-tier) | Ancient technology, magical infrastructure, the Restoration Engine itself
Post-18 | (all Affinities restored) | An entirely new progression tree

# SOURCE: Master_Archive_Part11.docx
---

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Master Archive — Part 11: Skill Tree, Paradox Pokémon & Identity Systems
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, under Free Reign Mode. Integrates the two full-sweep passes (Claude's own + a parallel ChatGPT sweep Wesley ran alongside it) per Wesley's direction to use discretion in combining them. Covers the full Skill Tree specialization system, Paradox Pokémon philosophy, and refinements to ranks/titles/marks from both sweeps.
1. The Skill Tree System
A full specialization system tied to the Cobblemon Skill Tree mod, deliberately separated from Affinity: Affinity determines what you resonate with; Skill determines how you've learned to work with it. This substantially deepens Part 10's Player Paths of Resonance with real mechanical structure — the two should be read together, not as competing systems (see Section 1.3).
1.1 The Branches
Battler sub-branches: Trainer (obedience/command efficiency), Tactician (battle information and tactical tools), Breeder (eggs/breeding/inheritance), Specialist (synergy with Personal Affinity), Champion (advanced battle mechanics).
1.2 The Occultist tradeoff
Worth its own note since it's a genuinely different design shape from the other branches: an Occultist gains better spirit interactions, access to dangerous rituals, alternate resource generation, unique information, and unusual Pokémon encounters — but potentially increases Anomaly risk. Explicitly not framed as “Occultism skill = bad,” but as “you are deliberately studying things the world was trying to forget” — a real cost attached to real power, not a trap option.
1.3 Governing Rules
Skill points should be scarce, earned through demonstrated mastery (e.g. Researcher mastery from documenting species/photographing behavior/discovering anomalies — not kill count or grinding).
Skills should unlock verbs (“You can identify migration patterns”), never percentages (“+3% XP”).
Skill Capacity mirrors Personal Affinity Capacity directly: 3 active specialties early → 5 mid → 7 late → 9 endgame. Unused skills aren't erased, just inactive (“Active Masteries”).
Skill × Affinity cross-specialization: Fire Specialist + Engineer → “Thermal Resonance” (heat-based Create machinery interacts with Fire Affinity); Water Specialist + Ranger → “Hydrological Restoration” (aqueducts/irrigation contribute to Water/Grass ecological recovery); Fairy Specialist + Arcanist → “Natural Spellcraft” (Ars Nouveau spells cooperate with Nature's Aura). Formula given directly: Skill × Affinity = specialization, not another independent progression system.
Player identity becomes earned titles rather than levels — “Field Researcher: You have documented 127 species and uncovered 14 ecological anomalies” instead of “Level 37.” Multiple titles are possible simultaneously (“The Ranger,” “The Engineer,” “The Occult Scholar”) without hard-locking anyone into one class — good fit for a multiplayer server specifically.
Full character-progression stack this implies: World Affinity (what the world can support) → Personal Affinity (what resonates with you) → Skills (what you've mastered) → Hearts (how much of yourself you've restored) → Virtues (who you've become) → Domain Authority (what's been entrusted to you). Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction for the branch concepts and governing rules; 🟡 Candidate for exact node names/thresholds throughout, which read as illustrative.
2. Paradox Pokémon
A dedicated encounter philosophy, explicitly its own category distinct from Fusion/Shadow/Ultra Beasts/Legendaries: temporal intrusion, not another generation unlock. Defined as Pokémon that do not belong to the world's normal timeline.
Explicitly NOT gated behind generation unlocks (would spoil the mystery outright — “Gen 9 unlocked → Paradox unlocked” was specifically rejected). Instead: Generation → Temporal Eligibility → Anomaly → Investigation → Encounter, mirroring how normal Pokémon follow Generation → Type Affinity → Habitat → Spawn. The Restoration Observatory would report “Temporal Resonance detected,” not a named Pokémon reveal — consistent with the atmosphere-before-identity principle already established for Legendary encounters (Part 10). Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
3. Ranks, Titles & Marks — Refinements
Confirms and extends the accomplishment-based rank system already in Part 1, plus the Resonance Mark candidates already in Part 9.
Multiple ranks can be earned, with one active rank/path identity selected at a time — changing the active one should avoid a tedious cooldown/delineation process.
Specialized titles (Master Researcher, Master Fisher) represent real accomplishment without becoming generic RPG classes.
Breeder is raised as another possible Player Path candidate alongside Trainer/Researcher/Ranger (Part 10) — status 🟡 Candidate, not yet at the same confidence as the three established paths.
Farmer/Agriculture is a further candidate path, proposed around controlled White Apricorn cultivation via Affinity-saturated growing conditions (Part 6) — status 🟡 Candidate, alongside Breeder and Artisan/Warden.
Resonance Marks get a proposed three-layer value model: Identity, Collection, and Resonance Interaction — giving marks structure beyond “cosmetic tag,” though the exact mechanical difference between the three layers isn't specified.
Two new mark candidates: a Launch-day/account-linked recognition mark (working title “You Were Here”) memorializing early/launch participation, proposed with account/UUID linkage; and Anniversary Marks/Phenomena/cosmetic skins tied to the same world-memory system as ordinary Resonance Marks.
Championship Hall, confirmed: a hall records multiple champions and their exact teams, letting each new champion update the living record without erasing previous ones — consistent with Part 2's “previous Champions aren't overwritten.”
Status: 🟡 Candidate throughout this section — real proposals, none locked.
4. Dens (flagged, not developed)
A separate Pokémon-world/encounter concept was raised in the source material and never developed beyond the name. Status: 🟠 Archival Unknown — noted here so it isn't lost, but there's nothing yet to build from beyond the fact that it was mentioned.
Branch | Progression sketch | Focus
Researcher | Field Observation → Ecological Survey → Anomaly Detection → Resonance Analysis → Unknown Lifeform | Documentation, behavior, anomaly/Fakemon recognition
Ranger | Trailcraft → Habitat Knowledge → Field Survey → Conservation → Sanctuary Keeper | Exploration, ecology, active habitat restoration
Battler | (sub-branches below) | Deliberately not flat combat bonuses — mastery and tactical options instead
Scientist/Geneticist | Species Study → Inheritance → Genetic Analysis → Mutation Research → Synthesis Theory → Advanced Fusion | Breeding, genetics, ties directly into Fusion/IV-Fusion (Part 9)
Engineer | Mechanical Knowledge → Automation → Infrastructure → Resonance Engineering → Restoration Engineer | Create-based manufacturing; “Resonance Engineering” lets machines interact with World Affinity directly
Arcanist | Arcane Apprentice → Spellcraft → Natural Conduit → Aura Manipulation → Resonance Magic → World Shaper | Ars Nouveau/Nature's Aura; teaches HOW you practice magic, not the magic itself
Occultist | Spirit Knowledge → Ritual Practice → Forbidden Study → Dimensional Research → Occult Resonance | Deliberately dangerous — real tradeoffs, not just “magic branch #2” (see 1.2)
 | Past Paradox | Future Paradox
Framing | Primal/Ancient/Temporal Regression | Synthetic/Futuristic/Temporal Progression
Feel | Biological, wild, prehistoric, overgrown, instinctive | Artificial, technological, sterile, energy-based, mechanical
Pillar tie | Life/Nature | Industry/Technology
Environmental clues | Strange weather, plants growing too fast, fossils appearing, ancient footprints, Pokémon fleeing, unusual Nature's Aura readings | Machinery malfunctioning, unexplained power drains, Create machines behaving strangely, electromagnetic disturbances, metallic footprints

# SOURCE: Master_Archive_Part12.docx
---

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Master Archive — Part 12: Ecological State, Biodiversity & World Health
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, under Free Reign Mode. A fuller sweep of pt1/pt2 turned up a substantially developed ecology layer sitting underneath the bare “World Affinity → Balance → Biodiversity → World Health” hierarchy already noted in Parts 1 and 9. This resolves two previously-open items: the “Dormant/Recovering/Stable/Flourishing/Resonant” naming flagged as possibly-alternate in Part 10, and the “Pokémon Ecological Roles” item flagged unresolved/future in Part 10.
1. Why Affinity Alone Isn't Enough
The core problem this layer solves, stated directly: 100% Affinity should not automatically mean “perfect ecosystem.” A region could have 100% Water Affinity and terrible biodiversity (a monoculture), or moderate Grass Affinity paired with extremely healthy biodiversity. High Affinity in one type is necessary but not sufficient for real ecological health — which is exactly why Biodiversity and World Health needed to exist as their own concepts rather than being read directly off the 18 Affinity numbers.
Two failure modes were explicitly designed against: a forest with 90% “ecological health” but nothing actually living there (a hollow number with no visible content), and the opposite — a forest with 100 trees and 40 Pokémon simply being declared “ecology = 82” without accounting for Nature's Aura, water availability, pollution, habitat fragmentation, climate, corruption, human activity, restoration structures, or species diversity. A forest full of trees isn't necessarily a healthy forest.
2. Ecological State as an Emergent Condition
The proposed fix: Ecological State is not a stored resource/number at all — it's an emergent, continuously-recalculated interpretation of several underlying conditions, closer to “the world's own read of what's happening” than a player-facing meter. A region might internally track something like:
...and from that combination, the region's resulting Ecological State might read as “Recovering Woodland.” That state — not the raw numbers — is what actually determines which Pokémon/flora are comfortable appearing there, and it updates continuously as the underlying conditions change rather than being a value the player directly edits.
Worked example: restoring a wetland
Given as the clearest illustration of the loop, worth preserving as a reference case: Water Affinity 30, Nature's Aura Weak, Vegetation Sparse → only hardy plants appear. Player restores an aqueduct and wetlands → Water becomes healthier → aquatic plants return → those plants create better habitat → small Pokémon return → those Pokémon disperse seeds and interact with vegetation → more plant species establish → biodiversity rises → more Pokémon become viable → the ecosystem becomes increasingly self-sustaining. Explicitly framed as far more interesting than “Ecology reached 50, unlock Swampert.”
Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction — fully reasoned through with a complete worked example; 🟡 Candidate for the exact underlying-condition list and how state-names get generated/labeled.
3. Fauna Feeds Back Into Ecology Too
Pokémon shouldn't be passengers in this system — they actively contribute abstracted ecological roles, resolving the item flagged unresolved/future in Part 10 with concrete examples:
Explicit implementation note: this does NOT need to be simulated per-individual-Pokémon — contributions are abstracted and aggregated (a thriving ecosystem might simply accumulate Pollination+, Seed Dispersal+, Soil Activity+, Aquatic Activity+), which then feeds back into the region's Ecological State above.
This also gives Pokémon absence real meaning, tying directly to the project's central premise: at the start of the game, the world isn't simply “Pokémon spawn rate = 0” — the ecosystem is missing an entire functional component. A region might have plants, functioning water, and recovering forest, but almost no fauna, leaving the ecosystem structurally incomplete until Pokémon actually return — directly appropriate given the premise that Pokémon were deliberately removed from the world in the first place.
4. Biodiversity & World Health (formal definitions)
Biodiversity could measure species variety, ecological roles present, population balance, and appropriate distribution — explicitly proposed to prevent “100% Affinity” from automatically reading as a perfect ecosystem. World Health sits a level above even that, proposed to synthesize Affinity, World Balance, Biodiversity, Resonance stability, and ecological damage/recovery together into one overall picture. Both explicitly marked as proposed architecture, not finalized formulas.
In consideration: Habitat → Ecosystem → World Health aggregation (Wesley, not yet locked)
A proposed way to actually make World Health computable rather than just “a synthesis of other things” in the abstract: individual habitats each carry their own quality rating (e.g. a specific wetland might rate “Really Good,” a specific woodland “Decent”), multiple habitats combine to rate the overall ecosystem they belong to, and multiple ecosystems combine to determine World Health — a literal bottom-up aggregation chain rather than a flat top-down number. Explicitly flagged by Wesley as just a thought for now, recorded here specifically so it doesn't get lost before a decision is made. Status: 🟠 In consideration — not evaluated or locked, purely preserved for later discussion.
5. Restoration Stages (resolves Part 10's naming flag)
Part 10 flagged “Dormant → Recovering → Stable → Flourishing → Resonant” as a possibly-alternate name for the World Affinity tier table in Part 1. This is now confirmed as a genuinely separate, real concept called Restoration Stages — describing the overall ecological condition of a region or the whole world (fed by Ecological State, Biodiversity, and World Health together), distinct from any single Affinity's own 0–999 tier table. Reaching the later Flourishing/Resonant stages was specifically tied to biodiversity being genuinely healthy, not just one Affinity being maxed. Governing rule, worth keeping close to verbatim: degraded does not mean unplayable — the early world should still support basic survival. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction; supersedes the “possibly alternate” framing in Part 10 — it's a distinct, real system, not a duplicate name for something else.
6. Pokémon Care (new)
A separate proposal for how Pokémon matter outside combat: feeding, resting, friendship, environment, activity, grooming, healing, and enrichment. Proposed to feed Trainer Affinity directly, and possibly indirectly influence ecological outcomes as well. Status: 🟠 Unresolved/future — named and scoped, not mechanically developed.
7. Capture → Release → Reintroduction (confirmed chain)
Ties together several pieces already scattered across Parts 1 and 10 into one explicit progression, worth preserving as a single reference chain: Capture (small World Affinity contribution) → Repeated capture (diminishing returns) → Release (returns the Pokémon to the ecosystem, potentially a positive contribution) → Research (knowledge rather than just material reward) → Reintroduction (a deliberate ecological action — capture → research → care → relocate → release into a suitable habitat — with much greater ecological significance than an ordinary release). Explicitly framed against “catch Pokémon because the Pokédex says 100%.” Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
8. The Full Habitat → Ecosystem → World Health Audit
A dedicated full audit recovered substantially more architecture than Sections 1–7 above captured alone. Central finding, worth stating as the section's thesis: the world does not become healthy because a number reaches 100 — the number rises because the world is becoming healthy. Ecology is a feedback system, not a master meter dictating a spawn table.
The locked hierarchy, restated with its full pipeline
18 World Affinities → Affinity Relationships → Regional Ecological Conditions/Habitat → Flora + Fauna → Ecological Balance → Biodiversity/Population Health → Resonance Stability/damage/recovery → World Health, with Nature's Aura operating underneath as the physical natural-energy layer and the Restoration Engine as the connective computational brain. Each layer answers a distinct question: World Affinity — can this kind of life exist? Habitat — does this specific place provide the necessary conditions? Ecological State — what condition is this ecosystem in right now? Biodiversity — is the living community diverse and functional? Ecological Balance — are its forces coexisting sustainably? Resonance Stability — is the underlying Resonance coherent? World Health — what's the resulting overall vitality? Status: 🕒 Locked for the hierarchy itself; formulas deliberately unspecified.
Dual-type ecology requires both Affinities AND proximity
A dual-type Pokémon shouldn't spawn just because one of its two Affinities is high. Both ecosystems need to exist, both Affinities need to be sufficiently high, AND their values need to be reasonably close together. Illustrative: Water 70 / Ice 64 supports a Water/Ice ecosystem; Water 90 / Ice 15 should not, despite Water being even higher. This is an early concrete example of Affinity Relationships feeding habitat/ecology directly.
Four sources of Affinity generation
Actions — casting, rituals, mining, smelting, farming, exploration, research, battles
Infrastructure — buildings/machines providing small ongoing contributions
Restoration Projects — major restoration producing meaningful jumps
Major Events — discoveries, miracles, Legendary events producing major changes
Multiple paths were explicitly favored over one fixed activity per Affinity — for Fire, illustrative alternatives included an industrial route (smelt 1,000 items), an exploration route (discover three volcanic locations), a survival route (survive 100 lava encounters), a magic route (complete a fire ritual), and a story route (recover the Ember Core), with any two or three able to contribute toward awakening rather than requiring one specific grind.
Diminishing returns and negative ecological pressure
Repeated identical actions should not scale linearly forever (illustrative, not locked: first spell +2, 10 spells +5, 50 spells +10, 100 spells +10, a major ritual +20, tapering rather than compounding). Not every action should be positive: over-extraction, overfishing, excessive clearing, and pollution/industrial pressure can apply negative ecological pressure — illustrative example: a huge industrial project giving Steel +20 but Grass −15 because habitat was destroyed. Explicit restriction: negative consequences should NOT happen constantly, or players become afraid to engage with any system — major ecological decisions should matter more than routine gameplay.
Restoration Structure Material Signatures (genuinely novel mechanic)
Restoring a structure doesn't have to follow one fixed recipe — the actual materials the player uses can determine what it becomes. Worked example: a shrine built from Obsidian/Blackstone/Soul Lanterns/Crying Obsidian becomes Ghost-dominant; one built from Prismarine/Sea Lanterns/aquatic materials becomes Water-dominant. Crucially, MIXED material ratios can produce genuinely new hybrid results — 40% Obsidian + 40% Sea Lantern + 20% Amethyst was given as an example producing an “Abyssal Tide Shrine” (Ghost + Water), which could then influence its own local encounters, quests, NPCs, rituals, and resources. The Restoration Engine would need to inspect the player's actual construction rather than reading a fixed recipe ID.
Restoration was also proposed to be meaningfully permanent — you shouldn't be able to swap a completed Ghost Shrine into a Water Shrine by breaking a few blocks. Proposed process: dismantle → purify the restoration core → remove the old Resonance → rebuild → perform reconstruction. This supports “this is what I chose this place to become” as a real, weighty decision.
Proposed building archetypes beyond shrines, each with type-variant flavors (illustrative, not a locked catalog): Observatory (Psychic/Fairy/Astral/Dark), Forge (Fire/Steel/Electric/Magitech), Garden (Grass/Poison/Fairy/Bug), Tower (Flying/Dragon/Ice/Ghost), Laboratory (Electric/Psychic/Poison/Genetics). Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction for the Material Signature concept and hybrid-result idea — genuinely one of the more novel mechanics recovered in this pass; 🟡 Candidate for the exact material-ratio math and building catalog.
Factions as competing ecological philosophies, with real trade-offs
Faction actions can pull World Affinity in different directions simultaneously — illustrative: expanding the Iron Rail through protected forest giving Iron Accord +50 but Verdant Circle −20. A “Forest Crisis” example (Grass Affinity reaching 90) offered three player choices with different consequences: Protect the forest (Verdant +100, Iron −50), Controlled mining (Iron +75, Verdant −30), or Joint conservation/mining (both +40, but requires high reputation with both factions and unlocks a unique facility). This makes World Health partly a consequence of which faction philosophy the player actually supports, not just an abstract score.
Non-Cobblemon wildlife (three layers)
Explicitly proposed as three coexisting ecological layers: (1) Mundane wildlife — rabbits, deer, birds, insects, fish; (2) Magical wildlife — creatures from magic/creature mods that can inhabit ley lines, guard ruins, migrate, react to weather, avoid Cobblemon, gather near magical structures, or turn aggressive near corruption; (3) Cobblemon — the missing apex/ecological component the whole project is about restoring. This matters because World Health isn't supposed to mean “number of Pokémon” — the entire living world matters, and Pokémon are specifically the piece that's missing.
Restored types change the visible world (partial list, not a locked 18-type table)
Explicitly NOT a complete 18-type environmental table — that would need to be built out later, not invented retroactively here. Broader Affinity associations already exist informally across the archive (Fire→industry/volcanism/heat, Ground/Rock→geology, Ice→frozen environments, Poison→contamination/remediation, Psychic→magic/Resonance, Ghost→spirits/ruins, Dark→corruption/forbidden systems, Dragon→rare high-order relationships) but were never assembled into one finished table.
The four types of unlocks (clean structural distinction)
This cleanly separates World Health from individual Pokémon discovery — restoring Fire doesn't automatically mean every player has personally earned every Fire Pokémon everywhere. Status: 🕒 Locked as a structural distinction.
Ecological Nature (descriptive, explicitly not another meter)
A proposed emergent descriptive state layered on top of the ecological hierarchy — Calm, Lush, Wild, Ancient, Magical, Corrupted — potentially influencing Pokémon behavior, flora, spawning, events, regional variants, Legendary signs, and Nature's Aura. Explicitly not meant to become another progression bar the player tracks directly — it's read FROM the world's state, not written TO by the player. Status: 🟡 Candidate.
Historical contradictions, preserved rather than silently resolved
Starting ecological health: one early concept had all 18 Affinities begin at 0; a separate historical proposal suggested overall ecological health beginning around 20–30% so the opening world wouldn't feel miserable. These may not actually conflict — World Affinity and the broader World Health layer are now understood as different things — but that reconciliation was never formally locked.
Restoration-stage naming: at least three different naming sequences exist in the source material (Dead→Dormant→Recovering→Stable→Flourishing→Resonant; Barren→Recovering→Established→Thriving→Lush→Ancient→Magical→Corrupted; and a shorter Barren→Recovering→Healthy→Lush→Wild→Ancient→Magical→Corrupted list). These may represent global restoration stages, regional ecological descriptors, and ecological “Natures” respectively, but that distinction was never formally completed — remains an open architectural question, not a locked table.
“Resonance Region” as a name was explicitly downgraded to TBD while the underlying Regional Affinity/regional-ecological-state concept remains strong — the concept survives, the specific name doesn't yet.
Affinity progression scale: at least four different numeric framings exist (0–999 with named tiers, per Part 1; 0–20/20–40/etc. conceptual bands, per Part 1's Trainer Affinity section; simple 0→25→50→75→100; and an illustrative Affinity-suppression matrix with values like Fire suppressing Grass/Bug/Ice while boosting Rock/Dragon slightly). No single scale is locked as final.
Per the Change Log Archive's own purpose, these are preserved as evidence of the architecture's evolution, not defects to quietly clean up.

Climate | Temperate

Player Unlock | Based on that player's actions | “You have earned the trust of Fire.”

# SOURCE: Master_Archive_Part13.docx
---

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Master Archive — Part 13: Resonance Synthesis Apparatus, Provenance & Design Gaps
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, under Free Reign Mode. Source: the deepest single sweep pass yet on Fusion/Resonance Synthesis. The large majority confirms what's already locked in Parts 2, 9, and 10 with no contradictions — a strong consistency signal. This part covers only what's genuinely new: the Anchor/Partner mechanic, the named apparatus, Resonance Provenance, and — most valuable — an honest, structured list of exactly what isn't designed yet.
1. Anchor Pokémon & Resonant Partner
A previously undocumented role system for two-Pokémon Synthesis, answering a real gap: if a Synthesis fails, who actually gets hurt? Anchor Pokémon: the one whose Resonance structure is intentionally being altered. Resonant Partner: the one whose Resonance Signature is being introduced/interacted with, without itself being the target of alteration. The player establishes this direction deliberately rather than the game picking arbitrarily.
Asymmetric failure outcome: if the attempt is rejected, the Anchor becomes Fractured (or Shadow, if corruption is present) while the Partner is NOT destroyed — it returns safely, but can retain a Resonance Imprint: Affinity Imprinting, temporary Affinity Exposure, an altered Aura, unusual move behavior, or residual Resonance, all of which are research-valuable evidence rather than nothing happening. Confirms and sharpens Part 9's Affinity Imprinting concept with a concrete mechanical trigger (being the Partner in a failed Synthesis) rather than a general “can happen sometimes.” Status: 🕒 Locked — the Anchor/Partner role system, the asymmetric failure outcome, and the Partner-retains-Imprint mechanic are all locked (Wesley, Sept 2026), including the underlying Affinity Imprinting linkage this depends on.
Material component added (Part 3, Section 1a½): Affinity Essence is the catalytic medium that makes this whole interaction possible in the first place — Anchor + Resonant Partner + Affinity Essence → Resonance Synthesis. The Essence doesn't determine the outcome; it's what allows the two Resonance signatures to attempt interaction at all.
2. The Resonance Synthesis Chamber (named apparatus)
The strongest recovered name for the actual physical apparatus where Synthesis happens — appears consistently across the Ditto Synthesis and Genesis (Mew + Mew) discussions already in Part 2. Proposed role: Research Center → Resonance Synthesis Chamber → insert Pokémon → establish synthesis → evaluate Resonance → compatibility → stability → outcome, with the Restoration Observatory reacting during the process for high-stakes cases (per Genesis, Part 2).
Confirmed direction, Wesley (Sept 2026): the Chamber is a genuinely important Resonance Machine — likely built as a small multiblock structure rather than a single block, reflecting its significance relative to ordinary Resonance Processing machinery (Part 6). Status: 🕒 Locked for the name, role, and multiblock direction; exact GUI, block/recipe structure, catalyst slots, and full multiblock layout remain 🟡 Candidate — real implementation detail, not yet specified.
⚠ Naming discrepancy, flagged rather than silently resolved: a separate sweep pass recovered a different apparatus name for what appears to be the same or a closely related machine — Resonance Synthesizer, reached via a slightly different recovered progression (Pokémon Research → Advanced Resonance Research → Study unusual Pokémon → Discover Synthesis → Build Resonance Synthesizer → Fusion Research). Both “Resonance Synthesis Chamber” and “Resonance Synthesizer” describe the same functional role (the place where Synthesis physically happens), recovered independently in two different sweeps — it's unclear whether these are two names for the same historical concept, sequential terminology evolution, or genuinely two different pieces of equipment (e.g. a research-tier Synthesizer preceding the more significant multiblock Chamber). Not resolved here — flagged for Wesley's direct call rather than picking one.
Synthesis direction, resolved: piggyback on an existing fusion mod's base-selection mechanic
Resolves the “Synthesis direction” item on the Design Gap List (Section 8): Wesley identified that at least one fusion mod already has a directional system where the same two Pokémon produce different results depending on which is treated as the base/primary in the recipe (A-as-base + B-as-component vs. B-as-base + A-as-component). Rather than inventing a new Anchor/Partner selection UI from scratch, this existing base-selection mechanic can be mapped directly onto Anchor/Partner: whichever Pokémon the player designates as the base IS the Anchor (the one being altered); the other is the Resonant Partner. This is a clean, low-effort integration — the mechanic already exists in a mod candidate (Cobblemon Poke Fusion and/or CobblemonStarlightFusion, Part 4), it just needed the right conceptual label applied to it. Status: 🕒 Locked as the direction-selection solution; which specific mod actually provides it is still pending the Poke-Fusion-vs-Starlight-Fusion resolution flagged separately.
Handling both Resonance Fusion and IV Fusion in one Chamber
Wesley confirmed this was the intended design: both Resonance Fusion and IV Fusion happen at the same Synthesis Chamber, with the player selecting which process they want before it begins, each with its own respective catalysts (e.g. Nature Mints for temperament-related IV Fusion effects, vitamin-style candies for stat/IV effects — consistent with the stat-item reclassification already locked in Part 4 Section 13).
Claude's proposed integration architecture, offered per Wesley's request for ideas: rather than building two unrelated machines, the Chamber should be one physical multiblock with a mode-select step as the first thing the player does when opening it — Resonance Fusion mode or IV Fusion mode. Selecting a mode changes which catalyst slots are active and which backend system actually runs the process: Resonance Fusion mode routes into the custom Poke-Fusion-based species pipeline (Part 1) using Affinity/environmental-exposure catalysts (Part 8's Fire/Water/Grass/etc. exposure methods); IV Fusion mode routes into the Brecher's-based pipeline (Part 4 Section 13) using the reclassified stat items as catalysts. This keeps one unified player-facing structure and interaction point (thematically: “one Chamber, two disciplines”) while cleanly separating the two technical backends under the hood, and it directly supports the already-locked chaining idea (Part 2: a Resonance Fusion result can later be brought back to the same Chamber and refined through IV Fusion mode). Status: 🟡 Candidate — a proposed architecture, not yet confirmed as final by Wesley.
3. Resonance Signature Shift
A Pokémon's Resonance signature isn't necessarily static — repeated exposure can shift its dominant color/signature over time (e.g. a Grass-associated Pokémon showing Green → Green+Blue interference → Blue-dominant after sustained Water exposure). The Field Journal could report this as “Resonance Signature Shift Detected.” Proposed specifically as a bridge between Affinity Exposure, Affinity Adaptation, and Resonance Synthesis without collapsing them into the same mechanic. Status: 🟡 Candidate.
4. Resonance Color Hierarchy
A priority order for what determines a Pokémon's displayed Resonance color/visual signature, useful for both ordinary Pokémon and Fusion results: (1) explicit Resonance Attunement, if one has been deliberately applied; (2) dominant Affinity signature; (3) the Pokémon's natural Affinity; (4) primary Pokémon type, as the final fallback. A fused Pokémon would visually inherit whichever dominant Resonance signature applies through this hierarchy, rather than defaulting to its primary type's color. Status: 🕒 Locked (Wesley, Sept 2026), alongside Resonance Signature itself (Part 9). A canonical 18-color Resonance palette was also proposed but left unfinished — flagged here as a real open task, not forgotten, and not covered by this lock.
5. Fusion Category History — Resolved
Part 1 originally listed eight early brainstormed Fusion categories (Genetic, Affinity, Shadow, Restoration, Fakemon, Triple, Corrupted, and Perfect Resonance) as an illustrative, unfinalized menu. Corrected terminology lineage, per Wesley's direct clarification (Sept 2026): “Tainted Fusion” and “Corrupted Fusion” were never a rejected idea — they are simply the historical/earlier names for the two Aberrations already locked in Part 2. Tainted Fusion → became Fractured Fusion (the rare Aberration). Corrupted Fusion → became Shadow Fusion (the extremely rare Aberration). Per the Canonical Terminology Normalization Principle (Authorities Archive), Fractured Fusion and Shadow Fusion are the current names used throughout this archive; Tainted/Corrupted Fusion are preserved here purely as the historical naming record, not as separate or competing concepts.
“Perfect Resonance” (an extremely rare legendary-tier Fusion, from early brainstorming) — superseded by Resonance Aberration, the broader locked framework both of the above Aberrations fall under (Part 9).
The remaining early categories (Genetic Fusion, Affinity Fusion, Restoration Fusion, Fakemon Fusion, Triple Fusion) are preserved as historical/proposed terminology per the Archive Preservation Principle — not promoted to canon, not deleted either. Status: 🕒 Locked — both the terminology lineage and the historical-preservation of the remaining categories.
6. The Adaptation “Rosetta Stone” — Eevee and Family
Eevee is confirmed as the flagship demonstrator of Affinity Adaptation — teaching the player that Affinity can produce legitimate biological adaptation, with other species then teaching specialized versions of the same underlying idea:
Eeveelutional Resonance — confirmed and scoped by Wesley (Sept 2026): this is now the name of the research category / questline / advancement set covering the whole Eeveelution-related story, not just a banked arc name. Rationale, worth preserving directly: Eevee already carries the flagship role as the poster child for Affinity Adaptation (the table above), and with as many Eeveelutions as the line has (natural, Extra Eeveelutions per Part 2, and the Synthetic branch below), that family earns a properly named, notable role of its own rather than being folded anonymously into general Research. Status: 🕒 Locked for the name and its scope (research category + questline + advancement set); 🟡 Candidate for the actual content/structure inside it, which hasn't been designed yet.
Synthetic Adaptation (third branch, via Cobblemon Syn. Eevees)
A third branch of Eevee's adaptive story, via the Cobblemon - Syn. Eevees datapack (CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0 — licensing must be respected if assets are ever modified/redistributed): Natural Eevee (“my environment changed me”, Affinity Adaptation) / Synthetic Eevee (“humankind changed me”, artificial intervention) / Resonance-driven Eevee (“Resonance changed me”, a proposed third state). Not a finalized mechanic, but a strong integration proposal building a clean philosophical triangle out of three different causes for the same creature changing. Status: 🟡 Candidate.
7. Resonance Provenance (proposed, not locked)
A bigger, not-yet-canonical concept the sweep itself flags as an emerging pattern rather than an established term: given that Materials already have Latent Provenance (Part 6), Pokémon can carry Resonance Imprints (Section 1 above, Part 9), and Research already produces detailed records, a fused Pokémon could plausibly remember its full synthesis history — original Species A and B, primary/secondary Resonance, the synthesis/environmental conditions, catalysts used, research state at the time, corruption exposure, and stability history. Explicitly NOT promoted to canon by the sweep itself — recorded here as a strong architectural fit worth revisiting, not a decision.
8. Honest Design Gap List — What's Actually Left
The single most useful output of this pass: a structured account of what's still genuinely undesigned, as opposed to philosophically unresolved. Worth treating as a working checklist for whenever Resonance Synthesis implementation actually begins.
Synthesis inputs — exactly what can enter the Chamber (2 Pokémon? 3? items, materials, Affinity, environment, catalysts alongside them?).
Synthesis direction — RESOLVED (Section 2 above): the base-selection mechanic already present in a candidate fusion mod determines Anchor/Partner directly.
Compatibility calculation — what variables actually contribute and how they combine mathematically (Affinity compatibility + signature compatibility + environment + equipment + materials + research knowledge + corruption = compatibility, but no formula exists yet).
Stability calculation — same gap, separate variable.
Catalyst architecture — which specific materials affect compatibility vs. stability vs. output vs. mutation vs. failure severity vs. rarity. The material ecosystem (Part 3) exists; its Synthesis-specific application doesn't yet.
Failure severity — what actually determines harmless rejection vs. Fractured vs. severe Fracture vs. Shadow vs. Aberration.
Recovery — can Fractured Pokémon recover? Can Shadow Pokémon be restored? Via Affinity, Resonance, research, purification, world restoration, or a dedicated process?
Fusion persistence — what happens to the original Pokémon(s)' moves, IVs, EVs, nature, friendship, Pokédex identity, ribbons, memories, caught date, and provenance data through the Synthesis?
Reversal — RESOLVED: Fusion cannot be undone (Wesley, Sept 2026). It is a permanent biological state, not something reversible via another Synthesis.
Framing worth preserving directly: the conceptual architecture of what Resonance Synthesis IS is substantially complete and consistent; what's missing is almost entirely the procedure — what a player physically does, what gets calculated, and how failure/recovery/persistence actually work. That's genuinely good news — it means future work on this system is implementation design, not further conceptual invention.
Process note from Wesley on how the remaining gaps get resolved: each will be fleshed out individually before everything gets reintegrated, rather than trying to solve all of them simultaneously. The reintegration pass itself follows a specific discipline — see the Meaningful Integration Principle, Authorities Archive — connecting pieces because there's an articulated reason they should connect, not simply because they technically could.
Pokémon | Concept demonstrated
Eevee | Affinity Adaptation (the baseline case)
Castform | Environmental adaptation
Silvally | Artificial/type-memory adaptation (ties directly to Type Memory materials, Part 3)
Rotom | Resonant interface (ties to the Shadow Rotom case already in Part 2)
Burmy | Environmental form adaptation
Mimikyu | Convergent adaptation
