# SOURCE: Master_Archive_Part5.docx
---

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Master Archive — Part 5: Technical Implementation Notes
Compiled by Claude, September 2026. Source: the “Cobblemon EcoRe JavaScript example” document. That document is mostly iterative KubeJS code drafts — the same two scripts (a Personal Affinity slot calculator and a slot-unlock blueprint) reworked roughly six times with small refinements, plus scattered one-off scripts for quest gating, starter selection, and catch-event hooks. Rather than reproducing draft code in a design archive, this note preserves the concrete design decisions the code reveals — the source document itself remains the implementation reference. Everything below is 🕒 Locked in the sense that it's concrete, tested-in-script logic rather than an open brainstorm, though none of it has been confirmed as actually built/running in the modpack yet.
1. Personal Affinity Slot Count — Resolved to 6
Part 1 left the active-affinity capacity as “4–6 depending on progression, exact numbers tunable.” The implementation scripts confirm the endgame number as exactly 6, unlocked via a specific gate sequence rather than a smooth curve. Refinement recovered via the parallel full-sweep pass: slot assignment is organic, not manually locked — the script automatically sorts the player's 18 Affinity scores and assigns the highest-ranking ones to however many slots are currently unlocked. If a lower-active Affinity (e.g. Ghost) eventually overtakes a currently-active one (e.g. Water), Ghost automatically claims the higher slot rather than the player needing to manually swap. Status: 🟡 Candidate — the parallel sweep flags this specific point as still unresolved: whether slot assignment should be fully automatic, fully manual, or a hybrid (automatic ranking with a manual override) has not been decided. Preserve as an open question rather than assuming full automation is final.
The gate sequence itself:
Slot 1: assigned automatically at character creation to the starter's element (example given: choosing Charmander auto-assigns Slot 1 to Fire at Personal Affinity 25).
Slots 2–4: unlocked through ordinary progression (mechanism not fully specified in the surviving script fragments).
Slot 5 (“The Legendary Chosen One”): unlocked by reaching Personal Affinity 100 on any one type, OR capturing a Legendary/Mythical Pokémon — requires crafting a Legendary Attunement Core (Resonance Shards + a Nether Star + a Diamond Block) and right-clicking it; the item refuses to consume and tells the player what they're missing if requirements aren't met yet.
Slot 6 (“Cosmic Convergence”): unlocked by clearing the modpack's endgame boss / main content (a persistent “pack_cleared” flag), via a Cosmic Nexus Key (Cosmic Lens + Netherite Ingot + Nether Star). Explicitly designed as a New Game Plus trigger.
2. Replay Mode (New Game Plus)
A previously undocumented system: completing the pack once (the same “pack_cleared” flag that unlocks Slot 6) enables a Replay Mode for future save files. On a Replay Mode save, choosing a starter grants an upgraded starting loadout beyond the normal kit — the given example: choosing a Dragon-type starter on a cleared-pack file grants the standard starting gear plus custom Dragon-attuned Resonance Balls, extra Ender Pearls, and an instant +25 starting Personal Dragon Affinity, letting a replaying player field-test a build immediately rather than regrinding from zero. This directly implements the Replayability goal from Part 4 (different playthroughs should diverge) as a concrete NG+ mechanic rather than just varied starting conditions.
Confirmed by the parallel full-sweep pass: the completion flag is persistent and stored outside the individual world (so it survives world deletion/recreation), and the plan discussed was to bypass/replace the native starter-selection screen via KubeJS so a Replay Mode save can expose secret/challenge starters unavailable on a first playthrough, on top of the bonus loadout above. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction — confirmed as a real designed system across two independent sweeps; exact bonus loadouts per element and the secret-starter roster are not fully specified.
3. Resonance/Affinity Ball Implementation — the Strange Ball Hijack
A concrete technical path was worked out for implementing the 18 Resonance/Affinity Balls (referenced only conceptually in Part 3's material families) without writing a full Java addon: Cobblemon's Strange Ball already has a fully-coded 3D model, inventory texture, thrown-entity state, and capture animation, and Cobblemon falls back to rendering it whenever it encounters a Poké Ball variant it can't otherwise resolve. The proposed approach hijacks this via resource packs (distinct visuals per Affinity) and data packs (distinct recipes per Affinity) layered on top of the single underlying Strange Ball item, avoiding custom Java code for the visual/recipe layer. Status: ⚪ Historical/superseded — this was the original plan; see the project-direction update further down this section for the current preferred approach.
4. Custom Poké Ball / Fakemon Implementation Options
This directly addresses the “Fakemon implementation” item on Part 4's design backlog, specifically for anything needing genuinely custom Poké Ball behavior (not just reskinning). Cobblemon's Poké Ball entities/items are hardcoded, so a purely data-pack-only approach cannot create new custom capture-logic behavior; three implementation paths were laid out:
KubeJS approach (recommended as easiest for a modpack, not a standalone mod): register the custom item via a KubeJS startup script, then intercept Cobblemon's capture events server-side to apply custom catch-rate math (e.g. weather or status-condition modifiers) without writing Java.
Lightweight NeoForge addon: register the item through Cobblemon's own API classes as a true PokeBallItem subclass, overriding the catch-calculation method or hooking Cobblemon's PreCaptureEvent/CaptureCalculationEvent directly — cleaner integration, more setup work.
Piggyback on an existing framework: the Modular Poké Balls mod was identified as a possible no-code backend, letting new ball logic be defined via data packs against its predefined catch-power “cores” rather than building capture logic from scratch.
Status: 🟡 Candidate set of implementation paths; no final choice was locked in.
Alternative path: extend the apricorn → ball crafting chain (AllTheMons precedent)
A second real-world approach, found by researching the All the Mons modpack (ATMons — All the Mods 10 + Cobblemon, 1.9M+ downloads) rather than from the original transcripts. Instead of hijacking the Strange Ball fallback or building fully custom PokeBallItem classes, ATMons' own coremod extends Cobblemon's existing native apricorn-tree-→-ball crafting pipeline: it adds new apricorn tree variants tied to the pack's own tech-tier materials (AllTheModium, Vibranium, Unobtainium, Soul Lava), each producing a themed custom ball through that same existing crafting chain, via a small dedicated NeoForge coremod rather than a parallel ball system built from scratch.
Applied to C.E.R., the equivalent would be: 18 new apricorn tree variants (or a smaller grouped set), each tied to a Type Gem/Affinity Essence input instead of a tech material, each yielding that Affinity's themed Resonance/Affinity Ball — plugging directly into a system Cobblemon already fully supports, rather than working around the Strange Ball's fallback rendering. This is a genuine alternative to the Strange Ball hijack from Section 3, not a replacement for it — which path to take is intentionally left open until implementation actually begins on the ball system. Status: 🟡 Candidate — proven at scale in a comparable pack, not yet chosen over the other option.
Project direction update: Strange Ball retexture → Modular Poké Balls as the base
Correction/update from Wesley, not a third AllTheMons technique: the project's own plan for the Resonance/Affinity Ball system has shifted. The original approach was the Strange Ball hijack in Section 3 (retexture the Strange Ball via resource/data packs, then layer custom implementation on top). The current direction is to potentially use the Modular Poké Balls mod (Section 4's Option 3) as the actual base framework instead — building C.E.R.'s own ball designs into Modular Poké Balls' predefined catch-power "cores" system rather than retexturing the Strange Ball fallback. Framed as "potentially," not yet fully locked in.
Status: the Strange Ball hijack (Section 3) is being held in reserve rather than treated as dead — Wesley's own framing, per his direct input: it was his first idea before he knew the other paths existed, and he wants it kept as a last-resort fallback if the other two don't pan out, not discarded outright. Downgraded from 🟡 Candidate to ⚪ Historical/superseded still applies for “not the current direction,” but read that status as “banked, not buried.”
Modular Poké Balls as base is now Wesley's stronger lean, with explicit reasoning worth preserving: most of the underlying system is already built (so it doubles as a fast proof-of-concept before deciding whether a standalone addon is worth it), and critically, it hands the player genuine creative agency over ball-making — “I wonder what this, this, and this will make” rather than “they made the ball do X, that's cool,” which fits the project's Research/Discovery theming (D.O.P.) far better than a purely designer-authored ball roster. The apricorn-crafting-chain path and the Section 4 KubeJS/NeoForge options remain open alternatives, not eliminated.
Noted want, not yet a design: Wesley would like to find a way to include a Dark Ball (from the anime) somewhere in this system — flagged here for whenever ball implementation actually starts.
5. Custom Species Fusion — Full Worked Example (Solara)
A second technical conversation (provided separately by Wesley, possibly from a different ChatGPT account/session — not certain it was already in pt1–pt3) confirms and extends the Fusion pipeline from Part 1 with a complete worked example. Confirmed: Poke Fusion's own documented recipe format uses speciesId for both inputs and the result, and does not need to understand or generate a model itself — it only needs a valid target species ID to exist somewhere in Cobblemon's species system.
Worked example given: Charizard + Lucario → a new custom species called Solara (ecoresonance:solara, Fire/Steel). The addon file structure laid out: a data/cobblemon/species/custom/solara.json (gameplay data — types, abilities, base stats, catch rate, egg groups, etc.) plus a resource-side set under assets/cobblemon/bedrock/pokemon/ — a Blockbench model (models/solara/solara.geo.json), a poser (posers/solara.json, binding idle/walk/battle/cry/faint animations), a resolver (resolvers/solara/0_solara_base.json, the file that actually links the species ID to its model/texture/poser), textures (including a shiny variant), and a lang file entry for name/Pokédex description. The Poke Fusion recipe itself then only needs three lines: the two input speciesIds and the result speciesId — confirming the pipeline diagram in Part 1.
A recommended dedicated namespace convention was reconfirmed: ecoresonance:<name> (e.g. ecoresonance:solara, ecoresonance:shadow_dragon, ecoresonance:verdant_eevee) to avoid colliding with Cobblemon's own species IDs and to let Shadow/Fusion/restored-ancient/Affinity-specific Pokémon coexist cleanly. Recommended practical next step, worth preserving as advice for whenever implementation actually starts: build one single Charizard+Lucario→Solara prototype end-to-end first (confirm it spawns with its custom model) before building out the rest of the Fusion roster, and lock down the exact Minecraft/Cobblemon/Poke Fusion versions being targeted before writing the addon, since folder structure and JSON schemas have changed across Cobblemon versions (older addons pre-1.4 used a different resolver folder convention). Status: 🕒 Locked — this is a confirmed-workable technical pipeline, checked directly against current Poke Fusion/Cobblemon documentation rather than assumed.
Cobblemon's aspect/resolver system was also reconfirmed as the mechanism for giving one fused species further progression states without needing separate species entries — e.g. a single ecoresonance:solara could carry normal / shadow / stellar / perfected / unstable / awakened visual aspects, meaning a Fusion result doesn't have to be a dead end (Charizard + Lucario → Solara → Fire Affinity Resonance achieved → Awakened Solara → Shadow Solara → Perfect Resonance Solara, all still one underlying species handled by Cobblemon's normal rendering).
6. Assigning Affinity to an Individual Pokémon
Confirmed design decision: Affinity should be an individual Pokémon property, not a species-level or form-level property — i.e. two wild Pikachu can have different Affinities from each other (e.g. one Storm-affinity, one Verdant-affinity), the same way two individual Pokémon can have different natures or IVs today. This was an explicit choice given how deeply Affinity was already planned to interact with Resonance, Shadow, Fusion, Fakemon, factions, and Dynamic Spawning — locking Affinity to species or form would have been simpler but much less flexible.
Explicit caution: don't just add an arbitrary “affinity” field directly to Cobblemon's species JSON — Cobblemon's species schema doesn't natively support it, so an unrecognized field would not automatically become a functioning mechanic. Three real implementation approaches were laid out:
Status: 🕒 Locked for “Affinity is per-individual-Pokémon, implemented via custom data/component, not a raw species-JSON field”; 🟡 Candidate for the exact component schema, which wasn't specified further pending confirmation of the target Cobblemon version.
Claude's technical opinion, per Wesley's request
Asked directly whether this approach holds up: yes, this is the right call, for the reason the original answer gave — Cobblemon Pokémon already carry persistent per-individual data (IVs, EVs, nature, ability, etc.), so a custom data component attached the same way is the idiomatic, low-friction approach rather than a workaround. Two things worth adding on top of what was already said:
Concretely, this should use NeoForge's Data Attachment API (the modern standard for exactly this kind of per-instance custom data, introduced to replace the older, more boilerplate-heavy Capability pattern) rather than a bespoke Capability implementation — less code, and it's what current Cobblemon addons generally reach for first.
The real risk isn't the initial assignment logic, it's persistence through everything Cobblemon does to a Pokémon afterward — PC storage, trading, dimension changes, server restarts. That's historically where these systems quietly break. Worth a small end-to-end prototype early (assign an Affinity, then deliberately trade/PC-store/reload and confirm it survives) before building anything else on top of the assumption that it will.
Status: 🔵 Current proposal — Claude's own technical opinion, offered as requested, not a recovered decision.
7. Attunement Cores (historical name: Resonance Cores)
Newly surfaced by Wesley via a separate ChatGPT conversation — not previously in the archives. This is the original, more developed meaning of “Resonance Core,” distinct from the Part 3 materials-table entry now renamed Resonance Nexus (see that entry's note for the naming history).
Concept: Type Gems were always meant to be more than ordinary crafting materials — they represent condensed, type-specific Resonance/Affinity. Attunement Cores are the next refinement step: Fire Type Gem → Fire Attunement Core, Water Type Gem → Water Attunement Core, Ghost Type Gem → Ghost Attunement Core, and so on across all 18 Affinities. Their primary intended use is specialized Poké Ball construction — giving a ball real type-specific properties instead of a generic “5× catch rate” modifier:
Better compatibility with Pokémon of that Affinity
Improved capture behavior
Increased research value
Interaction with the player's Personal/Trainer Affinity
Environmental bonuses
Special behavior around anomalies or particular ecosystems
Worked examples given: a Fire Attunement Core granting advantages around Fire Pokémon and high-temperature environments; a Ghost Attunement Core interacting with Ghost Pokémon, Occultism, Shadow phenomena, haunted structures, and death-related discoveries — directly relevant to the ball-system paths in Section 6 above, since this is likely the actual per-type specialization layer any of those three ball-implementation paths would ultimately use.
Naming history: originally called Resonance Core(s). Renamed to Attunement Core specifically because “Resonance Core” inside a “Resonance Ball” read as muddy — the established distinction became Core = the physical capture-power component, Attunement Core = the type/Affinity specialization component, Resonance Ball = the finished, Affinity-aligned ball configuration/philosophy that can contribute to World Affinity. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction for the core concept and the historical→current naming; 🟡 Candidate for exact mechanical properties and values, which were illustrative rather than finalized.
❓ Open question worth Wesley's input: Part 1/Part 5 already document a Legendary Attunement Core — a specific one-off consumable item (crafted from Resonance Shards + a Nether Star + a Diamond Block) that unlocks Personal Affinity Slot #5. It's unclear whether that item is meant to be a special/endgame member of this same Attunement Core family, or just an unrelated item that happens to share the name. Flagging rather than assuming either way.
Epistemic Caveat on This Document (added later, Part 10)
Everything in this part is drawn from the JavaScript example document, which is technical exploration and drafting — not a record of what was actually built or verified to work. Code appearing here is evidence someone thought through an approach, not proof it was implemented, tested, or functioning. Treat every implementation detail in this document as a design direction to prototype and verify, not a working foundation to build directly on top of.
Reconstruction note
With this note, all four uploaded documents (pt1–pt3 and the JavaScript example) have had an initial TriSweep pass. Remaining work: the Interaction Archive and Supplemental Archive, which draw on casual/contextual material and anything that didn't cleanly fit Master or Authorities — largely material not yet separately extracted from pt1–pt3, since this pass focused on locating and organizing Master/Authorities-relevant content first.
Approach | Best for | Relative difficulty
Aspect-based | Different Pokémon/forms having different Affinities (form-level granularity) | Low
Custom data/component | Every individual Pokémon having its own Affinity (individual-level granularity — the recommended approach above) | Medium
Dedicated mod/API system | Full Resonance mechanics — progression, abilities, reactions built on top of Affinity | High

# SOURCE: Master_Archive_Part6.docx
---

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Master Archive — Part 6: Resonance Processing, Poképower & Resonance Selection
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, under Free Reign Mode / TriSweep. Newly-swept material from pt3, not previously extracted: the Resonance Processing / Resonance Machine tech-progression system, Poképower, Drives (properly homed at last), the Apricorn Ingot latent-provenance material concept, C.E.R. Material Science as a Research category, and the Resonance Selection mechanic paired with Memory Phenomenon. Includes two F.R.M. terminology actions — see Section 4.
1. Resonance Processing & Resonance Machines
Official definitions, preserved close to as-written since both are marked Accepted status in the source material:
Resonance Processing — the refinement of material (items, fluids, etc.) via a Resonance Machine.
Resonance Machines — machines that produce refined outputs using Poképower as their primary energy source.
Poképower is explicitly not a real energy type (not RF, AE, MI, or any modded power unit) — it's a conceptual resource representing Pokémon voluntarily performing useful work, a deliberate play on “horsepower.” A Resonance Machine's UI reflects this: instead of a bar reading “RF: 2,000/10,000”, it reads something like “Processing: Awaiting Pokémon / Required: Fire-type move / Input: Apricorn / Cast: Apricorn Ingot Cast.” The Pokémon is literally the power source, supplying labor through using an appropriate move on/at the machine.
Move examples per Affinity (illustrative, not locked)
Concrete move examples recovered for what a Resonance Machine might recognize as valid processing input, by Affinity: Fire — Ember, Flame Charge, Flamethrower, Heat Wave, Overheat. Water — Water Gun, Scald, Hydro Pump. Electric — Thunder Shock, Thunderbolt, Discharge. Ice — Ice Beam, Blizzard. Psychic — Psybeam, Psychic. These support the already-locked “move quality affects yield” rule (Part 4, Section 13) with concrete examples of what a player might actually experiment with — not a finalized or exhaustive move-compatibility list for any Drive.
Imprinted vs. Assimilated machinery (proposal)
A proposed depth distinction for non-living Affinity Exposure applied specifically to machines (Part 9's Imprinting/Assimilation relationship): an Imprinted machine merely recognizes/remembers an Affinity (a Water-Imprinted Resonance Gem lets a machine recognize Water is present); an Assimilated machine actually operates differently BECAUSE of that Affinity — the Affinity has become part of its functional architecture, not just data it can read. This gives “upgrading” a machine two genuinely different depths to aim for, beyond just Resonant Transformation's Awakened Machines tiers (Section 6) — a machine could be both Awakened (transformed for a purpose) AND Assimilated (that purpose's Affinity is structurally part of how it runs), as two different, stackable dimensions rather than one linear tier ladder. Status: 🟡 Candidate.
2. Drives — Properly Homed
This resolves an open tension from earlier design passes: Drives were floated multiple times (Part 3's material families, Part 4's mod-architecture notes) but risked needing 18 variants to cover all types, which the project explicitly wanted to avoid. The Resonance Machine system gives Drives a cleaner job: a Drive defines what the machine is capable of doing, not which type it accepts.
SUPERSEDED, see Section 5a below for the final structure: an earlier pass in this archive locked a single flat set of 5 Drives (Thermal, Hydraulic, Cryogenic, Compression, Inductive). That flat structure has since been reorganized into two families of 4 — Genesis Drives (natural/ancestral) and Resonance Drives (man-made, modeled on Genesect) — with Compression Drive moving into the Genesis family, where it actually belongs thematically. Preserved here per the Archive Preservation Principle rather than deleted; do not treat the 5-Drive flat list as current.
Critically, a Drive does not supply the Poképower itself — it tells the machine how to interpret the Pokémon's labor. A Drive doesn't require a matching-type Pokémon specifically; it accepts appropriately-themed Poképower from whichever Pokémon and move fits. Architecture, worth keeping in its own words: Drive = machine capability; Poképower = labor/input energy; Pokémon move = method of supplying that labor; raw material = thing being processed; cast/form = desired output shape.
3. Machine Progression & Tech Identity
A two-stage unlock structure, deliberately built for Discovery Over Prescription — the player shouldn't start with a Swiss-army machine:
Resonance Machine — basic machine, limited processing capability, no Drive slots yet.
Resonance Interface — an upgrade/modification that unlocks Drive slots on an existing machine.
Resonance Machine + Drive — the specialized result, once a Drive is installed.
This reframes Tech progression's whole identity away from conventional “Furnace → Blast Furnace → Electric Furnace → Industrial Furnace” bigger-number tiers, and toward: Primitive Processing → Resonance Machine → Resonance Interface → Drive-based Specialization → Advanced Resonance Engineering. The story of the upgrade path is explicitly “we've learned how to cooperate with Pokémon to make technology work,” not “bigger machine number go brrrr” — phrasing preserved because it's a genuinely useful one-line design-intent check for anything added to this system later. Status: 🕒 Locked — both the core definitions and this progression were explicitly marked Accepted in the source material, not left as open brainstorming.
4. Terminology Notes (three F.R.M./correction actions)
A naming collision exists in the source material: an earlier, separate discussion in pt3 also used “Resonance Machine” as a working label for an unrelated Pokémon-selection mechanic (Section 5 below). That mechanic's own primary name in the transcript is actually “Gumball Machine” — “Resonance Machine” only appears there as a loose secondary header once. Since the Poképower/Drive system's identity depends heavily on the “Resonance Machine” name (it's load-bearing across the whole Tech progression in Section 3), F.R.M. action: this archive refers to the selection mechanic as the Resonance Selection going forward, reserving “Resonance Machine” exclusively for the Poképower processing system. Flagging clearly in case Wesley would rather resolve the collision a different way — easy to revert since it's a naming choice, not a mechanic change.
5. The Resonance Selection & Memory Phenomenon
Naming correction, second F.R.M. action for this part: Wesley checked and confirmed the mechanic below is actually called Resonance Selection (paired with Memory Phenomenon), not “Gumball Selector” — that was a working placeholder Claude used before Wesley verified the real term. Reconfirmed directly a second time (Sept 2026), after a ChatGPT reconciliation pass surfaced “Gumball Selector vs. Memory Echo” as still-current terminology on that parallel track: Wesley confirmed Gumball Selector and Memory Echo are the ORIGINAL, now-outdated terms; Resonance Selection and Memory Phenomenon are the current, up-to-date names. Per the Canonical Terminology Normalization Principle (Authorities Archive), the current names are used throughout this archive; the older names are preserved here purely as historical record.
Two related-but-distinct reward/encounter systems, explicitly designed as counterparts rather than competing options. Both are described as active candidates in the source (not yet fully locked), with Wesley noting a lean toward Memory Phenomenon as the preferred method by the end of the discussion.
Resonance Selection
Concerned with Pokémon specifically — “What Pokémon is resonating with this phenomenon?” A triggering event (e.g. a Water Resonance phenomenon) narrows a category/type pool, then presents exactly three candidate Pokémon drawn from that restricted pool (e.g. Feebas, Octillery, Wailmer for a Water trigger). The player chooses one. Randomness picks the three candidates; the player makes the final call — explicitly designed this way so rarity weighting can exist without the whole interaction feeling like gambling. Historical lineage confirmed via a follow-up sweep: Resonance Crate → randomized presentation → central token/currency → eligible pool determined by progression → small randomized selection → player chooses → Resonance Selection.
Memory Phenomenon
Concerned with everything else the world remembers — “What did this place/world/person/system remember?” An Echo resolves into several visible manifestations the player approaches and chooses between, rather than the game silently granting a reward. Example manifestation set given directly in the source: a Pokémon silhouette, a strange item, a memory fragment, a research phenomenon. Possible Memory Phenomenon outcomes: research discoveries, items, recipes, historical events, environmental phenomena, quest clues, lost technologies, faction information, rare resources, location clues, Virtue-related discoveries, and — very rarely — a Pokémon. That last case is deliberately rare specifically because it's the unexpected result, not the norm; the design intent is to keep the two systems from becoming competing reward boxes where players simply pick whichever gives better loot, by keeping their primary outputs different (Resonance Selection → Pokémon-oriented; Memory Phenomenon → discovery/research/world-oriented) while leaving the rare cross-over as a genuine surprise rather than a strategy.
One Central Echo Token (confirmed decision)
A specific decision recovered via a follow-up sweep: rather than a separate token per category (Shadow Token, Fusion Token, Fractured Token, etc.), the system uses ONE central Echo Token, with category/eligibility determined dynamically at the moment it's used rather than by which specialized token the player happens to hold. This keeps the system from exploding into a collection of specialized currencies. Flow confirmed: Echo Token → determine currently-eligible categories → randomly select a small presentation pool → display roughly 2–5 manifestations (3–4 as the normal expectation) → player chooses → that becomes the pursued Memory Phenomenon. Discovery-gating (Part 9) still applies — a category can only appear once the player has actually discovered it. Status: 🕒 Locked for the single-token decision.
Naming correction, third F.R.M./correction action for this part: this archive originally recorded this system's name as “Memory Echo.” A later Wesley research pass (see Part 9) confirmed the current terminology is Memory Phenomenon, with Memory Echo as an earlier stage in the same evolving concept (Resonance Crate → Resonance Selection/Memory Phenomenon; Memory Echo → Memory Phenomenon). Renamed throughout this part accordingly.
Status: 🟡 Candidate for both systems and the exact implementation split; 🟢 Strong reconstruction for “both systems should coexist as counterparts, not compete” as a design principle, and for the One Central Echo Token decision, which reads as settled even though other mechanics aren't locked.
5a. The Drive Family — Final 3-Tier Structure (Past/Present/Future)
The complete, final Drive lineage, confirmed by Wesley across several rounds of refinement. “Resonance Drives” is the umbrella term for the entire family, spanning three tiers across time — not two sub-families as an earlier draft of this archive had it. Genesect Drives are the literal, unmodified ancestor: Douse, Shock, Burn, Chill, exactly as they exist in Pokémon canon, not renamed or reinterpreted.
The Temporal Authority reversion mechanic is a deliberate small immersion touch: once a player has fully mastered the Genesect→Resonance progression for a given element, Time itself can show them what their own modern Drive used to be, before it was ever rebuilt. This is specifically why only the Present tier can revert — it has a real Past to revert to; the Future tier doesn't.
Discovery framing: Genesect Drives are uncovered through deep Resonance Processing research or literal archaeology, not handed to the player as an unlock. This keeps “the world remembers” intact (Genesect Drives genuinely did come before everything else) while the Temporal Authority path gives mastery of the Present tier its own distinct reward.
Present Tier — Resonance Drives
Future Tier — Genesis Drives
This also resolves the earlier “where did Electric go” confusion cleanly: Electric was never missing — it lives in both the Past tier (Shock) and the Present tier (Conduction), and Compression (Normal) simply belongs to the Future tier, where it always should have sat. Status: 🕒 Locked in full — the 3-tier Past/Present/Future structure, the Temporal Authority reversion mechanic, the Harmonic rename, and the Conduction Drive name are all confirmed. One open question remains, explicitly not resolved yet — see below.
Open question: what actually determines a Drive's Affinity? (Wesley, not yet decided)
Three genuinely different lore models were proposed for why each Drive pairs with the Affinity it does, and none has been chosen yet:
Model 1 — Fixed identity, flexible conduction: a Drive's Affinity isn't fixed to its function at all — a Conduction Drive conducts whatever Affinity happens to flow through it, and its “type” is just whichever Affinity it's currently channeling (a Conduction Drive that happens to conduct Ghost energy becomes, functionally, a Ghost-type Drive). This makes any Present-tier Drive theoretically able to become any of the 18 Affinities depending on what's fed into it.
Model 2 — Fixed Affinity, evolving purpose: each Drive keeps its original elemental Affinity permanently across all eras, but what it's USED FOR changes as “the needs of people” change over time — the same Fire Drive that once processed heat for cooking might later process heat for industrial smelting. This ties directly into the already-locked “move quality affects processing yield” rule (Part 4, Section 13) — the Drive's job evolves, not its identity.
Model 3 — Empty vessel, era-gated access: a Drive housing was always affinity-agnostic — what determines its function is purely which Affinity material gets installed into it. This opens a civilizational-technology angle: ancient/advanced past civilizations may have known how to harness rarer Affinities (Dragon, Fairy) that the present civilization has since forgotten and only relearned the basics of (Fire, Water), while a hypothetical future tier could regain access to all 18 through entirely different methods than the past ever used. This model would give Genesect Drives (Past) a plausible reason to include rarer Affinities eventually, not just the 4 confirmed ones.
Claude's honest lean, offered per Wesley's request: Model 2 is the strongest fit for what's already locked elsewhere in the archive — it requires no new mechanic (reuses the existing move-quality/Poképower system directly), it doesn't complicate the already-settled Affinity pairings above, and “the same tool, used differently across generations” is a very natural, low-overhead way to make the Past/Present/Future framing feel lived-in without adding new systems. Model 3's civilizational-tech-tier idea is the most narratively ambitious and could genuinely enrich the eventual war/disaster backstory (Part 15 candidate), but it's also the most work to design properly and would need its own dedicated pass rather than being folded in as a side effect of the Drive system. Model 1 is the most flexible but risks undermining the very Affinity pairings just locked above, since it makes every pairing provisional rather than fixed. Status: 🟠 Unresolved — genuinely Wesley's call, not decided here.
6. C.E.R. Material Science (Research category)
Accepted as an actual Research category (see Part 3, Section 4 for the base Research/Field Journal system this extends), initial scope: Apricorn material behavior and latent provenance, Resonance Processing, Poképower and Pokémon–machine interactions, type/material interactions, and discovery of latent material properties. Explicitly not locked as final in-game terminology — flagged as possibly needing to split into Material Science / Resonance Engineering / Pokémon Biology later, or possibly needing Material Science to become the broader discipline containing Resonance Engineering. Narrative progression given for the category's flavor: “We know how to make it” → “We know how it works” → “We know why it works” → “We discovered something it was doing that we didn't understand.”
Apricorn Ingots — latent provenance
The initial worked example for C.E.R. Material Science. Chain: Apricorn → Resonance Processing → Apricorn Ingot (retains its source Apricorn's origin/identity) → later systems interpret that origin. Deliberately not a simple color-variant system — the ingot doesn't announce its origin's significance immediately; a later Resonance process might react differently to an ingot depending on which Apricorn it came from, discovered rather than labeled upfront. Exact implementation (NBT/data component/other attachment method) explicitly left open as a build-time decision. Status: 🕒 Locked for the concept and its D.O.P.-aligned design intent; 🟡 Candidate for implementation mechanics.
Refinement recovered via the parallel full-sweep pass: move quality can affect processing yield — a weak, controlled, or overpowered move supplying the Poképower can produce different output quality or even failure/overheating risk, rather than any qualifying move working identically. Status: 🟡 Candidate, marked in that sweep as an accepted integration proposal rather than a firm mechanic yet.
White Apricorn — the base form (locked)
Confirmed and locked by Wesley: White Apricorn is the base/neutral form, and every colored Apricorn is an Affinity Adaptation of White Apricorn produced through Affinity Saturation exposure — a Living-subject Transformative Exposure outcome, in the exact vocabulary Part 9 already defines. This follows the same neutral-vessel-then-specialize pattern already established elsewhere in the project (Resonance Gem → Affinity Infusion → Affinity Gem, Part 3; Resonance Ball → Attunement → Affinity Ball, Part 8) — White Apricorn joins that same design language rather than being a one-off exception. Chosen over two alternatives — (a) pairing each individual base fruit with Saturation to directly produce a specific Apricorn color (Apple+Saturation=Red, Chorus Fruit+Saturation=Purple, etc.), and (b) researching Apricorns deeply enough to learn to convert other fruit into them — specifically because it's the only option that gives the whole Apricorn family one coherent origin story rather than several unrelated per-fruit recipes.
This meaningfully deepens Latent Provenance above: an Apricorn Ingot's retained “origin” isn't just “which color of Apricorn” as an arbitrary label — it's literally which Affinity Saturation a White Apricorn adapted under. A Red Apricorn Ingot's provenance is “White Apricorn, Fire-Saturated,” not just “Red.” That gives the whole Apricorn family a single coherent origin story instead of eight parallel, unrelated items. Status: 🕒 Locked.
Other fruit + Saturation → Berries, not more Apricorns (locked)
A natural discovery-shaped follow-up, confirmed by Wesley: once the player learns that Apricorns respond to Affinity Saturation, the obvious next experiment is trying the same process on other fruit, expecting more Apricorns. Instead, other fruit exposed to Affinity Saturation produces Berries — a related but genuinely different result, not a reskinned Apricorn. This is a clean D.O.P. moment (the player's reasonable hypothesis turns out to be half-right: Saturation does transform fruit, just into a different family depending on what you started with) and gives the already-existing Berry item family (Part 3's Berries/Mints/Coins Life-Ecology split) the same Affinity-Exposure origin story Apricorns just received, rather than leaving Berries thematically disconnected from the system. Status: 🕒 Locked for the concept; 🟡 Candidate for which specific fruit produces which specific Berry.
Farmer/Agriculture Path & AgriCraft (new proposal, Wesley)
A proposed addition to the Player Paths of Resonance (Part 10/14: Ranger, Researcher, Trainer, Artisan, Warden, Breeder) — Farmer/Agriculture, built around deliberately cultivating White Apricorn under controlled Affinity-saturated conditions (built greenhouses/fields/infusion setups) to reliably produce a desired colored Apricorn, rather than leaving color to chance. This turns Apricorn farming into a genuine C.O.C./D.O.P. activity: understand which Affinity produces which result, then build the environment that produces it on purpose.
Proposed mechanical foundation: AgriCraft (specifically the actively-maintained AgriCraft ReReloaded fork — confirmed real and current, with released builds for both NeoForge and Fabric 1.21.1, MIT-licensed, using a Mendelian genetics model: genes as allele pairs with dominant/recessive traits, cross-breeding to mutate new results). This maps cleanly onto White-Apricorn-to-colored-Apricorn adaptation — cross-breeding White Apricorn plants under different Affinity exposures, with dominant/recessive-style trait inheritance standing in for how strongly a given Affinity “takes.” A Create-compatibility addon for AgriCraft already exists on the same 1.21.1/NeoForge target (harvester automation, weed-mechanic integration), which would let this connect to the Industry pillar (Part 4) as well. Status: 🟡 Candidate for both the Farmer path and the AgriCraft mod choice — a strong, concretely-grounded proposal, not yet decided.
7. Resonant Transformation & Awakened Machines
A newly-recovered, fully-developed system found while chasing down Wesley's memory of “affinity/resonance transmutation” — it turns out Wesley's memory was more accurate than my first pass gave it credit for. “Resonant Transformation” is the mechanical/in-world name for this system (the crafting-table-into-workstation progression below), while Affinity Transmutation (Part 9's Affinity Exposure Taxonomy) is its correct formal classification: the non-living counterpart to Affinity Adaptation for TOTAL identity/function change, distinct from Affinity Attunement's form-preserving compatibility change. The two aren't competing names — Transmutation is the taxonomic category this whole system belongs under, the same relationship Resonance Ball has to Affinity Attunement. This directly solves a problem already on record elsewhere in this archive (the Mod Audit Anti-Bloat Policy's “I have six magic mods and now seventeen tables that look almost identical” complaint, Part 4).
The core concept
Rather than crafting a specialized apparatus from scratch, the player transforms an existing object into it: Crafting Table → perform an Arcane Transformation → Enchanting Apparatus. The physical object isn't destroyed and replaced by a conventional recipe — the player is teaching an existing object a new function. Framed narratively: the old civilization didn't necessarily build thousands of specialized machines; they transformed common tools according to purpose. A worked progression given directly: Crafting Table → (Arcane transformation) → Enchanting Apparatus → (Occult transformation) → Occult Altar → (Industrial transformation) → Assembly Station → (Research transformation) → Research Workbench — the humble crafting table becomes the literal seed of the entire creation system.
Three transformation types
Mod-specific transformation methods
Different mods provide different flavors of transformation, so the same base object can evolve in genuinely different directions depending on which system the player invests in: Technology (Create) — Assembly/engineering, build or retrofit machinery. Ars Nouveau — Enchant/infuse, change what an object can do. Occultism — Ritual transformation, bind a spiritual function to an object. Nature's Aura — Natural attunement, transform a machine into a living/ecological variant. Malum (if adopted, Part 4) — Spirit/soul infusion, give an object a metaphysical function.
Worked furnace example spanning all of them: Furnace (basic thermal processing) → Industrial Furnace (Create/engineering) → Arcane Furnace (Ars) → Spirit Furnace (Occult/Malum) → Resonance Furnace (late-game hybrid of technology + magic + Nature's Aura). These aren't just a pile of similar-looking blocks — they're stages of technological understanding.
Awakened Machines — tier terminology
The player isn't unlocking “Machine #47” — they're awakening capabilities in something they already have, giving a direct reason to upgrade existing infrastructure rather than replacing it outright and abandoning the old block in a chest.
The player should physically see it happen
Proposed as a real visual moment, not an instant menu-swap: the player places a Crafting Table on a pedestal, performs the appropriate ritual/process, and the table visibly floats, rotates, gains magical effects, changes texture, and becomes the new apparatus. Returning to the same structure later with a different ritual produces a different transformation — “the world is literally reawakening old tools,” directly reinforcing “the world remembers” (Part 9/10).
Ties to Player Paths and convergence
Two players starting with an identical Crafting Table can end up with completely different results depending on which path they pursue — the source material's own path names for this specific example (Hearth/Resonance/Forgotten/Wilds) read as early precursor naming for what's now the Player Paths of Resonance (Ranger/Researcher/Trainer/Artisan/Warden/Breeder/Farmer, Parts 10–11) rather than a separate system: Industrial Tempering → Engineering Workbench; Arcane Attunement → Arcane Workbench; Spirit Binding → Occult Workbench; Living Attunement → Natural Workbench.
Critically, early path choice isn't meant to permanently lock a player out of other systems: an Industrial Workbench that later gains Arcane knowledge and Natural attunement can converge into a Resonance Workbench — a hybrid capable of things none of the three original versions could do alone, representing synthesis earned through experience rather than a build the player picked once and is stuck with.
Applies to structures, not just individual machines
The same philosophy was proposed to scale up: a Ruined Forge can be restored into an ordinary Forge, then have industrial infrastructure added, following the same Dormant→Awakened→Resonant→Masterwork arc as a single workstation, just at building scale.
The nine-stage Creation Progression (confirmed via a follow-up sweep)
Resonant Transformation turns out to be one rung of a much larger creation ladder spanning the entire project's philosophy of making things, not a standalone crafting-table gimmick. Full progression: Handcraft (simple things by hand) → Workstations (specialized things) → Transformation (turn existing tools into specialized tools — this section) → Manufacturing (industrial production) → Automation (Create/AE2/RS orchestration) → Integration (technology + magic + ecology combined) → Replication (reconstruct known matter) → Synthesis (create things that don't naturally exist — Resonance Synthesis, Part 2) → Restoration Engine (manipulate the underlying systems of the world itself). Shorthand: Craft → Transform → Manufacture → Automate → Replicate → Synthesize → Restore.
🔵 Current proposal (Sept 2026): Replication Affinity Matter Framework. Replication (M.M.I.) supports fully custom KubeJS-registered matter types, not just its built-in eight (Earth/Organic/Living/Nether/Ender/Metallic/Precious/Quantum). Proposal: register all 18 World Affinities as their own genuine Replication matter types — Affinity becomes literal matter/energy the world's material system understands, not just a stat. The remaining 6 available custom-matter slots are deliberately RESERVED, not filled — candidate use: non-Affinity phenomenon/state matter answering 'what happened to this, not what it's connected to' (e.g. Corruption for Shadow research, Instability for Fractured research, Resonance, Memory, Life, Creation — none locked). Worked example of the payoff: Shadow Fire research could resolve to a real matter signature (Fire + Corruption) rather than needing a whole separate 'Shadow Research' currency invented from scratch — the same combinatorial logic could cover Fractured, Resonant, Ancient/Memory-based, and Fusion-adjacent research without each becoming its own bolted-on system. Status: concept sound, not implemented — needs verification that Replication's machines/UI/scanning actually handle arbitrary registered types correctly in practice, not just at registration.
The two-axis logic in full: Affinity matter answers 'which ecological/type force is this connected to?'; phenomenon matter answers 'what state or process is happening to/through it?' Worked examples beyond the Shadow Fire case above: Fractured Water Pokémon = Water + Instability; Resonant Psychic Pokémon = Psychic + Resonance; an ancient fossil discovery = Rock + Memory; living ecological restoration = Grass/Water/etc. + Life; artificial Resonance technology = relevant Affinity + Resonance + Creation. The pattern generalizes rather than needing a bespoke rule per phenomenon type.
Presentation idea worth preserving: the player likely never needs to see 'Replication matter' as an exposed game mechanic at all. Research could surface as a Research Signature readout instead — e.g. 'Research Signature: FIRE / CORRUPTION' or 'Research Signature: WATER / LIFE / RESONANCE' — with the actual matter-type registration staying pure implementation infrastructure underneath. Fits D.O.P. directly: the player reads a signature and investigates what it means, rather than managing an inventory of matter currencies.
Refinement to Affinity Essence's already-locked Catalyst role (Part 3), specific to this framework: rather than Essence being an arbitrary 19th/20th matter type invented to serve as Fusion's catalyst, it can be reframed as a CONCENTRATOR/container for the 18 Affinity matter types themselves — Fire Affinity Matter processed into Fire Essence, Water Affinity Matter into Water Essence, and so on. A Fusion catalyst input then doesn't need its own separate matter category; Fire+Flying could represent a composite Affinity state, while Fire+Flying+Resonance (if Resonance ends up one of the reserved slots) represents something categorically different — giving a physical material foundation for the distinction between ordinary Affinity, Affinity combinations, Resonance, and Synthesis outcomes, without inventing more unrelated materials.
The reserved-slot design constraint, worth preserving as a standing test for any future 6th-slot candidate (not just this one): a slot only gets assigned when the answer to 'what unique thing does this matter represent that cannot already be represented by an Affinity?' is genuinely clear — the same unique-verb discipline already governing the rest of the material family (Part 3), applied here to Replication's matter registry specifically. Candidate pool as discussed (still not locked, deliberately larger than 6 named ideas competing for 6 slots): Corruption, Instability, Resonance, Memory, Life, Creation, Discovery.
Important technical detail: an item does NOT need a single matter type — Replication's matter values are assigned per item and can combine multiple registered types with different weights on the same item. A hypothetical Resonance Essence, for instance, could be composed of something like 20 Affinity + 5 Living + 2 Resonance simultaneously, rather than needing to pick one exclusive category. That's what actually makes the two-axis Affinity+Phenomenon research grammar above mechanically real rather than just a naming scheme — a single specimen or material can carry a genuine composite signature. Confirmed current on the target stack: KubeJS 2101.x on NeoForge 1.21.1 supports this custom-registration approach directly, not a future-version assumption.
🔵 Current proposal: the Replication-as-research-instrument principle, distinct from Replication-as-resource-duplicator. Core reframe: Replication is a research instrument that helps the player understand, qualify, and eventually unlock legitimate reproduction methods — not a machine that turns one item into infinite items on contact. This is the actual justification for why owning a Type Gem doesn't mean manufacturing Type Gems forever — the player found it, studied it, determined its composition, understood its formation, and THEN qualified a replication pathway, preserving discovery rather than trivializing it.
Proposed knowledge-progression ladder (conceptual states, not necessarily literal UI labels): UNKNOWN → IDENTIFIED → STUDIED → CHARACTERIZED → REPLICATION POSSIBLE → REPLICATION STABLE → REPLICATION REFINED. The important point: what's actually leveling up across this ladder is the PLAYER'S understanding, not a machine tier — no 'Replicator Mk. II' power creep, just accumulated knowledge unlocking what the same machine is capable of.
Replication Fidelity, worth preserving as its own concept: a first successful replication attempt isn't guaranteed to be equivalent to the naturally-occurring original — purity, stability, Affinity concentration, Resonance signature, biological/ecological compatibility, and degradation can all vary, giving a real reason to keep researching something AFTER learning how to replicate it, rather than treating 'replication unlocked' as the end of that research thread.
Explicit scope boundaries, worth keeping precise given how many adjacent systems this touches: (1) Replication ≠ Resonance Synthesis — Replication asks 'can we reproduce something we understand,' Synthesis asks 'what happens when compatible Resonance signatures interact'; they can interact but are not the same process, and Fusion should never become 'put two Pokémon in a cloning machine.' (2) Failed replication should read as a replication failure, not automatically as Shadow/Fractured — those systems only get pulled in if the experiment genuinely involves Resonance or corruption, not by default. (3) Pokémon replication is explicitly a separate, much harder research frontier than material replication — a Pokémon carries biological identity, individual characteristics, Affinity, Resonance, and potentially memory that a material doesn't, which is a strong reason NOT to make generic Pokémon cloning the default mechanic.
🔵 Current proposal (Sept 2026): Blank Keystone → Full Resonance → Attunement → Megastone. Mega Evolution's Keystone stops being a crafted item and becomes something a player and Pokémon EARN together: a Blank Keystone attuned during a Pokémon's Full Resonance state permanently imprints that Pokémon's specific Resonance signature, becoming its Megastone. Reframes Mega Evolution from 'find rare ore, craft Megastone' into a relationship achievement — the same Blank Keystone could become Charizardite X or Y depending on which Charizard and what kind of Resonance relationship was actually built. Deliberately left open: whether every Full-Resonance Pokémon can attune (probably not — likely gated to Mega-capable species), whether different Resonance conditions determine X/Y-style variants, whether a specific location/apparatus/ritual is required. Ties naturally to the Reserved Matter proposal above if Resonance ends up as one of the 6 reserved Replication matter slots — the Megastone could then literally be Pokémon Affinity matter + Resonance matter, giving a physical explanation for why one Keystone becomes different stones rather than needing a separate 'Mega material' system.
Proposed ceremony sequence, worth preserving even though the exact apparatus/location requirement is still open: Pokémon enters Full Resonance → Blank Keystone placed in an Attunement apparatus/shrine → Pokémon is physically present → the system detects the Resonance → the Keystone visibly begins reacting → the Pokémon's signature imprints → the Keystone transforms into the appropriate Megastone. Confirmation flavor text worth keeping as a reference point for tone: 'RESONANCE STABILIZED. ATTUNEMENT COMPLETE.' — a visual/audio confirmation moment rather than an item silently swapping in the player's inventory.
This closes a real loop worth stating directly: the creation system ultimately feeds back into the ecological system — Pokémon → ecosystems → World Affinity → spawning/resources → player opportunities → Resonance → creation → further restoration. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction — a genuinely large unifying framework, confirmed independently rather than invented for this entry.
Technology and Magic: different philosophies, same origin
A thematic clarification worth preserving: Magic should feel like working with forces that already exist (nature, spirits, ancient civilizations, rituals, dimensional phenomena, dreams, supernatural forces); Technology should feel like reconstructing civilization (automation, electricity, machinery, transportation, laboratories, communications, processing, infrastructure). They aren't meant to stay opposed forever — the player eventually discovers they may have once been parts of the same civilization/system, which is exactly why a workstation can become Resonant rather than remaining permanently locked as “magic” or “tech.”
The abandoned-technology narrative beat
A proposed early-game emotional beat tying directly into the civilization-restoration premise: the player doesn't start with a functioning factory. They find an old machine — it doesn't work. Another — it has power. A third — it produces something never seen before. The realization: these machines were built to support restoration of the world. That reframes the player's first generator from “I need RF” into “I'm bringing an ancient system back online” — the technology progression becomes narrative, not just numbers.
The Modular Ball Workbench IS an Awakened Machine
A direct, valuable connection to Part 8's ball system: rather than building a second, separate custom-ball workstation, the existing Modular Ball Workbench (25 lids/16 colors/6 cores/live preview, Part 8 Section 3) should be integrated into this same Resonant Transformation progression rather than sitting outside it. Proposed arc: Early — discover/rebuild basic Poké Ball manufacturing → Mid — learn specialized ball construction → Late — Awaken the Workbench with technology, magic, Nature's Aura, faction knowledge, and Resonance. The player isn't unlocking “ball recipe #47” — they're relearning how humanity manufactured Poké Balls in the first place. Chain given directly: Apricorns → Processing → Workbench → (historical term “Resonance Core,” now Attunement Core per Part 8) → Treatment → Calibration → Automation.
Factions teach manufacturing knowledge, not just sell equipment
Confirms and sharpens Part 8's faction-ball associations (Verdant Circle/Iron Accord/Arcane Order/Umbral Covenant/Frontier Guild): faction reputation should unlock knowledge about what a workstation can become — manufacturing philosophies — rather than simply handing out finished equipment or discounts.
Research unlocks transformations (D.O.P. in practice)
A concrete discovery chain tying this system to the Field Journal (Part 3/9): “This material reacts to Resonance” (research entry) → “This machine can conduct Resonance” → “Workbench transformation discovered.” The player earns a transformation through observation and research rather than reading it off a recipe book — the same shape as Ball Research's progression in Part 8 (Environmental Capture → ... → Legendary Containment).
Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction for the whole system — fully reasoned through with worked examples throughout, and now confirmed twice independently; 🟡 Candidate for exact recipes/rituals/costs per transformation, and for whether the vanilla Crafting Table block itself is consumed or preserved through the process, which remain open implementation questions.
Reconstruction note
This part exists because a full transcript sweep specifically for “Resonance Processing” and “Resonance Machines” turned up substantial Accepted-status material that had not made it into Parts 1–5 — a useful reminder that the earlier five-part pass, while thorough, wasn't guaranteed exhaustive. If other named systems turn out to be similarly unswept, the same targeted-search approach is the fastest way to check.
Tier | Era | Drives | Nature/Obtainment
Genesect Drives | The Past | Douse (Water), Shock (Electric), Burn (Fire), Chill (Ice) | Two valid paths to the same item: (A) literal archaeology — dig them up via Research/Memory Phenomenon; or (B) master the matching Resonance Drive, then use Temporal Authority (once unlocked) to revert it backward in time to what it originally was
Resonance Drives | The Present | Thermal, Hydraulic, Cryogenic, Conduction | Engineered — built by modern humans studying the Genesect Drives to understand how the process works at all
Genesis Drives | The Future | Compression, Distillation, Harmonic, Transmutation | Pure invention — researched forward, no ancient counterpart exists, and specifically CANNOT be reverted via Temporal Authority because there's nothing in the past for them to revert to
Drive | Affinity | Function
Thermal Drive | Fire | Heat-based processing
Hydraulic Drive | Water | Fluid processing
Cryogenic Drive | Ice | Freezing/refinement
Conduction Drive | Electric | Power generation — named for electrical conduction, the real physical process (matching the other 3 Present-tier Drives' process-noun naming pattern), while the root word “conduct” still carries the dual electrical/command meaning Wesley wanted: “I touch energy, I save it, I move it, energy belongs to me.” Final name after weighing Conductor (personality, breaks the process-noun pattern) against Induction (pattern-consistent, less personality) — Conduction Drive gets both.
Drive | Affinity | Note
Compression Drive | Normal | No ancient counterpart — a genuinely new invention with nothing in the past to revert to
Distillation Drive | Poison | Fluid separation → purification/detoxification; ties to Poison's existing contamination/remediation association (Part 9). Flagship worked example (Wesley), now a complete two-way cycle: a normal Potato exposed to Poison Affinity Saturation becomes a Poisonous Potato (the same Affinity Exposure → Saturation → Affinity Adaptation pathway as White Apricorns, Section 6 below); running that Poisonous Potato back through a Distillation Drive extracts the toxin, restoring it to an ordinary Potato. Detoxification and contamination are the same mechanism running in opposite directions, not two unrelated item interactions.
Harmonic Drive | Psychic | F.R.M. rename, standing pre-authorized by Wesley: the original candidate name for this Drive was literally “Resonance Drive,” which directly collides with the umbrella family name. Renamed to Harmonic (resonant-frequency matching) to clear the conflict without losing the “Affinity/Resonance refinement” function or its Psychic pairing.
Transmutation Drive | Dragon | Advanced material conversion — Dragon's established role as the rare, high-order exception among Affinity relationships (Part 12)
Type | Meaning | Example
Conversion | One machine becomes another entirely — useful when two machines occupy the same physical role | Crafting Table → Enchanting Apparatus; Furnace → Arcane Furnace; Smithing Table → Specialized Forge
Augmentation | The original machine is retained and gains a capability, rather than being replaced — especially good for vanilla equipment | Furnace + Arcane Catalyst → Arcane Furnace (enhanced version of the same block)
Integration | Two systems combine into something genuinely new — a defining late-game feature | Create Mechanical Crafter + Ars Nouveau enchantment + Nature's Aura → Resonance Assembly System
Tier | Meaning
Dormant | An ordinary, untransformed workstation
Awakened | Has been transformed for a specialized purpose
Resonant | Has been integrated with the world's deeper systems
Masterwork | Endgame hybrid technology

# SOURCE: Master_Archive_Part7.docx

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Master Archive — Part 7: New Design Proposals (Domains & Nobles)
Compiled by Claude, September 2026, under Free Reign Mode. Unlike Parts 1–6, this part is NOT reconstructed from the transcripts — it's original design work, explicitly invited by Wesley to fill two gaps that were never fully developed in the source material: the unfinished Legendary Domains (Light, Night, Creation, Balance) and the Noble Pokémon ↔ Legendary Domain relationship. Every entry here is 🔵 Current proposal, Claude-originated, offered as a starting point for discussion — not a recovered decision. Treat this part as the most negotiable document in the whole archive.
1. The Light Domain (Solgaleo)
Solgaleo holds Fundamental Light/Solar Authority. Before recognition: solar-dependent tech caps out at a low ceiling no matter how it's upgraded (solar generators, light-based crafting, anything drawing on Ars Nouveau's or Create's light-adjacent mechanics), and certain crystal/prism structures in the world sit visibly dormant — present, inert, waiting. After recognition: DOMAIN UNLOCKED: LIGHT — solar infrastructure uncaps, light-based restoration tech becomes available (accelerated growth under artificial daylight, light-purification of corrupted zones), and dormant prism structures activate.
Necrozma sits as a specialized authority within Light rather than a separate domain — Consumed/Overcharged Light, governing unstable or weaponized light energy (dangerous, high-risk/high-reward tech: overcharge mechanics, prism-based weapons or amplifiers) as a deliberately riskier counterpart to Solgaleo's stable, foundational authority. Mirrors the Dialga/Celebi and Palkia/Hoopa broad-vs-specialized pattern already established for Time and Space in Part 2.
2. The Night/Dreams Domain (Lunala)
A structural fix as much as a new domain: Part 2 already placed Darkrai (Dreams/Nightmare) and Cresselia (Dreams/Moon, protective dreams) as floating specialized authorities with no fundamental holder above them. Lunala fills that gap naturally — Fundamental Night/Dream Authority, unifying what was already there rather than competing with it.
Before Lunala: night in C.E.R. is mechanically inert beyond normal darkness — no moonlit encounters, no dream-space anything, Darkrai/Cresselia content (however implemented) exists but doesn't connect to anything larger. After Lunala: DOMAIN UNLOCKED: NIGHT — the world's night cycle becomes an active second layer of play rather than just “when it's dark outside.”
3. The Creation Domain (Arceus)
Deliberately built to tie back into C.O.C. (Creation Over Crafting) rather than functioning like a normal domain gate. Arceus doesn't unlock a tech tree — it unlocks a capability: the authority to define new stable outcomes within systems that already exist, rather than only using outcomes the world/player has already discovered.
Concretely: before Arceus, Resonance Processing (Part 6), Resonance Synthesis/Fusion (Part 2), and Twisted-to-official transitions (Part 3) all operate on recipes and rules the player discovers, not ones they invent. After Arceus recognition, the player gains access to a capstone system — working title Authored Resonance — letting them propose and stabilize a genuinely new Fusion pairing, a new Resonance Processing recipe, or a new stable material variant that didn't previously exist in the world, subject to whatever balance/cost gating makes sense at implementation time. This is explicitly meant as the mechanical payoff for C.O.C. as a philosophy: the ultimate creation authority is earned, not crafted, and its price is understanding everything beneath it first.
This also gives Arceus a genuinely distinct identity from every other Legendary rather than just being “the strongest one” — its domain isn't a resource or a technology, it's authorship itself.
4. The Balance Domain (Zygarde)
This domain has a direct hook already sitting in Part 1: the hidden World Balance value (how unevenly the 18 World Affinities are distributed), explicitly floated as the true late-game objective instead of simply maxing all 18 to 100, tied to worldbuilding about why the ancient civilization removed Pokémon in the first place. Zygarde is the natural authority to gate that system.
Before Zygarde: World Balance exists and quietly affects the world (per Part 1) but is invisible to the player — no readout, no way to act on it directly, only individual Affinity numbers are visible. After Zygarde recognition: DOMAIN UNLOCKED: BALANCE — a Balance readout appears in the Restoration Observatory, and new tools become available to deliberately redistribute Affinity investment toward balance rather than toward maxing favorite types (e.g. a Redistribution ritual/device that trades surplus Affinity in an over-invested type for a boost to an under-invested one, at a meaningful cost).
Zygarde's own in-game gimmick (10% → Complete Forme) maps naturally onto this unlock: encountering Zygarde in its lesser Formes could be the “signs” stage of the Legendary-recognition flow from Part 2, with Complete Forme only appearing once the player has demonstrated enough actual balance (not just total Affinity) across their World Affinity spread — making Zygarde's transformation a genuine reflection of the player's ecological choices rather than a scripted cutscene.
5. Nobles ↔ Legendary Domains — A Proposed Synthesis
Update: Wesley separately verified this section with his own research after this part's first draft, and recovered real confirmed material — the synthesis below is being kept largely intact since it's compatible with what came back, but the recovered facts are now folded in and marked as such rather than presented as pure proposal.
Recovered: the spawn-rarity hierarchy
Nobles sat in an actual tiered hierarchy, recovered directly: Normal Pokémon → Rare Pokémon → Pseudo/very rare species → Noble Pokémon → Legendary Pokémon. Nobles required very high Affinity plus a special event; Legendaries required extremely high Affinity plus world conditions plus a dedicated quest/event — a real, meaningful gap between the two tiers, not a synonym relationship. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction.
Recovered: Alpha vs. Apex vs. Noble are three different distinctions
The archive already separately established Alpha as a biological/physical phenotype (an individual specimen being physically exceptional — see Part 1) and Apex as ecological significance, with Noble apparently sitting in similar territory to Apex but recovered as its own distinct concept. These three can stack rather than being mutually exclusive — a Noble Ninetales (ecologically significant guardian) is different from an Alpha Ninetales (physically exceptional specimen), and a Noble Alpha Ninetales (both at once) is explicitly supported by the architecture's general willingness to let significance layers intersect. Status: 🟢 Strong reconstruction for Alpha vs. Noble being distinct; ❓ the precise Apex/Noble boundary specifically wasn't fully resolved even in the recovery pass — flagged rather than asserted.
RESOLVED: Ecological Apex Pokémon is the umbrella (Wesley, Sept 2026)
Locked, finally answering the question that sat open above for most of this reconstruction: Ecological Apex Pokémon IS the umbrella classification — an individual Pokémon's ecological significance, expressed through one or more named origins/subfamilies rather than a flat list of unrelated “special boss” concepts. Confirmed subfamilies: Territorial, Ancient, Resonance-Saturated, Ecosystem Guardian, Distorted/Twisted, Shadow, and — the specific trigger for finally resolving this — Titan Pokémon (Part 4, Section 11a), whose two-stage vanilla-boss-then-Pokémon-battle mechanic is now locked as one Apex manifestation-class among several, not a standalone system.
This supersedes the “separate but stackable, not unified” leaning noted below — that evidence isn't wrong, it's just describing a different layer. Alpha remains its own phenotype axis, entirely separate from Apex (Alpha ≠ Apex was always correct and stays correct). What changes is that Apex's various origins (Territorial/Ancient/Resonance-Saturated/Guardian/Distorted/Shadow/Titan) are now confirmed to sit UNDER the Apex umbrella as named subfamilies, rather than being independent concepts that merely happen to co-occur with Apex. A Pokémon can still be, for example, a Resonance-Saturated Alpha Apex — Alpha (phenotype) + Apex (ecological significance, expressed via its Resonance-Saturated origin) — the stacking behavior described below remains accurate, it's just now understood as “which Apex subfamily, plus which separate phenotype/condition axes,” not “six unrelated labels.”
Historical note, preserved rather than deleted per the Archive Preservation Principle:
An unresolved question sitting in the transcripts for most of this reconstruction, worth preserving as the record of how this got settled: whether “Ecological Apex Pokémon” should replace “boss Pokémon” wholesale as a single unifying category, covering candidates like ancient Alpha, territorial apex predator, unusually old Pokémon, Resonance-saturated specimen, ecosystem guardian, Twisted alpha, and Shadow aberrant specimen — or whether these should stay as separate, distinct concepts. The original question, never answered for a long time: “how do they differ? Right now it just seems like they are all just 'special boss' Pokémon — can they be integrated as one, or rather, should they be?”
New evidence that once leaned toward “separate but stackable” rather than “unify into one category”: a sweep on Affinity Exposure (Part 9) confirmed Resonance-Saturated as its own trait arising specifically from Affinity Saturation, explicitly compatible with combinations like “Resonance-Saturated Alpha” or “Twisted Alpha” or “Territorial Apex” — with an explicit caution that the player shouldn't see six simultaneous labels stacked on one Pokémon at once, and the key framing that Exposure is a cause/process, while Alpha/Apex/Twisted/Shadow/etc. are resulting classifications from it. That evidence is preserved above as still relevant to how subfamilies and phenotype axes interact — it just no longer argues against the umbrella itself.
Apex Encounter-Tier Hierarchy (graduated from Supplemental Archive)
Now that the umbrella question above is resolved, this proposal — previously held in Supplemental Archive's Provisional Thoughts pending exactly this resolution — has a real home: a content hierarchy for how elaborate an Apex encounter should be, so “Apex” doesn't collapse into one uniform difficulty spike. Exceptional Pokémon (a normal encounter, no special structure) → Apex Pokémon (a special but single-stage encounter) → Major Apex (a full boss-to-Pokémon two-stage encounter — this is where Titan Pokémon, Part 4 Section 11a, sits) → Guardian Apex (an environmental trial leading to recognition, potentially with no conventional battle at all, matching Part 12's principle that an Ecosystem Guardian may be better restored/recognized than fought) → Story Apex (a full multi-stage event, the rarest tier) → Legendary (out of scope for this hierarchy entirely — governed by the separate, already-locked Domain/Recognition architecture, Part 2/9).
This gives every Apex subfamily (Territorial/Ancient/Resonance-Saturated/Guardian/Distorted/Shadow/Titan) a way to express itself at different intensities rather than each subfamily needing its own separate difficulty system. Status: 🟡 Candidate — a strong, now-unblocked proposal, not yet locked in its own right.
Explicitly NOT recovered: a direct Noble→Domain Authority link
Important honesty check from the recovery itself: no preserved source statement says “Nobles grant Domain Authority” or otherwise directly mechanically links the two systems. What's recovered is that the two systems were independently developing into similar philosophical territory at around the same time, without an explicit bridge ever being written down. That's exactly the gap the proposal below tries to fill — it remains a proposal, not a recovery, even after this round of checking.
The proposed synthesis (unchanged in substance, now better-grounded)
Proposed distinction: Nobles and Legendaries are different tiers of the same underlying idea, not competitors for the same narrative role.
Under this framing, meeting a Noble becomes the earlier, smaller-scale version of the exact same emotional beat as meeting a Legendary — “the world is thanking you for restoring it, locally” before the much bigger “the world is fundamentally changing because of what you did, globally.” A player might meet several Nobles across different regions over the course of a playthrough before ever encountering their first true Domain-holding Legendary, which would also help solve the pacing problem of Legendaries otherwise being purely late-game events — Nobles give the Legendary-adjacent fantasy an early- and mid-game presence too.
This would also finally explain why Nobles were floated so early in the original brainstorming and then never returned to: they may simply have been the right idea at the wrong layer of abstraction — proposed before the Domain system existed to give them a place underneath. Status: 🔵 Current proposal — this is new synthesis work, explicitly offered for you to accept, modify, or discard. If anything about the original Noble concept comes back to you, it slots in as evidence either for or against this framing rather than needing to overturn it entirely.
Pokémon | Authority | Governs
Lunala | Fundamental Night/Dream | Night-cycle mechanics, dream-space travel (a lightweight Ultra-Wormhole-style short-range teleport through dreams rather than Palkia's Space), moonlit-only content and structures
Darkrai | Nightmare (specialized) | Unconscious/sleep manipulation, nightmare-based hazards and rewards — unchanged from Part 2, now properly placed under a fundamental authority
Cresselia | Protective Dream (specialized) | Dream protection, positive dream mechanics, countering Darkrai's effects — unchanged from Part 2, now properly placed
 | Nobles | Legendaries (Domain holders)
Scope | Local/regional — tied to a specific biome or area's World Affinity | Cosmic/global — tied to a whole Domain of reality
Identity | Unnamed or loosely-named — “the spirit of this restored place,” potentially several per type across different regions | Individually named, unique, one per Aspect within a Domain (per Part 2's Domain/Aspect/Authority structure)
Trigger | A single region's Affinity crossing a high local threshold (per Part 1's original 750/900/1000 tiers) | World Affinity plus Personal Affinity plus Hearts plus Virtues plus location/research/events (per Part 2's eligibility → recognition flow)
What defeating/appeasing grants | Local affinity stabilization for that region only (per Part 1's original concept, unchanged) | Full Domain Authority — world-changing, permanent, affects everyone (per Part 2)
