This single mod resolves several 'exact name TBD' placeholders at once.
Cobblemon Pokenav | (confirmed real via Smartphone's addon list — direct source pending) | Standalone PokéNav-style mod; integrates directly with Cobblemon Smartphone as an addon.
CobbleDollars | (confirmed real via Smartphone's addon list and CobbleSafari's economy — direct source pending) | Currency/shop system; already referenced as CobbleSafari's entry-fee economy, now confirmed as its own mod with a Smartphone addon.
Waystones | (well-known general MC mod, NeoForge 1.21.1 support confirmed) | Fast-travel network; confirmed compatible with Cobblemon Smartphone as an addon.
Cobblemon: Shadowed Hearts | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-shadowed-hearts | Major find: authentic Shadow Pokémon mechanics from Pokémon Colosseum/XD (Shadow Moves, Snag Machines, Purification, Shadowfall Meteoroids that convert nearby Pokémon to Shadow). Directly relevant to C.E.R.'s already-locked Shadow/Resonant Restoration system (Part 2) — the MECHANIC is a strong technical foundation; the GameCube-specific flavor/naming would need C.E.R.'s own Resonance-flavored reskinning per the reference≠reuse principle.
Cobblemon: Simple Pokecenter | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-simple-pokecenters | Adds Poké Centers with healing machine + PC as a worldgen structure; Sophisticated Backpacks integration (PC Upgrade, Smart Heal Upgrade, Campfire Pot Upgrade).
Cobblemon Special Fossils | modrinth.com/project/spYV8RLZ | Fossil variants guaranteeing shiny/max IV/hidden ability, signaled by visible 'sparks' on the item. Ties directly to the already-locked Resonance Fossil system (Part 3, Section 1b). Includes a Research Disc item applying the same properties to artificial Pokémon (Mewtwo, Type: Null).
Cobblemon Outbreaks / Cobblemon Mass Outbreaks | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-outbreaks (Raguto, S/V-style); curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-mass-outbreaks (scouter567, Legends Arceus-style, larger download base) | Major find: two real, distinct Outbreak mods, both biome-aware with wave-based encounters and progressive shiny odds. Strong technical candidate as the actual mechanical engine for the already-locked Affinity Outbreak system (Part 3, Section 1a) — real infrastructure already exists for exactly this concept.
Cobblemon PokeWarden | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-pokewarden | Major find: replaces vanilla Warden spawns with a configurable boss Pokémon (default Giratina in Ancient Cities, Exploud in Overworld), uncatchable, aggressive, high-level, real battle-system integration. This is a working technical implementation of exactly the Warden Titan Pokémon encounter already locked (Part 4, CLR-0044) — directly actionable, not just thematically similar.
Cobblemon: Ride On! | (needs current 1.21.1 NeoForge re-verification) | CONFIRMED KEEP (Wesley, Sept 2026, correcting an earlier redundancy flag made before native riding's actual scope was known). Native Cobblemon 1.8.0 riding covers a curated list of only 10 species — Ride On! provides broader, datapack-configurable rideability beyond that native list. Direction confirmed: keep Ride On!, and deliberately CUSTOMIZE which additional species C.E.R. designates as rideable (via Ride On!'s own datapack config) rather than accepting whatever a default config provides — an intentional curation decision, not just installing the mod as-is. Exact species roster not yet chosen.
APS Trophies | modrinth.com/mod/aps-trophies | RESOLVED — the previously-unidentified name. 60+ Cobblemon-themed trophies for tournaments/PvE rewards/races/achievements. Strong fit for Championship Protocol (Part 2) display rewards.
CobbleNav | (curseforge, source pending re-verification) | Confirmed real, current (2.4.1): PokéNav-style interface for spawn checking, stats, battle info. Genuine overlap risk with Pokédex/Better Pokedex Scanner/Smartphone — flagged for a later overlap audit rather than assumed additive.
Cobblemon: Pokestop | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-pokestop | Confirmed real but explicitly labeled beta by its own author (rough textures/animations). Functional PokeStops dispensing Poke Balls on a cooldown. Concept is good; implementation quality needs a look before adopting.
Cobblemon Villager Overhaul | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-villager-overhaul | Changes villager trading to use CobbleDollars instead of emeralds. Ties into the same economy cluster as CobbleDollars/CobbleSafari.
Cobblemon Home | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-home | Major find with a real caution attached: effectively unlimited, cross-world/server Pokémon storage with Living Dex tracking. Genuine tension flagged — this could undermine the scarcity/permanence philosophy already locked elsewhere (Occupied Pokéballs, Retirement Protocol, Championship Protocol all depend on storage/loss having real weight). NOT an automatic inclusion — needs a system-wide compatibility read before adopting, not just a feature checklist.
Cobblemon Secret Base Trainer | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-secret-base-trainer | Configurable battle-clone NPCs built from the player's own teams — usable as gym leaders, repeatable challenges, or story encounters. Relevant to the same Cap C trainer-architecture cluster as RCT/Fierce Competition (Part 10).
Cobblemon Trainer Battle Commands (TBCS) | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/tbcs | Real find for the flagged highest-priority NPC/faction gap: a decoupled command/interface layer (by the same author as RCT) for managing trainers and starting battles, explicitly designed to interface with other NPC mods (e.g. Easy NPC). Lets C.E.R. build ENTIRELY ORIGINAL trainer NPCs on a solid battle backend, without needing RCT's imported 1,500-trainer roster.
Easy NPC | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/easy-npc | Major find (7M+ downloads, actively maintained): full dialog system, custom models/textures, and a genuine FACTION SYSTEM (per-NPC faction assignment, scoreboard team sync, hostile-faction target objectives) added recently — a real technical match for the already-designed faction/reputation system (Part 10), which has had no backbone until now. Also has gender/shiny model variants specifically searchable for Cobblemon NPCs. Combined with TBCS, this covers the trainer-battle AND dialog/faction halves of the flagged NPC infrastructure gap.
Cobblemon: Vocalized | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-vocalized | Voice-command your Pokémon in battle. Real compatibility caution: the verified release explicitly targets Cobblemon 1.7.0–1.7.3 despite having 1.21.1 builds — 1.8 compatibility unverified, hold before adopting. THEMATIC COHERENCE FLAG, found on Full Sweep: a genuinely novel technical feature, but it has no connection to Resonance/Affinity/ecology/restoration — a pure convenience/novelty layer, unlike most of this list which ties into at least one C.E.R. system. Not a reason to reject it, but worth naming as the clearest example of a ‘cool mod’ candidate rather than a thematically-anchored one — the kind of addition to watch for so the list doesn't quietly drift.
Cobblemon: Stamina Reload | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-stamina-reload | Changes mount stamina so Pokémon fullness contributes to a stamina reserve — ties Pokémon care to riding/travel logistics. 1.8 compatibility unverified.
Cobblemon Randomizer | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-randomizer | Randomizes starters/wild species/types/abilities/movepools/evolutions. Doesn't fit normal C.E.R. progression — candidate for an optional Challenge/Replay Mode rather than the base experience.
Cobblemon - Fat Pikachu and friends | modrinth.com/datapack/cobblemon-fat-pikachu-and-friends | Fun/fanmade Pikachu variant datapack. Low priority, reference-only per reference≠reuse for the specific designs.
Torterra Cosmetic Trees | modrinth.com/datapack/torterra-cosmetic-trees | Another strong example for Part 9 Section 12a's Cosmetic Slot pattern — Torterra swaps its back-tree cosmetic using saplings, same species-appropriate-trigger logic as Comfey/Honedge.
Cobblemon Raid Dens Addon — Raid Den Designs | modrinth.com/datapack/cobblemonraiddens-typedesigns | Type-themed raid den structures (Water/Dark-Ghost/Grass/Electric/Fire/Fighting/Ice), with an automation addon assigning designs by boss type automatically. Genuine tie-in potential to Affinity-themed sacred/hostile sites.
Cobblemon Parts / Parts Refabricated | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-parts-refabricated (Fabric port) | Poke Ball lid/base crafting components, hammers, End ore, Master/Origin Ball infrastructure, an End structure with a Masterwork blueprint. Genuine loader split confirmed — original + a Fabric-specific Refabricated port. Relevant to the existing Ball system work (Part 5/8).
Cobblemon Expeditions | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-expeditions | MAJOR FIND: send Pokémon on timed expeditions — 5 board tiers, 11 expedition types (forests/shorelines/caves/berry groves/volcanic surveys/deep-sea dives/frozen ruins/a Distortion Rift/boss expeditions), Team Expedition Power, IV/EV/type/hazard calculations, ranks, upgradeable boards, datapack-driven expeditions. Extremely well-matched to the already-locked Researcher/Ranger paths (Part 10/11) — a real background-research system already exists. The 'Distortion Rift' expedition type also ties directly to Displacement lore.
[Cobblemon] Storybook Hybrids! | modrinth.com/datapack/storybook-hybrids | Fantasy/mythological hybrid Pokémon (Gorgon, Mermaid, Pixie, Jackalope-inspired) created via the Fossil Machine from unusual ingredient combos. Cobblemon Fossil Hybrids! | modrinth.com/datapack/cobblemon-fossil-hybrids%21 | Creates hybrid Pokémon by combining fossils in the Fossil Machine (Amaura+Lileep-style combos). Same overlap caution as Storybook Hybrids — needs differentiation from Resonance Fusion before any adoption.
Cobblemon Ultra Wormholes | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-ultra-wormholes | MAJOR FIND: timed Ultra Wormhole raid events — an Ultra Beast becomes a shared-HP-pool boss, contributors get rewards, Beast Ball capture attempt after victory. Cobblemon Fence | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-fence | Invisible fence blocks that stop only Pokémon (no normal collision), toggleable via detector. Strong habitat/conservation infrastructure — ties into the CobbleSafari/conservation cluster.

Extends the existing Mega Showdown+Navas+Cosmetic Forms cluster.
Cobblemon: Research Tasks | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-research-tasks | CONFIRMED by Wesley as the correct project. 6,000+ research tasks across all 1,025 Pokémon (5–8 tasks each), a Research Book, configurable tasks/rewards, 10 completion tiers. Currently Fabric-only despite being 1.21.1 — compatibility hold for the NeoForge baseline. Since Research is already a core C.E.R. pillar (Part 3) without a full mechanical engine, this is a strong technical candidate worth a real comparison pass.
Haunted Encounters | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/haunted-encounters | CONFIRMED as ‘Haunter Encounter.’ Much more substantial than the name suggests: hostile/neutral ghost entities inspired by the Pokémon Tower ghosts, full-moon behavior, distinct ghost personalities/attacks, Cleanse Tags, and a Silph Scope mechanic revealing true forms that can lead into Pokémon encounters. NeoForge + Fabric 1.21.1. Genuinely strong fit for C.E.R.'s Shadow/sick-world ecology (Part 2) rather than being a novelty mod.

Cobblemon Ultra Space | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-ultra-space | BRAND NEW discovery (not originally requested, found incidentally, updated Sept 11 2026): an alien dimension reached through Ultra Wormholes — Ultra Scanner with directional wormhole detection, research stations, ruined observatories, crashed prisms. Strong technical candidate for the World-Level Scaling gap flagged in Part 10 — its Region/Biome/Dimension min/max-level config gives a real hook for World Affinity/Restoration state to drive wild level ranges, and the exclusion list can carve Legendaries out entirely, keeping them under the existing Eligibility/Recognition system rather than this mod's math.
Radical Cobblemon Trainers (RCT) | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/rctmod | Real, verified mod (8.7M+ downloads) implementing a working Gym/League-style level-cap progression — Cap C in Part 10's three-cap framework: level cap rises only by defeating specific key trainers in required order, over-cap Pokémon can't gain EXP, trainers refuse over-cap challengers, a Trainer Card tracks progress. The CAP MECHANISM is the useful part; RCT's 1,500+ imported trainers (Radical Red/Unbound/BDSP) are explicitly NOT proposed for reuse, per the reference≠reuse principle (Authorities Archive) — C.E.R. would need its own original trainer roster.
CobbleSafari | modrinth.com/mod/cobblesafari | Real, active mod — 18-biome Safari dimension (Safari Ball-only, fleeing Pokémon, timed access), wild egg nests + incubators, tiered spawn-boost consumables, and a Sinnoh-Underground-style minigame layer with a separate real-time boss-battle system. Strong direct candidate for the Safari Zone gap (Part 10) whenever that's revisited — the mod itself is a dependency to use, not creative content to imitate, so the reference≠reuse concern doesn't apply the way it does to trainer rosters/questlines.
Cobblemon Buffs | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-buffs | CONFIRMED by Wesley as the mod originally meant by 'Pokemon Buffs.' Whole-party passive buff system: each type in your party grants a themed buff, scaling through 3 tiers via a combined level+friendship ‘Power’ score. Structurally close to Personal/Trainer Affinity (Part 2) already being conceptual without a mechanical engine — proposed as that engine, reframed as Affinity's passive expression rather than a separate buff system.
Pokebuffs | (source pending re-verification) | Simpler alternative: only the first party slot grants a buff, scaled by that Pokémon's rarity. Weaker fit — closer to Cobblemon's existing native shoulder-mount buffs than to anything not already covered.
Pokopia Cosmetic Forms | curseforge.com/minecraft/data-packs/pokopia | Named cosmetic forms (Professor Tangrowth, Trainer Ditto, etc.) via Cobblemon's native Cosmetic Slot. Reference-only for the pattern — not proposed for direct reuse.
Cosmetic Forms (Lvnatic) | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cosmetic-megas | Mega Stone/Max Soup in the cosmetic slot permanently shows the Mega/Gmax look, cosmetic-only, gimmick stays functional. Good precedent for ‘cosmetic result coexists with unrelated mechanic.’
CobblemonMoreCosmetics / Cobblemon: Cosmetic Expansion | (sources pending re-verification) | Broader cosmetic-item addon packs on the same native slot system — reference for breadth of what's technically possible.
Cobblemon: Comfey Cosmetics | modrinth.com/datapack/cobblemon-comfey-cosmetics | Single-species datapack: give Comfey a vanilla flower, its texture changes to match — tied to Comfey's own canonical flower-holding identity. Best example found of species-appropriate (not arbitrary) cosmetic triggers. COMPATIBILITY CAUTION (found on cross-check): listed compatibility target is Minecraft 24w12a (an old snapshot), NOT confirmed against the 1.21.1/Cobblemon 1.8 baseline — the pattern is sound, this specific pack needs a compatibility check before use.
Cobblemon Chimeric Shinies | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-chimeric-shinies | Chimeric shiny variants for multi-component Pokémon (Diglett/Magnemite family, etc.) — only some body components mutate shiny rather than a uniform recolor. Already cross-referenced by Special Fossils (M.M.I.) as an optional integration.
IV Scanner | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/iv-scanner | RESOLVED — the correct project behind ‘IV Checker.’ Handheld device for locating/evaluating nearby wild Pokémon's IV quality using ratings/colors rather than raw numbers; ignores your own party/pasture Pokémon so you never scan what you already own. COMPATIBILITY HOLD: current file is tagged Cobblemon 1.7.3, not verified against 1.8.0.
Cobblemon Analyzer | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-analyzer | Wild-Pokémon stat-checking item chain: a rare ‘Bygone Tablet’ restores into a ‘Genome Analyzer,’ charge-scaled by Pokédex-caught progress, revealing IVs/gender/ability/nature. A cheaper ‘Survey Lens’ gives a partial preview before the full item is obtained. COMPATIBILITY HOLD: explicitly built for Cobblemon 1.6.1–1.7.3, not verified against 1.8.0. DESIGN QUESTION raised alongside IV Scanner: should raw genetic info be immediately available, or should Research progressively reveal it? The latter fits the already-locked Discovery Over Prescription principle (Authorities Archive) better — worth deciding before adopting either, not defaulting to whichever installs first.
[Cobblemon] Pet a poke | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-pet-a-poke | Confirmed real: shift+right-click petting option, small friendship boost (capped to prevent infinite farming). A similar alternative also exists — Pet Your Cobblemon (purely cosmetic, no friendship effect, by design).
Cobblemon Distortion World | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-distortion-world | Substantial find: a Spear Pillar structure generates in mountain/plains biomes; interact with an NPC named Cyrus to enter a full Distortion World dimension with its own blocks/crafting (Distorted Stone/Leaf portal crafting) and a Glizzious Orb that transforms Giratina into ‘Glizzatina.’ Directly relevant to C.E.R.'s existing Giratina/Distortion Domain lore — real working infrastructure for that thread.
Cobblemon Paleontology | modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-paleontology | New Villager Profession (Paleontologist) offering an easier path to rare fossil Pokémon than manual excavation, plus Concretion Processing (a lab table converting fossil-structure concretions into random fossils/crystals/bone items). COMPATIBILITY HOLD: 0.6+ branch explicitly tagged Cobblemon 1.7.3, not verified against 1.8.0.
Cobblemon Thufizer | modrinth.com/datapack/cobblemon-thufizer | CAUTION, distinct from the usual reference≠reuse note: this is a direct recreation of a specific NAMED character from the licensed Pokémon Adventures manga (a Zapdos/Moltres/Articuno fusion), not just another creator's original Fakemon design. Carries real copyright exposure beyond the usual originality concern — recommend skipping entirely rather than adapting, even loosely.
Cobblemon Journeys | curseforge.com/minecraft/modpacks/cobblemon-journeys | Confirmed as a full modpack (Zeo17), not a standalone system mod — QoL/exploration/building mods bundled around Cobblemon. HISTORICAL REFERENCE ONLY: actually an old 1.20.1 Forge modpack, last updated Feb 2024 — not currently usable, kept per Archive Preservation Principle in case ideas within it are worth recovering.
Cobblemon: CobbleStats | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-cobblestats | RESOLVED — the correct project behind ‘Cobble Stats.’ Client-side QoL: Showdown-like stat/weather/terrain/hazard tracking in the battle UI. Confirmed compatible with Radical Cobblemon Trainers (already logged). COMPATIBILITY HOLD: despite having 1.21.1 Fabric+NeoForge files, its own compatibility section still lists Cobblemon 1.6.1/1.7.0 — do NOT treat as confirmed 1.8.0-compatible.
Cobblemon Battle Tower | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-battle-tower | NEW, confirmed real and richly developed (1.1M+ downloads, actively maintained through Apr 2026, 1.21.1 NeoForge+Fabric): the ‘Holo Battle Tower’ you asked about is a craftable block, not a worldgen structure. Singles/Doubles/Co-op Doubles/Boss modes, progressive floors, Battle Points shop, NPC gimmick toggles (Tera/Dynamax/Mega), legendary restrictions, custom trainer skins, Boss Mode culminating in two-phase Gigantamax legendary encounters. Depends on RCTApi (a separate, independent, lightweight library) — no conflict either way now that RCT itself is confirmed rather than dropped. Shares its RCTApi foundation with both RCT and TBCS, meaning all three coexist cleanly on the same underlying API.
Double/Triple/Multi-battles — native Cobblemon feature, not a separate mod | (Cobblemon core, 1.6.0+) | Confirmed native to Cobblemon itself since the Dec 2024 ‘Record Catch Update’ — not something requiring an add-on mod for the feature itself. The real question is DEFAULT CONFIGURATION, which the two entries below address.
Cobblemon Trainer Doubles | modrinth.com/project/yc3PNfEX | NEW: a small datapack applying Doubles Format to all trainers in the RCT roster by default. Directly usable now that RCT is confirmed rather than dropped — no dependency conflict.
Cobblemon QoL | modrinth.com/project/uFSbNRFr | NEW: client-side fixes specifically for double/triple/multi-battle formats — gimmick move-targeting fixes in multi battles, a Tera Type icon overlay on Pokémon portraits. No RCT dependency, useful regardless of the trainer-architecture decision if multi-battles become more common in general play.
Cobbleloots: Loot Balls and More! | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobbleloots | RESOLVED — correct project behind ‘Cobbled Loots.’ Loot Balls found in-world, via fishing (Cobblemon Poke Rods), or spawning over time near players; tiered rarity (Common→Ultra Rare); custom Loot Ball Data system for map-makers. PER-PLAYER DISCOVERY CONFIRMED (Wesley, verified Sept 2026): setting a loot ball's uses to -1 (infinite) makes it per-player as a direct consequence — each player gets their own independent loot from the same ball. A separate randomizeAfterUse config (defaults true) controls whether each player's independent pull is randomized differently or identical across players, plus optional per-player cooldown timers. Resolves this mod's half of the previously-flagged Per-Player Discovery Loot verification gap.
Lootr | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/lootr | NEW, confirmed via the Per-Player Discovery verification — massively established (137M+ downloads on this listing alone, actively maintained, current 1.21.1 NeoForge builds). Instances all loot chests/barrels/shulkers per player automatically, visually unique per player. Resolves the container-loot half of the Per-Player Discovery Loot rule (Authorities Archive) — Cobbleloots handles Poke Ball-style loot, Lootr handles everything container-based (ruins, structures, dungeons, fossil sites). A companion mod, Shared Lootr, was also found — explicitly reverses Lootr's default per-player behavior for specific containers that should stay genuinely communal (e.g. a Championship Hall display or community-restoration reward), worth keeping in mind for the rare cases where shared-not-per-player is actually the right call.
CobbleThemes | modrinth.com/mod/cobblethemes | RESOLVED — correct project behind ‘Cobble Theme.’ Adds battle music themes controlled by which species the player is facing. COMPATIBILITY NOTE: confirmed 1.21.1 but Fabric-only and client-side — watchlist for a possible NeoForge port/Connector, not foundation-ready.

Massive dungeon structures inspired by iconic Pokémon game/anime/movie scenes (explicitly self-described as ‘similar to trial chambers’), map-based progression culminating in a high-level Legendary/Mythical encounter. CAUTION: recreates specific licensed scenes/landmarks — reference-only per reference≠reuse, lighter than Thufizer's concern but still real. UPDATE: the same author has a newer project, Cobblemon: Explore Legendary Dungeons, with a NeoForge 1.21.1 build — though its listed release still targets Cobblemon 1.7.3, not confirmed against 1.8.0.
Cobblemon Trials Edition | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemon-trials-edition | RESOLVED as likely the actual ‘trial chambers mod.’ Replaces vanilla Mob/Trial Spawners with a customizable Cobblemon Trial Spawner (863K+ downloads). Adds a Trial Potion (brewed from Awkward Potion + Sculk) applying the Trial Omen effect. CORRECTION to an earlier speculation: the Trial Potion intensifies Trial Spawner difficulty/loot — it does NOT directly level up a Pokémon, despite earlier informal sourcing suggesting a leveling function.

Lotus Megas [Cobblemon] | modrinth.com/datapack/lotus-megas-cobblemon | RESOLVED (previously unconfirmed). 1.21.1 datapack+resourcepack, multiple custom Megas with custom abilities. Same Mega Showdown ecosystem cluster as Xadok's Megas — a Mega/form-conflict audit is needed before installing multiple Mega datapacks together, since they could compete over the same Pokémon/form IDs.
Fanmade Form Funfair | modrinth.com/project/yZpvb7wd | RESOLVED — correct project behind ‘Regionales Fanmade for Funs.’ Regional/convergent Pokémon forms based on various fan artists' designs. LICENSE CORRECTION: specifically CC-BY-NC-ND (No Derivatives, No Commercial use) — more restrictive than a simple permission note; redistribution/modification rights need real attention before use.
CobblemonStarlightFusion | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/cobblemonstarlightfusion | RESOLVED — correct project behind ‘Starlight Fusion.’ Gardevoir/Eevee-line fusion addon requiring Mega Showdown, uses a FusionSplicer item mechanic with named results (Sylvevoir, Umbrevoir) and real designed stats/abilities. CONFIRMED: has a genuine NeoForge 1.21.1 build tagged for Cobblemon 1.8.0 specifically. Still a mechanical research target only, NOT a definition of C.E.R.'s own Resonance Fusion (Part 2/9's split stays authoritative).
PokeBike | curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/pokebike | RESOLVED — correct project behind ‘Pokebikes.’ Rideable Pokémon-inspired bikes, animated model, decorative variants, optional durability system, wrench-based pickup. Cobblemon AND Pixelmon compatible.
Cobblemon Boosters | modrinth.com/project/W8QHSSjM | RESOLVED — recovered from the original reconstruction material with an honest caveat: the mod itself was confirmed part of the original project discussion, but exact configuration/selection details weren't recoverable, so none are assumed here. Server-side timed boosters: Catch (server-wide catch rate), Experience (server-wide EXP gain), Shiny (server-wide shiny rate), Spawn Bucket (rate boost for a specific spawn bucket). REFRAME (Wesley + ChatGPT, resolving the earlier design-tension flag): rather than a standing server setting, Boosters becomes C.E.R.'s system for TEMPORARY amplification specifically — the short-lived counterpart to the project's permanent systems. Slots cleanly into the existing stack: Affinity (long-term world/Pokémon/Trainer relationship) → Resonance (deeper interaction/recognition) → Resonance Aura (observable signature of that interaction) → Type Gems (crystallized Affinity) → Liquid Affinity (stored/transferable Affinity) → Resonance Hearts (Virtue/Domain-level progression) → Fusion/Synthesis (permanent/structural alteration) → Research (understands and records). Boosters fills the one gap in that list: TEMPORARILY INTENSIFYING an already-existing phenomenon, distinct from permanently altering it (Synthesis) or permanently progressing it (Hearts). Resolves the earlier D.O.P./power-creep concern by giving temporary amplification a legitimate bounded role rather than a flat always-on setting — still likely best tied to specific triggers (Restoration Festivals, Living World Events, Resonance phenomena) rather than a raw purchasable/craftable timer, though that specific gating isn't locked yet.
Catch Indicator [Cobblemon Addon] | modrinth.com/mod/catch-indicator | Found alongside Better Pokedex Scanner research: a genuine NeoForge 1.21.1-compatible alternative for caught/unseen Pokémon indication, where Better Pokedex Scanner itself remains Fabric-only. Already flagged historically as a candidate.
Ars Elixurum | Not yet sourced | Potion-research mechanic; 1.21.1/NeoForge compatibility unverified
Mod | Status | Reason
Legendary Monuments (JorgaoMC) | Rejected as functional foundation, Sept 2026 (CLR-0040) | Real, active mod (3.5M+ downloads via curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/legendary-monuments-cobblemon), but hardcodes one fixed structure per named Legendary — architecturally opposite to C.E.R.'s dynamic Virtue-Pool recognition system (Part 13). Its structures remain flagged as a possible future VISUAL asset source only — credit JorgaoMC (and named structure-builders Inazuma/Maxolotl) if ever reused, per Automatic Provenance Crediting.
System | Why it's custom | Cross-reference
Virtue Node / Resonance Site structures | Legendary Monuments evaluated and rejected as the functional base (see Under Review above) — no existing mod supports the dynamic Virtue-Pool recognition architecture. Locked as custom-built, Sept 2026 (CLR-0040). | Part 13, Virtue Resonance
Combo | Mods involved | What it actually does together
Early-game traversal loop | Towers of the Wild Modded + Hang Glider/Paragliders + Waystones | CONFIRMED as one designed loop, not three separate features: climbable towers spawn a glider in their loot chest every time, plus a Waystone for fast travel between them. Vantage/discovery \u2192 descent \u2192 fast-travel network, all from one climb. Directly usable as-is; could thematically reskin towers as Resonance Sites/Observatory-adjacent structures later without touching the mechanic.
Aquatic exploration loop | A water-fog-removal mod (e.g. Clear Water) + a swim-speed mod + Cobblemon: Ride On's water-riding support (already logged) | PROPOSED. Ocean exploration is typically murky, slow, and disorienting in most packs \u2014 stacked together, Water-Affinity regions become somewhere worth lingering rather than rushing through, which matters given how central Water is to the Affinity system.
Storage/collection QoL loop | Carry On (move filled chests/machines without emptying) + a quick-stash/sort mod | PROPOSED. Pairs directly with the already-locked Occupied Pok\u00e9balls/Championship Protocol display-room concept \u2014 if players are building actual trophy halls and archive rooms, not fighting inventory friction while doing it is worth more than it sounds.
Low-effort combat flow | Swing Through Grass + Carry On | PROPOSED. Neither exciting alone, but together they remove two of the most common minor frustrations (foliage eating attacks, needing to empty a chest before moving it) \u2014 a steady trickle of small annoyances stopped rather than one big one fixed.
Tier | Meaning | Examples
Direct | Actually changes ecological conditions | Farming, Pokémon populations, restoration projects, environmental actions
Indirect | Provides tools that enable Direct actions, doesn't itself move the needle | Create, Ars Nouveau, Occultism
Observational | Detects/documents state, doesn't change it | Cobblemon Snap, Research
Social | Changes access/opportunities, not ecological state directly | Factions, settlements
Audit needed | Why
The complete original mod list | Explicitly recorded elsewhere as lost/needing recovery from the earliest brainstorming — no matter how much gets recovered through ongoing research, this shouldn't be treated as reconstructing THE original list, just building a strong current one.
Pokémon Hunt infrastructure | Cobblemon Hunt/PokeHunt/Picnic were flagged as strong fits for contextual Research Hunts, but Cobblemon 1.8 compatibility was never established for any of them.
NPC/Trainer/Community infrastructure | A large, still-mostly-unsolved category — C.E.R. has the DESIGN for factions/guards/towns/reputation/rallies (Part 10), but the actual mod-level infrastructure to build it on is largely unresolved. High priority.
Legendary Dungeons/Trials integration decision | Multiple overlapping systems now exist — Professor Porker's Legendary Dungeons, Cobblemon Trials Edition, vanilla Trial Chambers, Resonance Sites, Resonance Gateways/Outbreaks, custom Legendary Domains. Needs a decision: are these mods infrastructure, inspiration, or content? Not yet made.
PC Overhaul cluster — RESOLVED via Mod Audit/Optimization Sweep | Four overlapping mods (MoreCobblemonTweaks, CobblePCEnhanced, Cobblemon Quick Teams, Cobblemon Home) were sitting as separate candidates rather than a resolved cluster. RECOMMENDATION: MoreCobblemonTweaks as the primary QoL layer — broadest feature set (search/rename/wallpaper/swap/multiselect/IV display), and some of its features already got absorbed into base Cobblemon 1.7, a good sign it's aligned with where the core mod itself is heading. Cobblemon Home dropped from serious consideration — its unlimited cross-world storage genuinely conflicts with the scarcity/permanence philosophy behind Retirement/Championship Protocol (Part 2), not just a minor overlap. CobblePCEnhanced's upgrade-tier CONCEPT worth keeping as design inspiration rather than installing a separate mod for it. Quick Teams too narrow a single feature to justify as its own install once MoreCobblemonTweaks covers similar ground.
Trainer-architecture cluster — CORRECTED (Wesley never agreed to RCT's removal, Sept 2026) | Five overlapping trainer-battle mods: RCT, Fierce Competition, Secret Base Trainer, Cinematic Combat, TBCS+Easy NPC. CORRECTION: an earlier pass incorrectly treated TBCS+Easy NPC as a confirmed REPLACEMENT for RCT and marked RCT dropped — that was never actually approved; TBCS+Easy NPC was only ever meant as a SUGGESTED ADDITION/alternative to evaluate alongside RCT, not a decided substitution. RCT RESTORED to Confirmed — it's foundational infrastructure for multiple other real addons already in M.M.I. (Cobblemon Trainer Doubles depends on it directly; Battle Tower and TBCS both build on the related RCTApi library from the same author), so removing it would cascade into breaking those dependents. Current honest status: RCT (Confirmed, foundational) + TBCS/Easy NPC (Candidate, complementary NPC/dialogue/faction layer, not a replacement) + Secret Base Trainer (genuinely distinct — battle-clone NPCs from the player's own team) + Cinematic Combat (genuinely distinct — battle-camera layer) all coexist. Fierce Competition remains a design-inspiration reference given its original-content approach, not installed alongside RCT to avoid redundant trainer rosters.
Mega Evolution ecosystem conflict audit | Xadok's Megas, Lotus Megas, and potentially other Mega datapacks now coexist as candidates — risk of competing over the same Pokémon/form IDs if multiple get installed without checking overlap first.
Regional/Convergent/Custom Pokémon ecosystem | Fanmade Form Funfair, Syn. Eevees, the Mega datapacks, potential original C.E.R. Fakemon, Shadow/Fractured/Fusion/Twisted Pokémon, and Affinity adaptations are all becoming one large variant ecosystem — real risk of accidentally building several competing form systems instead of one coherent one.
Scanner/Research system integration audit | Native Cobblemon Pokédex, Better Pokedex Scanner, Catch Indicator, Cobblemon Snap, Cobblemon Analyzer/IV Scanner, and the eventual Resonance Scanner concept (Part 3) are all information-display candidates — needs a deliberate pass rather than installing every information mod found.
Global Loot Integration Sweep | Cobbleloots, Occupied Pokéballs, structure/dungeon/fishing/archaeology loot, and boss/Apex drops are accumulating without a formal pass checking how they interact. Loot Sweep run (Sept 2026): Loot Identity Rule compliance confirmed genuinely strong (Regional Occupied-Ball Loot, Fossils, Memory Phenomenon all already designed correctly against it). Per-Player Discovery Loot RESOLVED: Cobbleloots' infinite-uses setting handles Poke Ball-style loot per-player, Lootr (M.M.I.) handles all container-based loot per-player. Remaining open piece: a joint pass specifically checking overlap/balance BETWEEN Cobbleloots, Occupied Pokéballs, and structure/boss loot together — not yet done.
Magic/tech mod roster finalization | Create, Ars Nouveau, Occultism, Ars Ocultas, Nature's Aura, and others remain candidates, not confirmed — consistent with the project still being in a Foundation/System-Discovery phase for this cluster specifically.

C.E.R. — Missed Questions & Unanswered Offers
Honest caveat first: this is compiled from careful recall of the conversation, not a mechanical re-scan of every message — I don't have a way to grep this live conversation the way I could search an uploaded file. Where I COULD verify something directly (by searching my actual archive files for whether promised work was completed), I did — that's how CLR-0073 got found and fixed just now. Treat this list as a strong-effort pass, not a guaranteed-complete one.
1. Confirmed genuinely unresolved (verified by checking the archive, not just memory)
Fusion dynamic-generation fallback: Anchor-model + Partner-texture-blend
Proposed as a solution to ‘what happens when no curated custom Fusion species exists for a pair’: keep the Anchor's actual model geometry, texture-blend toward the Partner's palette, swap small cosmetic pieces. Two-tier result: Curated Fusions (flagship pairs, full custom model) vs. Generic Fusions (everything else, always available, no dead end). You said “not yet, need to read” when this first came up and it never got revisited. Still sitting unanswered — genuinely worth a yes/no whenever you're ready.
2. Offered but never confirmed or declined
Formal Implementation Milestone document
During the discussion of what the first buildable vertical-slice milestone should be (“The First Resonance” / “Does the World Actually Answer”), I offered to write this up as a formal Implementation Milestone document in the archive. The conversation moved into the Resonance Gateway/Z-Crystal recovery before that offer got a direct answer. Worth a yes/no if you still want that written up as its own tracked deliverable.
Full feature-by-feature Cobblemon Integration Bible
Part 16's audit ends by proposing an even deeper follow-up: going through every current Cobblemon 1.8 system individually (every item family, block, structure, spawning context, datapack hook) and assigning each one KEEP NATIVE / INTEGRATE / MODIFY / EXPAND / REPLACE / IGNORE / INVESTIGATE — explicitly framed as the next step before touching implementation. Never confirmed or declined.
Resonance Synthesizer vs. Resonance Synthesis Chamber naming discrepancy
Part 13 flags this directly: two different recovered sweep passes produced two different names for what may be the same apparatus, or may be a research-tier device (Synthesizer) preceding a more significant multiblock (Chamber). Explicitly “flagged for Wesley's direct call rather than picking one” — still open.
3. Design threads you just confirmed you want fleshed out (tracked here so they don't slip again)
Themed Teams (Valor/Instinct/Mystic/Harmony/Guardian, etc.) — needs real research into GO's teams, Team Harmony's actual fan-origin, and non-GO Pokémon purpose-factions before naming philosophies.
The late-game ‘Resonance is the world, not just a resource in it’ ontological revelation — flagged as the strongest single idea in the whole batch it came from.
Paradox Pokémon → Time Domain gating, Ultra Beasts → Space/Dimensional Domain gating — fits the existing Eligibility≠Recognition philosophy directly.
These aren't unanswered questions — you already said yes to all three — included here only so they're tracked in one place rather than risking getting lost again the way the IV Essence work almost did.
4. What I'd suggest
Given the honest limitation above, if you want real certainty that nothing else is sitting unanswered, the most reliable path would be pointing me at specific stretches of the conversation you remember being unsure about, rather than asking for one more ‘are you sure that's everything’ pass — my recall is good but it's still recall, not a search.

# SOURCE: Project_Governance_Protocol.docx
---

Project Governance Protocol
A Portable Framework for Long-Term Collaborative Reconstruction Projects
Extracted and genericized from the governance framework developed over an extended collaborative reconstruction project (working name in the source project: C.E.R.). Everything below is written to apply to ANY project, not the one it came from. Upload this document to a new conversation, and ask Claude to read it and adopt the framework for your new project — replace bracketed placeholders like [PROJECT NAME] as you go. This is meant to be internalized once, not re-explained by hand each time.
0. How To Use This Document
Tell the new Claude instance something like: ‘Read this Project Governance Protocol and adopt it for [PROJECT NAME]. I'll answer any placeholder questions you have, then we start building.’ Claude should read the whole document, ask you to fill in the placeholders it can't infer (project name, core creative pillars, the four-letter archive shorthand set), then begin operating under this framework immediately — creating the actual archive documents (Master Archive Part 1, Authorities Archive, etc.) as real files, not just acknowledging the framework conversationally.
This document covers PROCESS — how decisions get made, tracked, and organized. It deliberately does NOT include any actual creative content from the project it was extracted from. Your new project's actual ideas, lore, and mechanics start from zero; only the scaffolding is reused.
1. The Archive Structure
Split content across separate documents by PURPOSE, not chronology. This is the single most important habit — it's what makes a project's history reviewable months later instead of buried in chat scrollback.
Add project-specific archives as needed (the source project added a Master Mod Index once mod-selection tracking outgrew being a subsection) — same rule: split by purpose, give it a shorthand, cross-reference it from the Master Index below.
2. The Master Index
Once Authorities Archive accumulates more than a handful of rules, add a consolidated flat index near the top of it — every permission/rule/principle in one scannable list, organized by topic, each pointing to its full detail further down the document. This solved a real problem in the source project: after enough sessions, rules were genuinely hard to locate even though every one of them was properly documented somewhere. Don't build this on day one — add it once Authorities Archive actually gets long enough to need it.
3. Free Reign Mode (F.R.M.) — or your project's equivalent name
The core delegation framework: the human grants Claude standing authority to make certain categories of decision without asking permission each time, provided every action gets logged. The name 'Free Reign Mode' is arbitrary — rename it to whatever fits your new project's voice. What matters is the shape of the framework, not the label.
The actual standard
Every F.R.M. action should be evaluated against one question: ‘Does this make the project better?’ — not ‘did you ask first.’ Asking permission for everything defeats the purpose of delegating authority in the first place.
Locked decisions are a ceiling, not silently overridable
Once something is explicitly locked/confirmed by the human, F.R.M. discretion doesn't mean quietly changing it later. A locked decision can still be revisited — but that requires actually raising it with the human again, not treating F.R.M. as a license to override established calls.
Logging is non-negotiable
Every F.R.M. action gets a Change Log entry, no exceptions. This isn't bureaucracy for its own sake — it's what lets a future session (or a different AI instance) understand not just what the project currently says, but WHY, without the human needing to re-explain it.
Standing Meta-Rule: new grants are self-integrating
Any new authority the human grants gets written into the Permission Set immediately, as part of the grant itself — not as a separate follow-up step the human has to remember to request.
Starter Permission Set (adapt freely — this is a starting menu, not a fixed list)
Archive / Approve / Veto / Modify / Defer — the basic vocabulary for how Claude can respond to a proposal: record it, endorse it, push back on it, improve it before accepting, or postpone a decision pending more information.
Merge/split systems — combine two overlapping concepts, or separate one bloated concept into cleaner parts.
Propose implementation architecture — offer concrete technical/structural approaches, not just conceptual design.
Resurrect good discarded ideas — nothing marked historical/superseded is gone forever; a later session can bring it back if it turns out to fit somewhere after all.
Discretionary Sweeps/Audits — proactively inspect the project for gaps, redundancies, or forgotten ideas without being asked (see Section 4).
Sweep/Audit Creation & Integration — invent new Sweep types as the project's needs reveal them; retroactively recognize any existing process that fits the Sweep definition as a formal Sweep.
Umbrella Term Creation & Family Integration — create new organizing/classification terms when a genuine pattern emerges across several existing concepts, and place things under it. A thing can belong to MULTIPLE umbrellas simultaneously if it genuinely fits more than one — never force an exclusive single parent category for tidiness alone.
Content Consolidation — a Modification-type action: when something gets promoted to its own dedicated document after previously sharing space with other content, decide whether the old shared section should be removed entirely (unrelated content, coincidental sharing) or condensed to a brief cross-referencing overview (genuinely related content). Prevents concepts scattering across the archive as it grows.
Multi-Part Task Continuation — once a multi-part task is approved (a Sweep across many documents, a big multi-file pass), no permission is needed to proceed from one part to the next. Genuinely new decision points found mid-task still get surfaced to the human.
Proactive Suggestion Making — introduce ideas without waiting to be asked.
Term Coining & Term Banking — create new terminology when useful; preserve promising-but-unused terminology for later rather than discarding it.
Naming-Collision Auto-Resolution — if a newly-created name collides with something already established, rename the NEW thing to clear the conflict, notify the human, and log it — without needing approval first for this specific narrow case.
Change-log Creation — Claude writes Change Log entries directly as part of doing the work, not as a separate step the human has to request.
External Inspiration/Reference Research — permission to research comparable real-world or other-project precedents when useful (with the same meaningful-adaptation-not-borrowing standard as Section 5's Modification Sweep).
Automatic Provenance Crediting — if any real external creator's work (a mod, an asset, a specific idea) ever gets referenced or reused, credit them explicitly wherever that reuse is recorded. Non-negotiable, not situational.
4. Sweep/Audit System
A Sweep (or Audit — interchangeable) is formally defined by PURPOSE, not name: any process that analyzes, modifies, categorizes, or otherwise optimizes a field of the project. Any existing or future process meeting that description qualifies, whether or not it's literally named 'Sweep.'
Automated Sweeps (triggering on a schedule/condition rather than being explicitly requested) is worth banking as an idea but NOT adopting by default — keep it as a known option, mentioned briefly whenever a Sweep actually gets requested, without pushing toward it.
5. The Change Log — the project's actual memory
Every meaningful decision gets a numbered entry (e.g. CLR-0001, CLR-0002...) in the Change Log Archive, tagged with one of: ADD / REMOVE / MODIFY / REPLACE / RENAME / MERGE / SPLIT / RECLASSIFY / PROMOTION / DEMOTION / CONTRADICTION / RESOLUTION. Each entry should record: what changed, the reason, what it affects (cross-references to the actual archive sections), and current status.
Critical discipline: NEVER edit or delete a past entry to ‘fix’ a decision that later changed. If Decision A gets reversed later, write a NEW entry describing the reversal and cross-reference the original — the full back-and-forth stays visible. This is what makes the Change Log trustworthy: a future reader can see not just the current state but the actual reasoning history, including changes of mind, without anything being quietly smoothed over.
If two independent tracks of work on the same project (e.g. Claude and a separate ChatGPT conversation both reconstructing the same project) develop their OWN Change Log numbering independently, this WILL eventually collide — two different entries both claiming the same number for different content. Flag this immediately when discovered rather than silently picking one; it needs an explicit human decision about which numbering is authoritative or how to merge them.
6. Status Vocabulary
A consistent, visible-at-a-glance status tag for every piece of design content, so 'is this decided or just an idea' never requires re-reading the surrounding paragraph:
7. The Archive Preservation Principle
Nothing gets deleted just because it's superseded, rejected, or historical. 'Not canon' never means 'delete' — a rejected idea, a superseded mechanic, and a contradiction are all still evidence of the project's actual development history, and that history has real value: it stops the same idea from being silently re-proposed and re-rejected in a later session, and it lets a human explain to a collaborator WHY something is the way it is, not just that it is that way.
This creates one real tension worth naming explicitly: Content Consolidation (Section 3) wants to remove duplicate CURRENT descriptions when something gets its own dedicated page. The resolution: Consolidation only ever touches active/current duplicate descriptions — it never touches historical, superseded, or rejected material, which Archive Preservation protects unconditionally regardless of how old or seemingly irrelevant.
8. Provisional Thoughts — the staging area
Lives in the Supplemental Archive. For items that have a known eventual home in another archive/section, but haven't been formally reviewed/confirmed by the human yet. Get moved to their real home once reviewed; this section should stay short-lived by design, not become a second permanent archive.
Governing tone, worth stating explicitly to whoever operates this framework: the goal is to have as few things sitting in this staging area as possible by the end of the project — but that is NOT a reason to hesitate marking something provisional in the moment. A full staging area isn't a problem to feel bad about; it's the system doing its job. Marking something provisional should always feel like the safe default when in doubt.
9. Canonical Terminology Normalization
When a project accumulates multiple names for the same underlying concept over time (a natural byproduct of iterative design), the current/official name gets used consistently going forward, with the historical names preserved explicitly as ‘this used to be called X’ rather than silently forgotten or silently retconned as if the old name never existed.
10. Core Creative Principles — a pattern to replicate, not specific rules to copy
The source project had several short, memorable, named design-philosophy slogans it referenced constantly (examples: 'Discovery Over Prescription' — the user should discover mechanics through play rather than being handed a rigid instruction manual; 'Creation Over Crafting' — favor meaningful constructive processes over simple recipe-following; a 'Justified Complexity Rule' — every added system must answer 'is this complexity earning its place?'). These specific principles came from THAT project's specific creative identity — don't copy them verbatim into an unrelated project.
What IS worth replicating: the PATTERN of naming a small number of core philosophies early, referencing them by their short name constantly throughout design discussion, and using them as an actual filter for evaluating new ideas ('does this fit our [Principle Name]?') rather than leaving them as unstated vibes. Ask the human what their new project's 3–8 core creative pillars are, name them, and use those names the same way going forward.
11. Editorial Integrity
Claude may freely fix formatting, clarity, grammar, and organization — but must never silently change the MEANING of an established decision while doing so. If a fix requires changing what something actually says, that's a MODIFY-type Change Log action, not a formatting pass.
12. Getting Started Checklist for a New Project
1. Name the project and decide on the archive shorthand set (or reuse M.A./A.A./I.A./S.A./C.L.A. from Section 1).
2. Create the initial Authorities Archive with this framework adopted — rename 'F.R.M.' if desired, confirm the Permission Set, note the Sweep definitions.
3. Create Change Log Archive, start numbering at CLR-0001.
4. Identify and name the project's own 3–8 core creative pillars (Section 10) — this is genuinely new work, not something this document can hand you.
5. Begin Master Archive Part 1 with whatever existing source material/ideas the human brings, using Preserve → Recover → Classify → Reconcile → Canonize as the reconstruction order if starting from scattered prior material (chat logs, notes, etc.) rather than a blank page.
6. Do NOT reference or pull content from any other unrelated project's archive, even if the human has one open elsewhere — this framework is meant to be reused across projects, not to merge them.
Archive | Shorthand | Purpose | Typical structure
Master Archive | M.A. | What the project IS — systems, mechanics, design, lore, decisions. | Split into numbered Parts as it grows (Part 1, Part 2...) rather than one giant document. Each Part covers a related cluster of systems.
Authorities Archive | A.A. | HOW the project is governed — rules, permissions, delegated authorities, core principles. (This document's ideas end up living here in the new project.) | One document; grows by adding sections, rarely needs splitting.
Interaction Archive | I.A. | WHAT HAPPENED around the project — process tangents, personal context, the human side of the collaboration that isn't itself a design decision. | One document. Often stays short — that's fine, it means the collaboration was on-task.
Supplemental Archive | S.A. | Material that doesn't cleanly fit the others yet, PLUS a staging area for things pending review (see Section 6). | One document, two clearly separated sections: permanent miscellany, and a short-lived 'Provisional Thoughts' staging area.
Change Log Archive | C.L.A. | The permanent, numbered record of every meaningful decision — what changed, why, and what it affects. | One continuously-growing document. This is the project's memory across sessions — treat it as load-bearing, not optional.
Sweep type | What it does
Modification Sweep | Reconstruct (review accumulated ideas, recover forgotten concepts) → Consolidate (remove repeats, merge divergent concepts, settle terminology) → Critically Re-evaluate (find weak systems, contradictions, unneeded complexity) → Modify Freely (add/remove/replace/rework/simplify/expand/rebalance/rename) → Preserve Strong Ideas (mark genuinely good material 'unchanged, no modification necessary') → Final Reassessment → Change Log entry.
Integration Sweep | Do the project's systems actually work together? Checks shared resources/states, contradictions, redundancy, missing connections between systems that should relate but don't yet, gameplay/user-experience loops, and complexity creep.
Compatibility/Component Audit | Reviews the project's external building blocks (mods, libraries, assets, whatever your project's equivalent is) for additions, removals, version compatibility, and overlap. Never claim a dependency is safe to remove without actually checking usage first.
Full Sweep | An umbrella term covering whichever combination of the above is appropriate for the current need — doesn't mean literally every sweep type must run every time.
Bank Sweep | A dedicated pass specifically cataloguing terminology regardless of status — official, locked, banked, candidate, historical, rejected, ambiguous — so nothing gets silently forgotten.
Dependency Sweep & Missing Link Integration (M.L.I.) | Dependency Sweep maps what systems rely on what other systems, so a proposed change's downstream impact is visible before it's made. M.L.I. specifically records 'System A and System C obviously belong together, we don't yet know how' as an explicit open item — rather than unconsciously inventing a connection just to feel complete.
Symbol | Meaning
🕒 Locked | Explicitly decided and current — the ceiling for F.R.M. discretion (see Section 3).
🟢 Strong reconstruction | High-confidence recovered or developed material, not yet formally locked but treated as reliable.
🟡 Candidate | A real proposal on the table, not decided.
🔵 Current proposal | Similar to Candidate — a strong, live proposal awaiting a decision.
🟠 Unresolved | An open question with no settled answer yet, genuinely needing a decision.
⚪ Historical/Superseded | Was once relevant or was the previous version of something now changed — preserved, not deleted (see Section 7).
🔴 Rejected | Considered and explicitly turned down — preserved as a record of what was tried, not erased.

# SOURCE: Supplemental_Archive.docx
---

Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance
Supplemental Archive
Compiled by Claude, September 2026. Per the Authorities Archive, this archive covers material that doesn't cleanly fit Master, Authorities, or Interaction, but is worth preserving — not a garbage bin; entries here can be promoted to another archive later via Cross-Referencing once they have somewhere to go. Also honestly short: most “loose ends” in the transcripts turned out to already have a clear home in the Master Archive's own open-questions/circle-back sections (Part 4), so this archive holds only what genuinely has nowhere else to go yet.
1. The Term Bank
A formally established concept distinct from the locked terminology list: the Term Bank holds terms that have been coined or reserved but don't yet have a confirmed application — not officially part of the design, just preserved in case a use emerges later. Per F.R.M. (Authorities Archive), Term Banking is an authority Claude has inherited.
Currently banked terms
“Affinity Relationship” — reserved during a discussion about which type pairings should have Resonance interactions (Master Archive Part 1's Harmonized/Conflicting pairs). Explicitly saved as possibly useful for an official term later, with no confirmed application yet.
Status: 🟡 Candidate / banked, not in use. Add future banked terms here as they come up rather than starting a new list.
2. Stray Design-Restraint Quotes
A few short lines from the transcripts function as memorable shorthand for principles already documented at length elsewhere in the Master/Authorities Archives. Preserved here as quick-reference phrasing rather than re-explained:
On keeping magic in its place: “Magic should interact with the world rather than become Ars Nouveau: The Modpack” — shorthand for the Three Pillars principle that no single mod should swallow the project's identity (Master Archive Part 4).
On Fusion failure: explicitly and repeatedly reinforced as never permanently punishing (Master Archive Part 3) — stated with enough emphasis in the transcripts (including a rueful “we learned this lesson before implementing it”) that it's worth flagging as a hard-won rule rather than a casual preference.
3. Genuinely Unplaced Items
Nothing else surfaced during this pass that didn't already have a home in Master, Authorities, or Interaction. If review of the finished archives turns up something that doesn't fit elsewhere, it belongs here — this archive stays open for that purpose going forward.
4. Provisional Thoughts
A staging area, distinct from Section 3 above: these items DO have a known eventual home in another archive, but haven't been formally reviewed/confirmed by Wesley yet, so they're held here rather than written directly into their target document. Once reviewed, each entry gets moved into its real home and this section's copy is removed — this section should stay short-lived by design, not become a permanent second archive.
Governing tone, stated directly by Wesley and worth preserving exactly: the goal is to have as few things sitting in this section as possible by the end of the project — but that is NOT a reason to hesitate marking something provisional in the moment. A full section isn't a problem to feel bad about; it's the system doing its job. The right read of an entry here is “we have this idea and haven't decided what to do with it yet, let's see if it finds a use” — not “oh no, there are so many of these.” Marking something provisional should always feel like the safe, correct default when in doubt, never something to avoid for the sake of a shorter list.
4.1 Observatory socket-color-lighting mechanic (target: Master Archive Part 1)
Discussed at length and well-received in conversation, but never actually written into Part 1: each of the Observatory's 18 sockets lighting up in its own Affinity's color specifically when that Affinity reaches 100% — Fire glowing red/orange, Ice light blue, Grass green, and so on. Proposed as the actual visual answer to “how do we show 100% Affinity without a HUD percentage,” consistent with Part 12's existing principle that World Health shouldn't become a number the player watches climb. Note: Part 3's Convergence Gateway proposal already cites this mechanic as if it were locked (“each lighting up at 100%”) — that citation will need to be reconciled once this is formally reviewed, either by locking this mechanic to match, or by softening Part 3's wording if it ends up not being adopted as written.
4.2 Archive file-naming & folder-routing rule (target: Authorities Archive)
Confirmed in conversation as the standing rule for managing the physical C.E.R.A folder structure, never yet written into Authorities Archive's governance section: a brand-new archive part/document, the first time it's ever created, gets “_original” appended to its filename and is filed permanently into an Archive Originals folder, never touched again. The moment that document receives its first real revision, a second file is created WITHOUT the _original suffix, filed into the matching current-archive folder (Master Archives / Authorities Archives / Interaction Archives / Changelog Archives, etc.), and that second file is the one that keeps absorbing every future update. Anything with _original in the filename is permanently frozen — no exceptions, regardless of what changes elsewhere.
4.3 Citation correction (already fixed directly, noted here for the record)
Part 3's Convergence Gateway proposal cited the Observatory's mysterious 19th position as living in “Part 12/Interaction Archive.” It actually lives in Part 10. This was a simple factual correction rather than a design decision needing review, so it was fixed directly rather than staged here — included in this section only so the audit that found it stays fully accounted for.
4.4 (Graduated to Part 7) — formerly the Apex encounter-tier hierarchy
Was held here pending Part 7's “does Ecological Apex Pokémon unify these” question. That question is now resolved (Wesley, Sept 2026, alongside locking Titan Pokémon's place in the taxonomy) — this item has been moved to Part 7 as its proper permanent home. Entry kept here only as a pointer, consistent with the Change Log's practice of not silently erasing the record of where something used to sit.

# SOURCE: CLAUDE_IMPORT_INSTRUCTIONS.md
---

# C.E.R. — Claude Migration Package

## Purpose
This package is a full-fidelity transfer of the Cobblemon: Ecological Resonance (C.E.R.) project context available to the sending ChatGPT project at migration time.

**Do not treat this as a curated summary.** The goal is to preserve the project's history, decisions, proposals, unresolved questions, governance, mod research, and implementation context so the receiving Claude project can reconstruct and maintain its own canonical memory.

## Required ingestion order
1. Read this file.
2. Read `PROJECT_MEMORY_MIGRATION.md`.
3. Read `Project_Governance_Protocol.docx` in full.
4. Read `Authorities_Archive.docx` in full.
5. Read `Master_Mod_Index.docx` and `Cobblemon_Deep_Dive.docx`.
6. Read `Master_Archive_Part1.docx` through `Master_Archive_Part17.docx` in numerical order.
7. Read `CER_Open_Questions_and_Loose_Ends.docx`, `Missed_Questions.docx`, `CER_Reintegration_Sweep_Findings.docx`, `EcoRe_Open_Questions_and_Gaps.md`, and `EcoRe_Full_Sweep_Findings_UNINTEGRATED.md`.
8. Read `Change_Log_Archive.docx`, `Interaction_Archive.docx`, and `Supplemental_Archive.docx`.
9. Consult `EXTERNAL_SOURCE_MANIFEST.md` for links/research that were discussed alongside the archive.

## Canonicality rules
- Preserve historical material even if it is superseded. Do not silently delete old ideas.
- Distinguish **locked/confirmed**, **current proposal**, **open/unresolved**, **rejected/shelved**, **banked**, and **historical/superseded** material.
- Do not promote an old proposal merely because it appears in an archive.
- Do not demote a locked decision merely because a later conversation explored alternatives unless the archive explicitly records that decision as reopened or superseded.
- Treat the Governance Protocol and Authorities Archive as the process authority.
- Treat the Master Archive as the main content authority.
- Treat the Mod Index as the primary mod-selection/reference authority.
- Treat the Interaction Archive as the record of collaborative/process context.
- Treat the Supplemental Archive as preserved material that does not yet have a cleaner canonical home.
- Where two records conflict, surface the conflict and trace it through the Change Log/open-question material instead of guessing.

## Important migration principle
Claude should build/refresh its own project memory from this package. It should **not** replace the archive with a short summary. The archive is the evidence base; the generated memory is a navigation/working layer over that evidence.

## Reverse migration
When Claude later provides its C.E.R. memory/export back to ChatGPT, import it as a second evidence set. Merge it against this archive rather than overwriting this package wholesale.

# C.E.R. — External Source Manifest

This manifest preserves external links that were discussed in the C.E.R. work. Some are current technical references; others are historical/version-specific research. **A link being present does not mean the mod is approved for the pack.**

## Directly discussed references

### GitHub / project source
- C.E.R. update repository supplied during project work: https://github.com/xgoodxeatsx/C.E.R--update-4
  - Use as a project/code reference when available.
  - The migration search did not produce a reliable indexed result for this exact repository, so this entry is retained from the project context rather than represented as independently verified web metadata.

### Cobblemon Counter
- https://modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-counter
- Current checked compatibility includes Minecraft 1.21.1, 1.20.1, and 1.19.2; tracks captures, knockouts, resurrections, fish-ups, egg hatchings, Pokémon eating nearby snacks, counts/streaks, and exposes commands. This is relevant to quest/event/condition architecture research. Source checked 2026-09-23.

### Cobbledex × Field Guide Compat Scan
- https://modrinth.com/mod/cobbledex-x-field-guide-compat-scan
- Historical compatibility bridge: Minecraft 1.20.1 Forge; Cobblemon 1.5.2; Cobbledex 1.1.0; Field Guide 1.8.3. It demonstrates a thin bridge pattern for dual registration and scan interoperability. Do not treat it as a 1.21.1 dependency.

### Cobbleworkers
- https://modrinth.com/mod/cobbleworkers
- Current checked compatibility: Minecraft 1.21.1; Fabric/NeoForge; server-side/singleplayer. Supports datapack-defined jobs. Relevant to Pokémon-as-workers/utility systems.
- Example 1.7-era release/source page: https://modrinth.com/mod/cobbleworkers/version/bbTUCGY1

### Cobblemon Fossil Deposits
- https://modrinth.com/mod/cobblemon-fossil-deposits
- Current checked compatibility: Minecraft 1.21.1–1.21.11, Fabric. Adds fossil deposits and exposes `FossilExcavationEvents.FOSSIL_EXCAVATED`, useful as a compatibility/event hook.

### Attuned
- https://modrinth.com/mod/attuned-mod
- Current checked source spans several Minecraft versions including 1.21.1. Relevant conceptually to affinity/attunement/loadout architecture, but compatibility with C.E.R.'s final stack must be independently tested.

### Stained Lenses
- https://modrinth.com/mod/stained-lenses
- Checked source currently lists Minecraft 1.20.1–1.20.6, Fabric. Useful as a design reference for spyglass/lens/module mechanics but not a current 1.21.1 assumption.

### KyuremBoss
- https://www.curseforge.com/minecraft/mc-mods/kyuremboss
- Checked source lists a 1.21.1 NeoForge release and describes replacement of the Ender Dragon with a Gigamax Kyurem boss. Relevant to the boss-replacement research thread.

## Other references discussed in project context
These should be searched/verified again before implementation because their version/support state can change:
- Mega Showdown / AllTheMons compatibility and datapack references
- Complete Galar Fossilmon
- Cobbledex / Field Guide
- Ars Nouveau and its Starbuncles
- Productive Metalworks
- Silent Gear
- Apotheosis-style affix concepts
- Ars Affinity / Affinity Jar source/code discussions
- Kyogre/Guzzlord/Wither/Elder Guardian replacement concepts
- Conduits / custom conduit concepts
- XMind project map

## Version rule
Every external mod reference must be recorded with the exact Minecraft version, loader, Cobblemon version, and relevant mod version when the project moves from research into implementation. Never infer compatibility from a project's existence alone.

# SOURCE: EcoRe_Full_Sweep_Findings_UNINTEGRATED.md
---

# Full Sweep — Findings for Review (NOT yet integrated)

Per your request, this is a fresh full-sweep pass looking specifically for material not yet in any of the 10 Master Archive parts, Authorities, Interaction, or Supplemental. **Nothing here has been added to the archives** — this is purely for you to review first. Coverage note: this pass went through the largest previously-unswept blocks of pt1–pt3 (several thousand lines each) rather than every remaining byte of the ~105,000-line transcripts; it's substantial, not literally exhaustive. If you want a second pass after this one, that's easy to do.

Organized by finding, roughly in order of significance.

---

## 1. The Skill Tree System (major find)

A full specialization system tied to the Cobblemon Skill Tree mod, deliberately separated from Affinity: *"Affinity determines what you resonate with. Skill determines how you have learned to work with it."* This significantly extends Part 10's "Player Paths of Resonance" with real mechanical depth.

**Eight branches, each redesigned around professions rather than generic stat bonuses:**
- **Researcher** — Field Observation, Ecological Survey, Anomaly Detection, Resonance Analysis, eventually "Unknown Lifeform" (Fakemon recognition)
- **Ranger** — Trailcraft → Habitat Knowledge → Field Survey → Conservation → Sanctuary Keeper
- **Battler** — deliberately *not* flat combat bonuses; sub-branches Trainer (obedience), Tactician, Breeder, Specialist (Personal Affinity synergy), Champion
- **Scientist/Geneticist** — Species Study → Inheritance → Genetic Analysis → Mutation Research → Synthesis Theory → Advanced Fusion (ties directly into the Fusion/IV-Fusion split from Part 9)
- **Engineer** — Mechanical Knowledge → Automation → Infrastructure → **Resonance Engineering** (machines interacting with World Affinity) → Restoration Engineer
- **Arcanist** — ties to Ars Nouveau/Nature's Aura; Arcane Apprentice → Spellcraft → Natural Conduit → Aura Manipulation → Resonance Magic → World Shaper
- **Occultist** — deliberately framed as a *dangerous* specialization with real tradeoffs (better spirit interactions and unique access, but increased Anomaly risk) — "you are deliberately studying things the world was trying to forget"

**Governing rules, all explicit:**
- Skill points should be **scarce**, earned through demonstrated mastery (e.g. Researcher mastery from documenting species/photographing behavior/discovering anomalies, not kill count)
- Skills should unlock **verbs** ("You can identify migration patterns") not percentages ("+3% XP")
- **Skill Capacity** mirrors Personal Affinity Capacity: 3 active specialties early → 5 mid → 7 late → 9 endgame; unused skills aren't erased, just inactive
- **Skill × Affinity cross-specialization**: e.g. Fire Specialist + Engineer → "Thermal Resonance" (heat-based Create machinery interacts with Fire Affinity); Water Specialist + Ranger → "Hydrological Restoration"
- Player identity becomes **earned titles** rather than levels — "Field Researcher: You have documented 127 species and uncovered 14 ecological anomalies" instead of "Level 37"

This would give the full character-progression stack as: World Affinity (what the world supports) → Personal Affinity (what resonates with you) → **Skills** (what you've mastered) → Hearts (how much of yourself you've restored) → Virtues (who you've become) → Domain Authority (what's been entrusted to you).

---

## 2. Hostile Mobs & Boss Philosophy

Explicit recommendation: keep vanilla hostile mobs, add a **small number of highly curated bosses**, skip a large boss-mod catalog. Ender's Cataclysm specifically discussed and leaned against — not because the bosses are bad, but because a boss with no answer to "what does this mean in our world?" dilutes the pack's identity.

**Region-based danger tiers** (new, not previously captured): Restored Hearthhome (mostly safe) → Abandoned settlement (normal hostiles) → Corrupted region (harder mobs + Shadow phenomena) → Ancient ruin (unusual enemies + Anomaly mechanics) → Legendary domain (completely different rules).

Bosses, when included, should belong to an existing system rather than being generic — e.g. an Occultism boss tied to an escaped ritual (defeating it could restore an area, unlock research, and reveal ancient-civilization lore), or a Shadow boss framed as "an ecosystem has become fundamentally wrong" requiring investigation rather than just a fight.

---

## 3. Paradox Pokémon — Full Philosophy

A dedicated encounter philosophy, explicitly its own category distinct from Fusion/Shadow/Ultra Beasts/Legendaries: **temporal intrusion**, not another generation unlock.

- **Past Paradox** = "Primal/Ancient/Temporal Regression" — biological, wild, prehistoric, tied to the Life pillar
- **Future Paradox** = "Synthetic/Futuristic/Temporal Progression" — artificial, technological, energy-based, tied to the Industry/Tech pillar
- Explicitly **not gated behind generation unlocks** (would spoil the mystery) — instead: Generation → Temporal Eligibility → Anomaly → Investigation → Encounter
- Environmental clue sets differ by direction: Past = strange weather, fossils appearing, ancient footprints, plants growing too fast; Future = machinery malfunctioning, power drains, electromagnetic disturbances, metallic footprints

---

## 4. Ditto Synthesis

A special-cased Fusion outcome: Ditto + Ditto → "Anomalous Synthesis" rather than a normal compatibility-checked Fusion, since Ditto's whole biology is transformation. Result is a **controlled random Pokémon**, weighted by current World Affinity (high Grass → more Grass results; high Corruption → possible Shadow result; active temporal/dimensional anomaly → possible Paradox/Ultra Beast result). Explicitly **excludes Legendaries/Mythicals** — those stay gated behind the Recognition system regardless of Ditto Synthesis odds.

---

## 5. Crafting Removal — Concrete Production Philosophy (significant)

This looks like it may be the actual origin point of C.O.C., not just a restatement of it. Explicit goal, in your own words: remove **hand assembly via the 3×3 grid** as the default production method — not remove crafting/creation itself.

- Hard rule proposed: the 3×3 grid becomes an early-game emergency tool at most, largely replaced for real progression recipes
- Player still gathers/processes/smelts/mills/presses/infuses/enchants/brews/transmutes/ritualizes/engineers/synthesizes/automates — just not "combine 6 things in a grid, get a machine"
- **Create becomes the backbone of physical manufacturing** (raw material → processing → components → assembly via Mechanical Crafters/Deployers/Presses/Sequenced Assembly → finished product)
- **New mod candidates surfaced here, not in any mod list yet: AE2 (Applied Energistics 2) or Refined Storage**, specifically for bulk auto-crafting once a recipe is known — explicitly requested by you as an alternative to hand-crafting at scale
- Ars Nouveau's enchanting apparatus and Occultism's rituals are also named as legitimate "creation" methods alongside Create

---

## 6. Confirms the Origin Story + Possible Alternate Ecological-Stage Names

Directly found the moment you referenced earlier in this conversation — a message where a large chunk of prior discussion appeared to have vanished, and ChatGPT confirmed it couldn't recover a perfect transcript, only retained/summarized context. This is good independent confirmation of the story behind why this whole reconstruction project exists.

In the recap that followed, one possibly-new detail: an ecological-stage naming set — **Dormant → Recovering → Stable → Flourishing → Resonant** — mentioned as an established concept. This may be an alternate/earlier name for the World Affinity tier table already in Part 1 (Forgotten/Stirring/Returning/Established/Thriving/Dominant/Restored), or a genuinely separate framing. Not resolved by this pass — flagging rather than merging.

---

## 7. Concrete Mod-Specific Technical Findings

- **Cobblemon Whiteout** requires **Kotlin for Forge** as a dependency (this was likely the missing piece in a past failed install) — confirmed compatible versions exist for Minecraft 1.21.1/NeoForge/Cobblemon 1.8.0. Whiteout triggers player death on full-team faint/battle loss/fleeing, with each condition configurable — explicitly categorized as **Nuzlocke infrastructure** (toggleable), not a mandatory pack rule.
- Occupied Poké Balls mod discussion confirms a Fabric-only original version with a NeoForge port question — matches what's already in Part 8, no new contradiction, but worth confirming which exact port ended up targeted.

---

## Still not covered in this pass

Given the scope, several large blocks remain genuinely unswept: the back half of pt1 (roughly lines 30,000–48,000, covering starting-world details, the "5 worlds" numeric discussion, modular ball/magic-machine integration discussion, and more), and most of pt2 past the materials/F.R.M./retirement content already captured, and pt3 beyond the sections already mined for Authorities/Part 6/Part 8. If you want, I can keep going through those next — just say so once you've reviewed what's here.
